## Data model (Firestore collections)

### users
`{uid, email, name, role: "victim"|"donor"|"volunteer"|"admin", phone, location, nic, homeAddress, available, createdAt}`. `nic`/`homeAddress` only populated for role "victim" (both `null` otherwise, and `null` for victim accounts created before this field existed) — `nic` is unique across victim accounts, both feed the `possibleDuplicate` check on new requests. Sensitive PII — see Privacy section, non-negotiable constraints on where these can ever appear. `available` (boolean) only meaningful for role "volunteer" — absent on existing volunteer docs pre-dating this field, always treated as `available !== false` (i.e. missing = available) rather than requiring a backfill, see "Volunteer availability toggle" below.

### aidRequests
`{victimId, victimName, victimNic, victimHomeAddress (both normalized, or null for pre-existing accounts), possibleDuplicate, disasterType, items: [{category, quantity, unit, status: "pending"|"matched"|"delivered", donationId}], requestedCategories: string[] (denormalized item categories, for array-contains matching queries), severity, peopleAffected, vulnerableGroups[], location, notes, status: "pending"|"verified"|"rejected"|"in_progress"|"delivered", priorityScore, priorityBreakdown, createdAt, updatedAt, verifiedAt}`. A request can ask for several categories at once, each capped (see `server/src/utils/categories.js` — medicine has no cap but is instead flagged for review). Overall `status` is derived from item statuses via `computeOverallStatus()` — `in_progress` means at least one item matched/delivered but not all delivered yet. `possibleDuplicate` is a flag for admin attention, not a block — set when an existing active request shares the same NIC or home address. `verifiedAt` is set once, the first time a request is approved (never overwritten on a later re-verify) — absent on rejected or still-pending requests; powers the admin dashboard's average time-to-verification KPI, see "Admin dashboard KPIs" below.

### donations
`{donorId, donorName, category, quantity, location, deliveryMethod: "self"|"volunteer", notes, status: "available"|"matched"|"delivered", matchedRequestId, assignedDeliveryId, deliveryStatus (mirrors the active delivery's status, or null), lastRejectionReason (set when a volunteer rejects, cleared when the next one accepts), createdAt, updatedAt}`. One donation still targets exactly one category — a multi-category request gets fulfilled by several separate donations/deliveries, one per category.

### deliveries
`{requestId, donationId, category, volunteerId (null for self-delivery), method: "self"|"volunteer", status: "pending_acceptance"|"accepted"|"rejected"|"picked_up"|"delivered"|"confirmed", rejectionReason, confirmToken (set once status reaches "delivered"), currentLocation: {lat,lng}|null, createdAt, updatedAt}`. Volunteer deliveries start at `pending_acceptance` and need an explicit accept before progressing; a reject resets `donations.assignedDeliveryId`/`deliveryStatus` to null so admin can assign someone else (the request item stays `matched` throughout). Self-delivery donations skip straight to `accepted` (auto-created at match time) and the donor marks `delivered` directly (no `picked_up` step).

### categoryLimits
`{label, max: number|null, unit, createdAt, updatedAt}`, one doc per category, doc ID = a slugified key derived from the label (lowercase, non-alphanumeric → `_`, trimmed). `max: null` means no fixed cap (medicine — always flagged for admin review instead, see `requests.js`). Self-seeds from `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_LIMITS` in `server/src/utils/categories.js` the first time the collection is read empty; after that Firestore is the sole source of truth — the defaults constant is a seed value only, not consulted again.

### broadcasts
`{message, severity: "info"|"warning"|"critical", active: boolean, createdBy, createdByName, createdAt, updatedAt, deactivatedAt}`. Only one broadcast is ever `active: true` at a time — posting a new one deactivates the current one in the same batch write, so there's a full history rather than a single mutable doc. Nothing self-seeds here; an empty collection just means no banner has ever been posted.

### caseNotes
`{requestId, authorId, authorName, text, createdAt}`. Top-level collection (not a subcollection of `aidRequests`, consistent with how `deliveries`/`donations` reference their parent by id rather than nesting) — one doc per note, append-only, no edit or delete. Fetched with a single `where("requestId", "==", ...)` and sorted by `createdAt` in JS rather than `orderBy` in the query, deliberately, to avoid needing a Firestore composite index (see "Internal case notes" below for why this matters).

### auditLog
`{action, actorId, actorName, targetType, targetId, details, createdAt}`. Append-only, admin-read-only. `action` is one of `request.approve` / `request.reject` / `request.bulk_approve` / `request.bulk_reject` / `donation.match` / `delivery.assign` / `delivery.reassign` / `district.activate` / `district.deactivate` / `area_alert.approve` / `area_alert.reject` / `sos.status_update` / `community_report.verify` / `community_report.dismiss` / `flood_model.retrain` (this list has grown past what earlier handoffs kept updated here — `AuditLog.tsx`'s `ACTION_LABELS` map is the actual source of truth if this drifts again); `details` is a small action-specific object (e.g. `{victimName}` for a verify, `{ids, count}` for a bulk verify, `{category, matchedRequestId, deliveryId}` for a match, `{requestId, donationId, volunteerId}` for an assign, `{district, sourceAlertTitle}` for a district activate/deactivate). See "Audit log" and "Active emergency districts" below.

### activeDistricts
`{district, activatedAt, activatedBy, activatedByName, sourceAlertTitle}`, doc ID = slugified district name (e.g. `colombo`), one doc per **currently active** district — deactivating deletes the doc rather than flipping a boolean, so "does this doc exist" is the whole active/inactive check. `sourceAlertTitle` is set when activation came from the DMC-alert quick-action on `/admin/map`, `null` for a manual activation from `/admin/active-emergencies`. Public read (no PII), admin-only write. See "Active emergency districts" below.

### deliveryChats
`{deliveryId, requestId, donationId, pairKey: "donor_volunteer"|"volunteer_victim"|"donor_victim", partyAId, partyARole, partyBId, partyBRole, consentA, consentB, contactRevealed, status: "active"|"locked", createdAt, updatedAt}`. Doc ID is the deterministic `${deliveryId}_${pairKey}`. One chat per (delivery, pair) — a volunteer-delivery gets two (donor↔volunteer, volunteer↔victim), a self-delivery gets one (donor↔victim). Real name/phone are never stored here — only resolved on demand once `contactRevealed` is true. See "Delivery chat + consent-gated contact sharing" below.

### chatMessages
`{chatId, senderId, senderRole, text, createdAt}`. Top-level collection (same "reference the parent by id, don't nest" convention as `caseNotes`), append-only, sorted by `createdAt` in JS to avoid a composite index. See "Delivery chat + consent-gated contact sharing" below.

## Category management

Category caps used to be a hardcoded object requiring a code deploy to change. Now admin-editable at runtime:
- `server/src/utils/categories.js` — `getCategoryLimits()`, the shared async accessor every route uses (self-seeding, see above).
- `server/src/routes/categories.js` — `GET /api/categories` (any authed user — victim/donor forms need it too), `POST /api/categories` (admin, add a new category), `PATCH /api/categories/:key` (admin, adjust an existing category's cap/label/unit).
- `AdminCategories.tsx` (`/admin/categories`) — admin UI: inline cap editing per category + a form to add new ones.
- `VictimRequestForm.tsx` and `DonorDonationForm.tsx` both fetch `GET /api/categories` on mount instead of using a hardcoded list — a category added via the admin UI is immediately requestable and donatable with no code change. `VictimRequestForm.tsx` falls back to the raw Firestore `label` (via i18next's default-value param, `t(\`categories.${key}\`, limit.label)`) for any category not present in the locale JSON files — so new categories show in English until someone adds translated strings, rather than showing a raw i18n key.
- `requests.js` and `donations.js` both validate against `getCategoryLimits()` at request time rather than an imported constant — a cap change or new category takes effect on the very next request, no restart needed.
- Verified live end-to-end: lowered the baby-formula cap (4→2 tins) and confirmed the server rejects a 3-tin request with the updated number in the error message; added a new "Tarpaulins" category and confirmed it appeared in and was usable from both the victim request form and the donor donation form without any code change.

## Emergency broadcast banner

The manual, admin-curated equivalent of the DMC alerts feed — for messages the DMC RSS won't have (e.g. "Cyclone approaching Batticaloa, expect delivery delays").

- `server/src/routes/broadcasts.js` — `GET /active` (public, returns the current banner or `null`), `GET /` (admin, last 20 for history), `POST /` (admin, posts a new banner; body `{message, severity}`, severity defaults to `"warning"`), `PATCH /:id/deactivate` (admin, clears without replacing). Posting a new banner and deactivating the previous one happens in a single Firestore batch, so there's never a moment with two banners active.
- `AdminBroadcast.tsx` (`/admin/broadcast`) — shows the current active banner with a "Clear banner" button, a form to post a new one (message + severity: info/warning/critical), and a short history list below.
- `EmergencyBanner.tsx` — fetches `GET /api/broadcasts/active` once on mount and renders a colored bar (blue/amber/red by severity) with a "Close" button. Closing is **session-only** (the dismissed broadcast's id is stashed in `sessionStorage`) — it hides the banner for that browser tab's session, it does **not** deactivate it for anyone else. Only an admin clearing it from `/admin/broadcast` actually removes it for everyone.
- Embedded in two places: `Landing.tsx` (top of the public page, above the header) and `DashboardLayout.tsx` (top of the page, above the sidebar+content row, so every logged-in role — victim, donor, volunteer, admin — sees it regardless of which dashboard page they're on).
- No live-update mechanism yet — the banner is fetched once per page load, so if an admin posts a banner while a user already has a page open, they won't see it until they navigate or refresh. This is the same gap the upcoming "Live updates via Firestore `onSnapshot`" task (next up after this one) is meant to close — worth switching `EmergencyBanner.tsx` to a real-time listener at the same time rather than handling it twice.
- Verified live end-to-end: posted a "critical" banner as admin, confirmed it rendered on both `/admin/broadcast` and the public landing page (logged out), dismissed it client-side and confirmed the dismissal survived a reload but the banner reappeared for a fresh session, then cleared it from the admin page and confirmed `GET /api/broadcasts/active` returned `null` again everywhere.

## Internal case notes

A small, per-request comment thread for admin-to-admin handoff context ("called the victim, they confirmed access road is passable") — separate from the emergency broadcast banner, which is public-facing and one-at-a-time.

- `server/src/routes/requests.js` — `GET /:id/notes` and `POST /:id/notes` (both admin-only), reading/writing the top-level `caseNotes` collection. Both 404 if the request itself doesn't exist.
- `CaseNotesModal.tsx` — a modal (same overlay pattern as `QrScanModal.tsx`) showing the thread in chronological order plus a textarea + "Add note" button. Opened from a new "Notes" column/button on every row of `AdminRequests.tsx`.
- **Hit a real bug while building this, worth remembering:** the first version of `GET /:id/notes` used `.where("requestId", "==", id).orderBy("createdAt", "asc")`, which is a valid-looking query that actually throws `FAILED_PRECONDITION: The query requires an index` at runtime — Firestore needs a composite index for equality-filter-plus-orderBy-on-a-different-field, and none exists in this project (no `firestore.indexes.json`, everything so far has relied on either single-field queries or sorting in JS). Fixed by dropping the `orderBy` and sorting the fetched array by `createdAt` in JS instead — the same pattern `GET /api/requests` already uses for `priorityScore`. **If a future feature needs a Firestore query that filters on one field and orders by another, default to sorting in JS unless there's a real reason to push it to Firestore** — it avoids a deploy-time index dependency this project has otherwise avoided entirely.
- Notes are append-only by design — no edit or delete endpoint — so the handoff trail stays trustworthy as a lightweight record, not because it's meant to be a full audit log (that's its own upcoming task).
- Verified live: added two notes to a request, confirmed they rendered in order with author + timestamp, then opened a different request's notes and confirmed the thread was empty and correctly scoped (not shared across requests).

## Live updates (Firestore `onSnapshot`)

Directly answers dissertation Research Question 2 ("how can real-time digital information sharing improve collaboration") — demonstrable live in the viva: open `/admin/requests` in one browser, submit a request as a victim in another, watch it appear with no refresh.

- **Architecture: reads go live, writes stay on the Express API.** `AdminRequests.tsx`, `AdminDonations.tsx`, and `EmergencyBanner.tsx` now subscribe directly to Firestore via the client SDK's `onSnapshot` instead of a one-time `apiFetch` — `web/src/lib/firebase.ts` exports `db` (`getFirestore(firebaseApp)`) for this. Every *write* (verify, match, assign, post a banner, etc.) still goes through the Express backend exactly as before, because that's where business-rule validation lives (priority scoring, category caps, one-active-banner-at-a-time). The listener just means the UI updates the instant Firestore's data changes, regardless of whether that change came from this browser tab, another admin's tab, or the backend — no manual `load()`/refetch needed after a mutation.
- `AdminRequests.tsx` subscribes to the whole `aidRequests` collection unfiltered and sorts by `priorityScore` in JS (same sort as before, just now live). `AdminDonations.tsx` subscribes to `donations` the same way; its volunteer list stays a one-time `apiFetch` since that data doesn't need to be live. `EmergencyBanner.tsx` uses a `where("active", "==", true), limit(1)` query instead of `GET /api/broadcasts/active` — this is the one live listener that also runs for **logged-out** users on the public landing page.
- **Important discovery made while building this, corrects a wrong assumption repeated across earlier handoffs of this file:** Firestore security rules were believed to be undeployed/"test mode." They were not — `firestore-rules/firestore.rules` (deployed via `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules`, config in `firebase.json`/`.firebaserc`) was already live and enforced, which is exactly why building this feature surfaced a real bug: `broadcasts`, `categoryLimits`, and `caseNotes` had **zero rule coverage**, so `EmergencyBanner.tsx`'s direct read was silently denied (`permission-denied`) even though the same data was readable fine through the Express backend (Admin SDK bypasses rules entirely — this is why the gap went unnoticed until a client made a *direct* Firestore read). Fixed by adding rule blocks for all three collections and redeploying — confirmed with the user before deploying, since it's a live security change. See the corrected note in "Privacy and security notes" below.
- Verified live end-to-end, three separate ways: (1) approved a request via a `curl` call to the Express API while `/admin/requests` stayed open in a browser with no reload — the row's status and action buttons updated instantly; (2) mutated a donation's `status` field directly via the Admin SDK (bypassing the Express API entirely, simulating any external writer) while `/admin/donations` stayed open — same instant update, confirming this isn't just an artifact of the app's own fetch calls; (3) posted and then deactivated a broadcast via the API while the **logged-out** public landing page stayed open — the banner appeared and disappeared live, proving the public read path works under the newly-deployed rules, not just the authenticated admin path.
- Not made live in this pass (kept in scope with what the user asked for): `AdminCategories.tsx` and `AdminBroadcast.tsx`'s own history view still use one-time fetches. Extend them the same way later if it's ever worth it — lower value since caps/banners change far less often than requests/donations.

## Bulk approve/reject

For a real surge, going row-by-row through pending requests doesn't scale — this lets an admin select several at once.

- `PATCH /api/requests/bulk-verify` (admin) — body `{ids: string[], approve: boolean}`. Looks up all ids, batch-updates whichever ones actually exist, and reports `{status, updated, skipped}` rather than failing the whole call over one bad id.
- `AdminRequests.tsx` — a checkbox appears only on `pending` rows (mirrors the existing per-row Approve/Reject visibility), plus a "select all pending" checkbox in the header. Selecting any row reveals a bulk action bar (`N selected` + Approve/Reject/Clear). A `useEffect` keyed on the live `requests` data prunes the selection automatically if a selected row's status changes from somewhere else (another admin tab, a direct write) — so the selection can never point at a row that's no longer pending.
- Because `AdminRequests.tsx` already runs on the live `onSnapshot` listener (see "Live updates" above), no manual refetch happens after a bulk action — the listener picks up the batched Firestore write and the rows update themselves, same as every other write path on this page.
- Verified live: selected both existing pending requests via "select all," clicked "Approve selected," confirmed both flipped to `Verified` with no reload and the bulk bar/checkboxes disappeared correctly (no longer pending); reverted them back to `pending` afterward to leave demo data as found.

## Filters and search

The requests table stops being usable past ~30 rows without this — a filter bar sits above the table in `AdminRequests.tsx`.

- Filters: free-text search on `victimName` (case-insensitive substring), `disasterType` (fixed dropdown — flood/landslide/cyclone/drought/other, same list `VictimRequestForm.tsx` uses), `status`, `district`, and a `createdAt` date range (`<input type="date">` from/to, compared as ISO-string prefixes rather than parsed `Date` objects — works because `createdAt` is always a full ISO timestamp and lexicographic comparison of an ISO prefix behaves correctly here).
- All filters combine with AND, computed client-side via `useMemo` over the already-live `requests` array from the `onSnapshot` listener — no new backend endpoint, filtering is just a derived view over data that was already being fetched.
- **District isn't a stored field** on `aidRequests` — same as the public severity map, it's derived on the fly from `location` via nearest-centroid matching. Rather than adding a backend endpoint for this, `server/src/utils/districts.js`'s `DISTRICTS` list and `nearestDistrict()` function were mirrored into `web/src/lib/districts.ts` (identical Haversine formula, verified to match `server/src/utils/geo.js`'s exactly) so the filter can compute it client-side with no round trip. The district dropdown's options are derived from whatever districts are actually present in the current data (via the same `nearestDistrict` call), not the full static list of 25 — keeps the dropdown relevant instead of mostly-empty on a small demo dataset.
- Bulk-select ("select all pending") now operates on the *filtered* set, not the full dataset — selecting "all" while a filter is active only selects the visible pending rows, which is the behavior an admin would expect.
- A "Showing X of Y" counter and a "Clear filters" button (only rendered when a filter is active) sit at the end of the filter bar.
- Verified live: name search narrowed correctly to substring matches; status filter isolated exactly the delivered requests; district filter combined with status correctly returned zero results for a status/district pair with no overlap and the expected rows for one that did; a future "from" date correctly zeroed out all 7 seed requests, proving the date-range comparison works; "Clear filters" reset every field and made the button disappear.
- **User-reported alignment bug (fixed)**: the "Clear filters" button sat visibly higher than the rest of the filter row. Root cause: the filter bar is `flex items-end` (so every field's *bottom* edge lines up, since each field is a label-above-input stack of varying height), and the "Stale only" checkbox — the other label-less, single-line item in the row — already had `pb-1.5` to compensate and sit on the same baseline; "Clear filters" was missing that same padding. Fixed by adding `pb-1.5` to the button's className, matching the checkbox exactly. Verified live: `getBoundingClientRect()` on both elements after triggering a filter showed identical `bottom` values (293px), confirmed pixel-aligned.

## Resource gap view

Directly answers the "resource shortfall alerts" idea already sketched in the user's own Figure 1 rich picture — a panel showing categories in high demand vs. low donation supply (e.g. "12 pending water requests, 2 available water donations").

- `ResourceGapView.tsx` (`/admin/resource-gap`) — subscribes live to both `aidRequests` and `donations` via `onSnapshot` (same pattern as `AdminRequests.tsx`/`AdminDonations.tsx`), plus a one-time `GET /api/categories` fetch for labels/units. No new backend endpoint — this is a derived view computed client-side (`useMemo`) over data that's already being streamed for the other admin pages.
- Per category: **pending requests** = count of request *items* with `status === "pending"` in that category (not summed quantities — matches the "12 pending water requests" phrasing, i.e. how many separate needs are outstanding); **available donations** = count of donations with `status === "available"` in that category. `gap = pending - available`.
- Rows sort by `gap` descending, so the worst shortage is always at the top. Each row gets a badge: red "Short by N" when `gap > 0`, green "Covered" when supply meets or exceeds demand, gray "No activity" when both are zero (keeps a mostly-idle demo dataset from looking alarming).
- Verified live: cross-checked the rendered counts against a direct API tally (medicine: 1 pending/0 available → "Short by 1"; water: 0 pending/1 available → "Covered"; everything else 0/0 → "No activity"), then inserted a food donation directly via the Admin SDK (bypassing the Express API entirely) and confirmed a new "Food" row appeared with the correct count and "Covered" badge with no reload — proving the live-listener architecture extends cleanly to a derived/aggregated view, not just raw record lists.

## Stale request flags

Auto-highlights anything sitting unactioned too long, so an urgent case doesn't silently sink to the bottom of a long, priority-sorted list.

- Purely a derived/computed flag on `AdminRequests.tsx` — no new backend endpoint, no new Firestore field. `isStale(r)` is true when `status` is `"pending"` or `"verified"` (i.e. no item has matched yet — `"in_progress"` already means something moved) **and** `createdAt` is older than an admin-adjustable threshold (number input in the filter bar, default 6 hours).
- Stale rows get an amber `⏱ Stale (Nh)` badge in the existing Flags column (age shown in hours, or days once past 48h) and a subtle amber row tint — distinct from the blue selection tint, which still takes priority when a row is both selected and stale.
- A "Stale only" checkbox in the filter bar composes with every other filter (name/type/status/district/date range) the same way, since it's just one more clause in the same `filteredRequests` `useMemo`.
- **One real wrinkle worth remembering:** staleness changes purely because *time passes*, not because Firestore data changed — the existing `onSnapshot` listener only re-renders on a write. Without something extra, a request sitting at 5h59m wouldn't visibly flip to "stale" until some unrelated write happened to touch the collection. Fixed with a `setInterval` that ticks a dummy state value every 60 seconds purely to force a re-render (included as a dependency of the `filteredRequests` memo) — cheap, and the right fix for any future feature whose truth value depends on wall-clock time rather than data.
- Verified live: backdated one seed request's `createdAt` to 9 hours ago directly via the Admin SDK and confirmed it picked up "⏱ Stale (9h)" and the amber tint with no reload; independently, a different seed request already showed "⏱ Stale (24h)" on its own from real elapsed time, confirming the age math wasn't just matching the one forced test case. Toggling "Stale only" correctly narrowed the table to exactly those two rows ("Showing 2 of 7"); raising the threshold to 30h correctly excluded both ("Showing 0 of 7"). Reverted the backdated timestamp afterward.

## Audit log

Directly ties to the ethics chapter's commitment to reporting findings "fully and accurately" — a real, queryable trail of who approved/rejected/matched/assigned what and when, not just a claim that the system supports accountability.

- `server/src/utils/auditLog.js` — `logAction(actor, action, target, details)`, a small reusable helper. Wired into exactly the actions the user asked to cover ("who approved/rejected/assigned what") rather than every possible admin write, to keep this pass scoped: `PATCH /:id/verify` and `PATCH /bulk-verify` in `requests.js` (`request.approve`/`request.reject`/`request.bulk_approve`/`request.bulk_reject`), `POST /:id/match` in `donations.js` (`donation.match`), and `POST /` in `deliveries.js` (`delivery.assign`, the volunteer-assignment step). Category/broadcast/case-note CRUD is **not** logged yet — easy to extend with the same helper later if that's ever wanted.
- `GET /api/audit-log` (admin) — last 100 entries, `orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(100)`. Safe without a composite index here specifically because there's no `where` filter alongside the `orderBy` — the composite-index requirement that bit "Internal case notes" only kicks in when you combine an equality filter with an orderBy on a different field.
- `AuditLog.tsx` (`/admin/audit-log`) — a one-time fetch (like `AdminBroadcast.tsx`'s history view, not a live `onSnapshot` listener — deliberately kept out of the live-updates architecture for now, consistent with the "lower value to make live" reasoning already used for caps/banner history). Renders a color-coded action badge plus a human-readable one-line description per action type (`describeEntry()`).
- Deliberate architectural choice: writes still go through the Express API as the single source of truth; `logAction` is called from inside each route handler right after the Firestore write it's documenting succeeds, not via some separate hook or middleware — keeps the "what got logged" mapping to "what the code actually does" directly visible in each route file rather than inferred from a generic wrapper.
- Verified live end-to-end: triggered all four wired actions directly against the API — a single approve, a bulk-reject of two requests, a donation match, and a volunteer assignment — and confirmed each produced exactly one correctly-shaped `auditLog` entry, in the right order, rendering correctly on `/admin/audit-log` with accurate human-readable details (e.g. "Donation mWtf9XwHo9mHi2QrqES6 (water) → request YCu0nUm2Evcyr6rKq3qA", "Volunteer WRhx... → request YCu0n..."). All test requests/donations/deliveries and the test audit entries themselves were cleaned up afterward.

## CSV/PDF export

Lets an admin hand data upward (supervisor, examiners, a real DMC-adjacent org) without needing direct database access.

- `web/src/lib/export.ts` — two generic helpers, `exportToCsv(filename, headers, rows)` and `exportToPdf(filename, title, headers, rows)`, both pure client-side (a Blob + `<a download>` for CSV, `jspdf` + `jspdf-autotable` for PDF — new dependencies, added specifically for this feature). Both take plain `string[]` headers and `(string|number)[][]` rows, so the same two functions work for any table on the site, not just these two.
- **Requests** (`AdminRequests.tsx`) — "Export CSV"/"Export PDF" buttons sit next to the existing "Showing X of Y" counter and export exactly `filteredRequests` — i.e. whatever the filter bar currently shows, including the date range already built for "Filters and search". This is what gives "for a given period" for free: set a From/To range, then export, no separate period picker needed.
- **Donations** (`AdminDonations.tsx`) — had no filter bar yet (out of scope to build one just for this), so a small standalone "Period from/to" date pair was added purely to scope the export, plus the same two buttons. Both disable when the current period has zero rows.
- Columns: requests export Priority/Victim/Disaster/Items/Severity/Affected/Status/District/Created At (items joined as `category xN; category xN`); donations export Donor/Category/Quantity/Delivery/Status/Created At.
- Verified live: downloaded and read back both formats from both pages. CSV rows matched the on-screen table exactly (multi-item requests correctly semicolon-joined). The PDF included a title, a generated-at timestamp + row count, and a correctly formatted table — confirmed by actually opening the downloaded file, not just checking the click didn't error. Confirmed the donations period filter's disabled state: setting "from" to a future date correctly zeroed the row count and disabled both export buttons.

## Volunteer workload view

Small addition once there are multiple volunteers in test data — a table showing deliveries per volunteer so an admin doesn't overload one person while others sit idle.

- `VolunteerWorkload.tsx` (`/admin/volunteer-workload`) — one-time `GET /api/users/volunteers` fetch (volunteer roster changes rarely) plus a live `onSnapshot` on the `deliveries` collection (same live-listener pattern as everywhere else). No new backend endpoint — purely a client-side aggregation (`useMemo`) over data that's already flowing.
- Per volunteer: **Active** = deliveries in `pending_acceptance`/`accepted`/`picked_up`; **Completed** = `delivered`/`confirmed`; **Rejected** = `rejected`; **Total assigned** = every delivery ever assigned to them regardless of status. Rows sort by Active descending (busiest first), then Total.
- Starts from the full volunteer roster, not just volunteers with deliveries — a volunteer with zero assignments still gets a row with an "Idle" badge, which is the point of the feature (surfacing who's sitting idle, not just who's busy). Active count also gets a badge: gray "Idle" at 0, plain number at 1–2, amber "N — High load" at 3+.
- **One real data-quality find while verifying this, not a bug in the new code:** one pre-existing test delivery had `status: "assigned"`, a value that doesn't appear anywhere in `deliveries.js`'s actual status enum (`pending_acceptance → accepted/rejected → picked_up → delivered → confirmed`) — leftover from an earlier iteration of the schema before this session. It correctly counts toward **Total assigned** but doesn't fall into Active/Completed/Rejected (none of which match "assigned"), so those three columns don't sum to the total for that one row. This is accurate given the data, not a display bug — flagging here in case a future session wonders why the numbers don't add up for volunteer1. Worth a one-off cleanup of that stray doc if it's ever noticed during a demo.
- Verified live: cross-checked rendered counts against a direct Firestore query (Volunteer Reject: 0 completed/2 rejected; Test Volunteer: 2 completed/0 rejected; volunteer1: 0/0 with 1 stray "assigned" delivery in Total only) — exact match. Then inserted three `picked_up` test deliveries for volunteer1 directly via the Admin SDK and watched their row jump to the top with a live "3 — High load" badge and Total updating from 1 to 4, with no reload. Test deliveries cleaned up afterward.

## Volunteer availability toggle

Lets volunteers mark themselves active/inactive so the admin's assignment dropdown only shows people actually available right now, instead of everyone regardless of whether they're currently free.

- `PATCH /api/users/availability` (volunteer-only) — body `{available: boolean}`, updates the caller's own `users` doc. No admin override endpoint — only the volunteer themselves can flip their own flag, consistent with how `nic`/`homeAddress` etc. are self-owned fields.
- `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` — a pill toggle next to the page title ("Available for new deliveries" in green / "Unavailable" in gray), fetches current status from `GET /api/users/me` on mount and calls the new endpoint on click. Deliberately kept local to this component rather than added to the global `AuthContext` profile type — nothing else in the app needs to react to a volunteer's own availability changing, so there was no reason to widen a shared type for one page.
- `AdminDonations.tsx` — the assign-volunteer `<select>` now filters `volunteers` down to `available !== false` before rendering options (excluded, not just disabled — matches "only shows people actually available"). If every registered volunteer is currently unavailable, the dropdown is replaced with "No volunteers currently available." instead of an empty select; a similar note appears at the bottom of the page distinguishing "no volunteers exist yet" from "volunteers exist but none are available."
- `VolunteerWorkload.tsx` also picked up an "Availability" column (green "Available" / gray "Unavailable" badge) since it's a natural companion view — an admin deciding who to assign wants both "are they free" and "are they already overloaded" in one place.
- **Backwards compatibility:** every check uses `available !== false` rather than `available === true`, so volunteer accounts created before this field existed (no `available` field at all) are correctly treated as available by default — no backfill migration needed.
- Verified live: toggled volunteer1 to unavailable from their own `/deliveries/mine` page, confirmed the change persisted in Firestore directly via the Admin SDK, then confirmed from the admin side that volunteer1 disappeared from `AdminDonations.tsx`'s assignment dropdown (down to 2 of 3 volunteers) and showed "Unavailable" on `/admin/volunteer-workload` — both live, no reload. Reverted afterward.

## Automatic volunteer assignment

Restores the original workflow described in the dissertation's Chapter 02 (§2.5.3, step 6) — "Available volunteers are assigned to matched request-donation pairs based on proximity to the donation pickup location" — which the earlier manual-dropdown-only version had deviated from. Decided while writing the thesis's Chapter 4 (SRS); two open questions were confirmed with the user before building: **the rule** is nearest-available-volunteer by Haversine distance, tie-broken by fewest active deliveries; **manual assignment stays** as an admin override, not replaced outright.

- **`server/src/utils/autoAssignVolunteer.js`** — `findBestVolunteer(pickupLocation)`. Queries `users` where `role == "volunteer"`, filters to `available !== false` **and has a `location` on file** in JS (not a second Firestore `where`, avoiding a composite-index dependency — same convention as "Internal case notes"), ranks by `distanceKm()` (reusing `server/src/utils/geo.js`, the same function `donations.js` already uses for donation-to-request matching), tie-broken by each candidate's count of deliveries in `ACTIVE_DELIVERY_STATUSES` (`pending_acceptance`/`accepted`/`picked_up` — same "active" definition `VolunteerWorkload.tsx` already uses). Returns `null` if nobody qualifies, rather than throwing — matching is never blocked by assignment failing.
- **A real gap this surfaced, not initially obvious: volunteers never had a location to rank by.** `Register.tsx` never captures one for any role, so `users.location` was always `null` for volunteers — "nearest by distance" would have silently never matched anyone. Fixed with a new **`PATCH /api/users/location`** endpoint (volunteer-only, same self-owned-field pattern as `PATCH /availability`) and a **"Set my location" pill on `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx`**, right next to the existing availability toggle — amber when unset, green "Location set — update" once captured, with the same `navigator.geolocation` pattern used elsewhere in this app. A volunteer who's available but hasn't set a location simply isn't eligible for auto-assignment yet (still shows up in the manual "Reassign" dropdown, which isn't distance-aware) — no backfill, no forced prompt, just opt-in like every other self-owned field in this project.
- **`donations.js`'s `POST /:id/match`** now calls `findBestVolunteer()` for volunteer-delivery donations (self-delivery is untouched — it already auto-creates its own accepted delivery, no volunteer involved) right after the request match, auto-creating the `pending_acceptance` delivery in the same request/response cycle rather than requiring a second admin action. Logs `delivery.assign` with `details.source: "auto"`. If nobody qualifies, the donation is left unassigned exactly like before this feature existed, and the response's `autoAssignedVolunteer` field is `null` so the frontend can say so.
- **`deliveries.js`'s `POST /` is now also the reassign path**, not just fresh-assign. If the donation already has a delivery: reassigning is allowed (updates the *same* delivery doc's `volunteerId`, keeps `status: "pending_acceptance"`, logs `delivery.reassign` with `previousVolunteerId`) only while that delivery is still `pending_acceptance`; once the volunteer has accepted (or moved further), it's blocked with a 409 — swapping volunteers on someone already committed would corrupt state they're actively acting on. Deliberately updates the existing doc rather than creating a new one, so nothing downstream (the volunteer's `GET /mine` list, QR confirmation, audit log) needs a second doc to reconcile.
- **`AdminDonations.tsx`** — "Find match" now surfaces who got auto-assigned ("Auto-assigned to X (nearest available)") or that nobody qualified. The volunteer picker no longer only appears when unassigned — it also appears (relabeled "Reassign to...") whenever a delivery is still `pending_acceptance`, and disappears once accepted, matching exactly what the backend allows.
- **`AuditLog.tsx`** — `delivery.assign` entries now show the volunteer's name and, when auto-assigned, "(auto-assigned, nearest available)"; a new `delivery.reassign` entry type shows both the new and previous volunteer.
- Verified end-to-end via real HTTP calls (temp admin + 2 temp volunteers at different simulated locations + a donor + a victim, real Firebase ID tokens): matched a volunteer-delivery donation and confirmed the **nearer** of the two volunteers was auto-assigned, not the farther one; reassigned to the second volunteer while still `pending_acceptance` and confirmed it succeeded and updated the same delivery doc; had that volunteer accept, then confirmed a further reassign attempt was correctly rejected with 409; confirmed both a `delivery.assign` (`source: "auto"`) and a `delivery.reassign` (`previousVolunteerId` set) audit entry were recorded with accurate details. Also click-verified in the browser: registered a throwaway volunteer, confirmed the "Set my location"/"Available for new deliveries" pills render correctly side by side, and confirmed clicking "Set my location" correctly shows the "Couldn't get your location" error state — this browser automation environment can't grant geolocation permission (same known limitation already noted elsewhere in this file for QR camera scanning and the active-districts soft note). All test accounts and data deleted afterward.

## Admin dashboard KPIs

The last item on the user's operational-features list — turns `/dashboard` from a quick-links menu into an actual command-center view for the admin role specifically (other roles are unchanged).

- `Dashboard.tsx` — a new `AdminKpiRow` component, rendered only when `profile.role === "admin"`, sits above the existing quick-links grid on the same shared `/dashboard` route. Subscribes live via `onSnapshot` to `aidRequests`, `donations`, and `deliveries` (same pattern as every other admin view) and renders four `StatCard`s (the same shared component used on `Landing.tsx`/`TransparencyDashboard.tsx`):
  - **Pending requests** — count of `aidRequests` with `status === "pending"`.
  - **Unmatched donations** — count of `donations` with `status === "available"`.
  - **Active deliveries** — count of `deliveries` with `status` in `pending_acceptance`/`accepted`/`picked_up` (same `ACTIVE_STATUSES` concept as "Volunteer workload view").
  - **Avg. time to verification** — average of `verifiedAt - createdAt` across every request that has ever been verified (regardless of what status it's since moved to), formatted as minutes/hours/days depending on magnitude. Shows "—" when no request has been verified yet, rather than 0 or NaN.
- **Needed a real backend change to be accurate, not just a frontend view:** averaging verification time requires knowing exactly when each request was first approved. `updatedAt` doesn't work for this — it gets overwritten by every subsequent change (matched, delivered, etc.), so it stops meaning "time of verification" the moment a verified request progresses further. Added a `verifiedAt` field to `aidRequests`, set once in both `PATCH /:id/verify` and `PATCH /bulk-verify` the first time a request is approved (`approve === true && !doc.data().verifiedAt`) — never overwritten on a later re-verify, and never set at all for rejected requests, so the average is only ever computed over genuinely-verified requests.
- Verified live: cross-checked initial KPI values against a direct API tally (2 pending, 1 unmatched donation, 0 active deliveries, "—" for avg. verification since no request had a `verifiedAt` yet) — exact match. Then approved one pending request via the API while `/dashboard` stayed open with no reload — Pending requests dropped 2→1 and Avg. time to verification populated with "2.7 days" instantly, correctly reflecting that request's real elapsed time since submission. Reverted the test request back to pending (and cleared its `verifiedAt`) afterward.

## Active emergency districts

Not from the original "one at a time" list — a follow-on request refining and extending the emergency broadcast banner. Came with five explicit, non-negotiable constraints from the user, all honored by design:

1. **"Never hard-block submission. The form is always open... non-negotiable given your own research problem statement."** `server/src/routes/requests.js`'s `POST /` handler has zero references to `activeDistricts` anywhere — not "checked but always allows," genuinely no code path connects them. District status only ever adds an informational note; grep for `activeDistrict` in that file to re-confirm this if it's ever in doubt.
2. **"Admin marks specific districts as 'active emergency,' not a single system-wide toggle — Sri Lanka's disasters are usually localized."** Modeled as one Firestore doc per *currently active* district (collection `activeDistricts`, doc ID = slugified name) rather than a single boolean — each of the 25 districts is independently activated/deactivated. Deactivating **deletes** the doc rather than flipping a flag to false, so "does this doc exist" is the entire active-check, both server- and client-side.
3. **"DMC alerts feed into this as a suggestion, not an automatic switch... a one-click 'mark [district] as active' button right next to each incoming alert, rather than trying to auto-parse district names out of bulletin text reliably."** `SituationMap.tsx`'s existing DMC alerts panel (`/admin/map`) now has a district `<select>` + "Mark active" button under every alert. The admin reads the bulletin and picks the district themselves — deliberately no NLP/regex attempt to extract a district from `alert.title`/`alert.summary`, exactly per the instruction. The activation call includes `sourceAlertTitle` so `/admin/active-emergencies` and the audit log both show which DMC bulletin (if any) prompted it, purely for context — it plays no role in the activation logic itself.
4. **"If a victim submits from a district that's not currently marked active, show a soft note, not a block."** `VictimRequestForm.tsx` fetches `GET /api/active-districts` once on mount, computes `nearestDistrict(location)` (same client util as `AdminRequests.tsx`'s filters) once the victim captures their location, and shows *"No declared emergency currently active in your area — your request will still be reviewed."* directly under the location button when their district isn't in the active set. Purely additive to existing markup — never touches `error` state, never disables the submit button, never appears in `handleSubmit`'s validation at all.
5. **"This also powers the emergency broadcast banner — same underlying 'active districts' data drives both."** `EmergencyBanner.tsx` now renders **two independent, stackable bars**: the existing manual broadcast (unchanged) and a new one driven by a live `onSnapshot` on `activeDistricts` — e.g. *"Active emergency declared in: Colombo, Kalutara"* — shown whenever the active-district list is non-empty, on both the public landing page and every logged-in dashboard. Both bars are independently dismissible per session (the district bar's dismiss key is the sorted, joined district list, so a *change* to which districts are active — not just any Firestore write — makes it reappear even if the old set was dismissed).

Also built: `AdminActiveDistricts.tsx` (`/admin/active-emergencies`) — the primary management surface, a full table of all 25 districts (from the same `DISTRICTS` list `AdminRequests.tsx`'s filters use) with live status, who activated each one, its source (DMC alert title or "Manual"), and an activate/deactivate button per row.

- `server/src/routes/activeDistricts.js` — `GET /` (public), `POST /` (admin, body `{district, sourceAlertTitle?}`, validates against the real 25-district list, idempotent — reactivating just refreshes the record), `DELETE /:district` (admin). Both writes call `logAction` (`district.activate` / `district.deactivate`), extending `AuditLog.tsx`'s `ACTION_LABELS`/`describeEntry` to cover them.
- Needed a new Firestore rule block (`activeDistricts`: public read, admin write) since `EmergencyBanner.tsx` and `AdminActiveDistricts.tsx` both read it directly via the client SDK, same reasoning as `broadcasts`. Deployed via `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` — confirmed with the user before deploying, consistent with how the live-updates and category/broadcast/case-note rule additions were handled earlier.
- Verified live end-to-end: activated Kalutara from `/admin/active-emergencies`, watched it appear instantly on the public landing page's banner ("Active emergency declared in: Kalutara") with no reload; confirmed via direct API calls that `POST /api/active-districts` rejects an invalid district name (400) and that `POST /api/requests` has no dependency on district state at all (grep, not just testing, per constraint #1 above); confirmed the audit log recorded both the activate and a subsequent deactivate correctly. Deactivated Kalutara afterward to leave demo data clean.
- **Two things not exercisable in this dev/tooling environment, noted rather than glossed over:** (a) the DMC RSS feed happened to be empty at verification time (its documented normal state — see "External disaster data" above), so the per-alert quick-action button couldn't be clicked against a real bulletin; the same `POST /api/active-districts` call it makes (including `sourceAlertTitle`) was verified directly instead. (b) `navigator.geolocation` isn't available in the browser automation tooling used for verification (same limitation already noted for the QR camera-scan flow), so the victim-form soft note couldn't be click-tested through a real "capture location" round trip — verified instead by confirming `GET /api/active-districts` returns correct data and that `nearestDistrict()` is the same already-verified function used elsewhere. Worth a manual phone/browser check before treating this as fully proven.

### Follow-on: activating a district notifies the victims already in it

Direct user ask, once the mobile notification-history feature existed to actually surface it: "if the victim is also from that district and I enable active emergency send notification."

- **`server/src/routes/activeDistricts.js`'s `notifyDistrictActivated(district)`** (new, exported by attaching it to the router — same "Express Router instances are just functions and can carry extra properties" convention `waterLevelAlerts.js`'s `router.fetchWaterLevels` already established) reuses `findVictimsInDistrict()` from `waterLevelAlerts.js` (exported for this purpose) — the exact same district-matching logic already built and proven for water-level/reservoir alerts. There's no separate "location" field on a victim's own profile to match against (only volunteers have one, for auto-assignment ranking) — a victim's only location signal is whichever active (`pending`/`verified`/`in_progress`) request they have, same as every other "find victims in a district" feature in this app.
- Called from `POST /` (the actual activation route) right after the Firestore write and audit log succeed, and returned in the response as `notifiedCount` so an admin can see it worked without needing to check the database. **Also wired into the community-reports promotion path** (`communityReports.js`'s `PATCH /:id/verify` with `activateDistrict: true`) — that route writes to the same `activeDistricts` collection through its own direct Firestore write rather than calling this route over HTTP, so without explicitly importing and calling `notifyDistrictActivated()` there too, a district activated via a verified volunteer report would have silently skipped notifying anyone, while one activated from `/admin/active-emergencies` or the DMC quick-action would not have — an inconsistency worth catching rather than leaving as a gap only one of the two paths closes.
- **FCM only, deliberately no SMS** — unlike the water/reservoir area alerts (which the user explicitly approved spending Text.lk quota on for their specific trigger points), this is a brand-new trigger that fires on every one of 25 districts' activations; extending real SMS spend to a new trigger needs its own deliberate go-ahead per this project's standing SMS-testing discipline, not an assumed extension of an earlier approval.
- Verified live end-to-end via a real HTTP call to the actual route (not a direct Firestore write bypassing it): created a throwaway admin + victim, gave the victim a real pending request located at Ratnapura's centroid, called the real `POST /api/active-districts` with `{district: "Ratnapura"}` as the admin — got back `notifiedCount: 1`, then confirmed via `GET /api/notifications/mine` as the victim that the exact notification ("Active emergency declared" / "An active emergency has been declared in Ratnapura...") was actually there. Confirmed Ratnapura wasn't already an active district before running this, to avoid clobbering real state. All test accounts, the test request, the notification, and the test activation itself were cleaned up afterward.

## GDACS disaster alerts

Tropical cyclone tracking and general disaster event alerts (earthquakes, floods, droughts, wildfires) from GDACS, filtered to ones relevant to Sri Lanka — requested as a new integration, not from the original feature list.

- **The originally-requested GeoJSON REST API doesn't currently work — verified by repeated testing, not assumed.** `https://www.gdacs.org/gdacsapi/api/Events/geteventlist/SEARCH` was hit ~10 times over several minutes, with and without query params (`eventtypes=TC`, date ranges). Every attempt either timed out at the connection level or returned a 400 with GDACS's own backend error: `"Timeout expired. The timeout period elapsed prior to obtaining a connection from the pool... max pool size was reached"` — a server-side resource-exhaustion error on GDACS's end, not a malformed-request problem on this project's end. Flagged to the user directly (their message had asserted the endpoint was "confirmed working") rather than silently substituting; the user chose to proceed with the working alternative below.
- **Built against GDACS's own RSS feed instead** (`https://www.gdacs.org/xml/rss.xml`) — reliably reachable, and turned out to have richer structured per-event data than the REST API's response shape was ever actually confirmed to have (this project never got a single successful response from it to compare). Parsed with `fast-xml-parser`, same as the DMC alerts feed. Exact same substitution pattern already established for DMC (their RSS works, their PDF-only situation reports don't get scraped) — verify what's actually usable, use that, document why rather than force the nominally-requested source.
- `server/src/routes/external.js`'s new `GET /gdacs` — public, cached 15 min (`utils/cache.js`, same pattern as `/alerts` and `/water-levels`). Each RSS `<item>` carries `gdacs:eventtype` (TC/EQ/FL/DR/WF/...), `gdacs:eventname` (e.g. "DOLPHIN-26" for a named cyclone), `gdacs:alertlevel` (Green/Orange/Red), `gdacs:country`/`gdacs:iso3`, a `geo:Point`, and a `gdacs:bbox` (`lonmin lonmax latmin latmax`) — confirmed by parsing the real feed and inspecting actual events (a live Pacific cyclone, Canada/Japan earthquakes), not guessed from documentation.
- **Sri Lanka relevance filter (`isRelevantToSriLanka`)** — a GDACS event is kept if `iso3 === "LKA"`, or `country` mentions Sri Lanka, or the event's `bbox` geographically covers Sri Lanka's centroid (`7.8731, 80.7718` — the same point `SituationMap.tsx` already centers its Leaflet map on), or the title/description mentions Sri Lanka as a last resort. The bbox check matters because tropical cyclones over open ocean often have no `country`/`iso3` at all (confirmed on 2 of the 3 real TC events in the feed at research time) — country-only filtering would silently miss exactly the event type this integration was requested for.
- Frontend: a new list panel — same visual pattern as the existing DMC warnings list (event-type badge, alert-level color badge, title/name as a link, summary) — added to both `SituationMap.tsx` (admin, English-only, alongside the DMC quick-action list) and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx` (public, translated — new i18n keys `severityMap.gdacs*` added to all three locale files). Usually shows "no events" — same as DMC — since Sri Lanka rarely has an active GDACS-tracked event; that's real state, not a broken feature.
- Verified live: unit-tested `isRelevantToSriLanka` against 6 synthetic cases (iso3 match, country-text match, bbox-contains match, bbox-excludes match, title-text match, and a genuinely irrelevant Canada earthquake) — all correct, confirming the filter is actually selective rather than trivially returning empty. Cross-checked the real feed's normalized event shape against exactly what the frontend's `GdacsEvent` interface expects (field-for-field match, including `summary` from `stripHtml(item.description)`). Confirmed both `SituationMap.tsx` and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx` render the correct "no events" empty state live in the browser, matching the feed's actual current content (no Sri Lanka-relevant events at verification time).

### Cyclone track polygon + colored marker on `/admin/map`

A follow-on request: draw the storm's geometry on the existing Leaflet map, not just list it as text.

- **The RSS `<item>` itself has no geometry beyond a single current point — had to go find the real per-event geometry resource, not guess a field name.** GDACS's `/gdacsapi/api/polygons/getgeometry` endpoint (the natural guess for a "geometryUrl") is under the same `/gdacsapi/` path as the broken `geteventlist/SEARCH` API and failed with the identical connection-timeout pattern — confirmed by testing it directly, not assumed to be broken by association. What does work: every RSS item's `gdacs:cap` field links to that event's **CAP (Common Alerting Protocol) XML** file, served from a different path (`/contentdata/resources/...`, reliably reachable, confirmed 200). Its `<info><area><polygon>` element contains the storm's current hazard-area polygon as space-separated `lat,lng` pairs — already Leaflet's native `[lat, lng]` order, no coordinate-swap needed, confirmed by fetching a real cyclone's CAP file and parsing it (450 points, properly closed).
- **Important, honest caveat — this is the current episode's hazard/uncertainty-area polygon, not a literal multi-point historical track line.** GDACS's RSS only ever exposes the latest episode; the REST API that would provide episode-by-episode history is the same one that's down. So the polygon shown is "here's the storm's current affected-area shape," not "here's everywhere it's been." Worth knowing before describing this as track history anywhere it matters (e.g. the dissertation write-up).
- `server/src/routes/external.js` — new `fetchCapPolygon(capUrl)` helper, called for every Sri Lanka-relevant event's `capUrl` inside the same cached `GET /gdacs` response (cheap since there are rarely more than 0–2 relevant events at once). Fails soft to `geometry: null` per event on any error, same pattern as the rest of this file.
- `SituationMap.tsx` — each event renders a Leaflet `<Polygon>` (when `geometry` is present) and a `<CircleMarker>` at its current position, both colored by `alertLevel` using `GDACS_ALERT_LEAFLET_COLOR` — the exact same hex values (`#16a34a`/`#f97316`/`#dc2626`) already used by `SEVERITY_COLOR`/`AREA_LEVEL_COLOR` elsewhere on this page, just re-keyed by GDACS's Green/Orange/Red instead of a status string, so the map reads consistently regardless of which layer you're looking at. The marker's popup follows the same `<strong>name</strong><br/>detail<br/>detail` pattern as the existing request/donation/gauge popups: event name, `severityText` (e.g. wind speed), and a formatted from–to date range.
- **Its own fourth view-mode tab ("Global Alerts (GDACS)"), not layered on top of the other three.** The first version rendered the GDACS layer unconditionally across all view modes on the theory that a cyclone is relevant context regardless of lens — the user pushed back immediately ("its in request tab, lets have a separate one for this so it doesnt mess up"), so `ViewMode` gained a fourth `"gdacs"` value alongside `requests`/`areas`/`gauges`, matching the existing pattern exactly: its own toggle button, its own legend entries (swapped from an unconditional `gdacsEvents.length > 0` check to `viewMode === "gdacs"`), its own empty-state message, and the `<Polygon>`/`<CircleMarker>` rendering gated the same way. The Requests tab's legend and map are back to showing only request/donation markers, nothing GDACS-related bleeding in.
- Verified live end-to-end: temporarily relaxed the Sri Lanka relevance filter (`.filter(i => i["gdacs:eventtype"] === "TC")`, one real cyclone forced through) to pull a genuine event with real geometry through the full pipeline — confirmed 450 polygon points and correct `severityText`/`location` via direct API call, then confirmed in the browser: the DOM's `.leaflet-overlay-pane` contained `#16a34a`-stroked path elements (correctly matching that storm's `Green` alert level), and after panning the map out to the Pacific (via dispatched wheel events — click-based zoom controls were unreliable in this browser-automation environment, not a sign of an app bug) the actual elongated hazard-area polygon was visible on screen at the right location. Reverted the temporary filter change immediately after and confirmed the endpoint returned to its normal empty-array state, with no leftover test code.

### Global scope toggle — always something real to demo

Sri Lanka realistically has an active GDACS event on only a small fraction of days. The user flagged the obvious viva risk directly: "if was to be questioned in my viva saying nothing shows i could still show global event."

- `GET /api/external/gdacs` now takes an optional `?scope=global` query param (default stays `sri-lanka`, i.e. today's filtered behavior, unchanged for every existing caller). Global scope skips `isRelevantToSriLanka` entirely and returns every current GDACS event worldwide — typically 300+ (mostly wildfires; the live feed at verification time had 324 total across EQ/WF/FL/TC/DR). Cached separately per scope (`gdacs-events-sri-lanka` vs `gdacs-events-global`) since they're different payloads with different costs.
- **CAP polygon geometry is only ever fetched for `eventType === "TC"`, in both scopes** — fetching it for a global list that can include 280+ wildfires would be a real N+1 cost for data those event types don't have anyway (only cyclones were ever confirmed to carry a usable `<polygon>`, see above). This also slightly tightens the sri-lanka-scope behavior from the previous pass (which fetched geometry for any event type with a `capUrl`, harmless since it was never populated for non-TC events but not technically correct either).
- `SituationMap.tsx`'s GDACS tab gained a "Show all global events" checkbox, wired to a `gdacsScope` state that drives three things at once so they never drift out of sync: the map's markers/polygons, the legend's empty-state text, and the "Global Disaster Alerts" list panel below the map (shared `gdacsEvents` state — flipping the checkbox re-fetches once and every consumer updates together). The list panel caps itself to the first 20 events in global scope with a "Showing 20 of N" note, since rendering 300+ list items would be unusable; the map layer renders markers for all of them (Leaflet handles hundreds of `CircleMarker`s fine) plus polygons for whichever are cyclones.
- Verified live: toggled the checkbox on `/admin/map`'s GDACS tab and confirmed real global events rendered immediately — actual earthquakes (Canada, Japan, Peru), wildfires (Greece, Angola), correctly labeled and colored, "Showing 20 of 324 current events worldwide" note present, and 183 marker paths confirmed in the DOM (the subset of the 324 events with valid coordinates). Toggled back off and confirmed it cleanly reverted to the real (empty) Sri Lanka state with no leftover global data.

### GDACS + earthquake map layers on the public severity map

A follow-on request, worth documenting exactly how it was disambiguated since the ask itself was genuinely unclear at first. The user pasted the five `SituationMap.tsx` (admin) tab labels — Requests, Areas Affected, River Gauges, Global Alerts (GDACS), Tsunami Risk (Earthquakes) — and asked "this 3 also should be available in the page dont you think?" without saying which page or which 3. Asked a clarifying question rather than guess; the user's answer just re-pasted three of the same five labels (Requests, GDACS, Earthquakes), still not naming a target page. Given the context — GDACS and Earthquakes existed as **list panels only** on the public `/severity-map` page (not as map layers, unlike the admin map), and this project's own established precedent is the user pushing back exactly this way before (*"isnt it better to have USGS earthquake tsunami-risk indicator as another tab like global alerts?"*) — the most consistent reading was: add GDACS and Earthquakes as real map layers on the **public** severity map too, matching what admin's map already has.

**"Requests" was deliberately left out, and this was stated directly rather than silently dropped.** Admin's "Requests" tab plots exact victim pinpoint locations — publishing that on the public map would violate this project's own non-negotiable privacy design (see "Privacy and security notes"), which is exactly *why* `SituationMap.tsx` (exact pins, admin-only) and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx` (aggregates only) are two separate pages backed by two separate endpoints in the first place. The public page's "Areas Affected" tab already *is* the privacy-safe public equivalent of "Requests" — it was there already, nothing new needed.

- **`AreaSeverityMap.tsx`** (the shared component behind both `Landing.tsx`'s embedded preview and the full `/severity-map` page) gained two more `ViewMode` values, `"gdacs"` and `"earthquakes"`, reusing the exact rendering already proven on `SituationMap.tsx`: GDACS renders a `<Polygon>` (when CAP geometry exists) + `<CircleMarker>` colored by `GDACS_ALERT_COLOR` (Green/Orange/Red); earthquakes render `<CircleMarker>`s sized and colored by magnitude, with a `MapViewController` (`useMap()` + `useEffect` on `viewMode`) that pans out to the regional Sumatra-inclusive view on that tab and back to the normal Sri-Lanka view on every other tab — same recentering trick already used on the admin map, now generalized into the shared component.
- **Gated behind a new `extraLayers` prop, default `false`.** `PublicSeverityMap.tsx` passes `extraLayers` (so its four tabs show); `Landing.tsx`'s embedded preview does not (so the homepage teaser stays exactly as restrained as before — two tabs, no GDACS/earthquake clutter on a first-impression page). Both `gdacsEvents`/`earthquakes` are lazy-fetched only when their tab is first opened, matching the existing river-gauge lazy-load pattern in this same component.
- The list panels already on `/severity-map` for GDACS and earthquakes are unchanged and still there below the map — same "both a map layer and a list" pattern already established for these two feeds on the admin page.
- Verified live: all four tabs render correctly on `/severity-map`; clicking "Regional Seismic Activity" recentered the map out to the Bay of Bengal automatically and showed real gray/amber earthquake markers with accurate popups (e.g. "M 5.0 — 180 km NW of Sabang, Indonesia"); clicking back to "Global Disaster Alerts (GDACS)" correctly recentered to the normal Sri-Lanka view with the right (empty, real) state and legend; confirmed via `get_page_text` that `Landing.tsx`'s embedded preview still shows only "Areas Affected"/"River Levels" with no regression from the shared-component change; no console errors on either page.

### Follow-on: the landing page's restraint was explicitly reversed

The "two tabs only, no clutter on a first-impression page" decision immediately above was a deliberate design choice at the time — but the user later asked directly for River Gauges/GDACS/Earthquakes to be on the homepage too, "just like in admin's situation map." This supersedes that restraint, not a bug fix: `Landing.tsx`'s `<AreaSeverityMap>` now passes `extraLayers` (one-line change), so the homepage's embedded 420px preview shows all four tabs, same as `/severity-map` and admin's map. `AreaSeverityMap.tsx`'s own doc comment was updated to stop describing the old "off by default for the landing page" behavior. Verified live: all four tab buttons present on `/`, clicking "Regional Seismic Activity — Tsunami Risk Indicator" rendered 14 real earthquake markers on the homepage map.

### Admin Situation Map: list panels no longer bleed across tabs

User-reported: viewing the "Requests" tab on `/admin/map` also showed the full GDACS and Earthquakes list panels below the map (the earthquake list alone is 13 entries) — visually unrelated clutter on a tab about something else entirely. Unlike the map *layers themselves* (already correctly gated by `viewMode`), the two **list panels** below the map had been rendering unconditionally on every tab since they were first added, and nobody had come back to fix that after the GDACS/Earthquakes tabs existed.

- **Fix**: wrapped both the "Global Disaster Alerts (GDACS)" and "Regional Seismic Activity — Tsunami Risk Indicator" list-panel `<div>`s in `SituationMap.tsx` with `{viewMode === "gdacs" && (...)}` / `{viewMode === "earthquakes" && (...)}` respectively — same gating already used for their map layers and legends, just extended to the list panels underneath.
- **Deliberately left alone at the time**: the DMC alerts list, the reservoir storage panel, and the community reports list still showed on every tab — the user's report specifically named GDACS and Earthquakes as unrelated clutter on the Requests tab; the other three panels weren't flagged and weren't touched. (The reservoir storage panel was later removed entirely — see the follow-on below.)
- Verified live: registered a throwaway admin, confirmed the Requests tab now shows only DMC/Reservoir/Community-Reports panels (no GDACS or Earthquake content), and confirmed clicking the "Global Alerts (GDACS)" tab still correctly shows its own list panel. Test account deleted afterward.

### Follow-on: DMC and Community Reports panels visually separated; Reservoir Storage Levels panel removed

Direct UI polish requests against `/admin/map`'s Requests tab, once the user was looking at it closely enough to want the always-visible panels below the map to read as distinct, separated cards rather than a plain stacked list.

- **"Recent DMC Emergency Warnings"** and **"Verified Community Reports"** each lost their explanatory caption paragraph (the "DMC alerts are a suggestion, not automatic..." and "Road closures and water conditions... Manage and verify new ones at..." lines) and gained a `rounded-xl border bg-*-50/60` card wrapper so each panel reads as a clearly separated block, matching the "soft rounded card" visual language already used elsewhere on this page (the tab-controls card, the map-legend card).
- **Verified Community Reports' card color is dynamic, DMC's is not** — a deliberate distinction per the user's own instruction: green (`border-green-200 bg-green-50/60`) when `communityReports.length === 0`, switching to red (`border-red-200 bg-red-50/60`) the moment any verified report exists, since an active road-closure/flooding report is exactly the kind of thing that should visually stand out rather than blend in. DMC stays a plain, always-green card — not extended to the same conditional logic, since that wasn't asked for.
- **"Reservoir Storage Levels" — initially removed entirely, then re-added scoped to its own tab** (see follow-on below) — the first pass removed the whole panel per the user's request at the time; a later message clarified they wanted it back, just no longer bleeding into the Requests tab. This is the always-visible *list* panel only — the map's own "Reservoirs" tab (colored pin layer + its own legend/caption) is a completely separate code path and was untouched throughout.
- Verified live: registered a throwaway admin, confirmed the DMC and Community Reports panels render as separated green cards with the captions removed, then inserted a real test document directly into `communityReports` and reloaded — the card correctly switched to red the instant a report existed, while the DMC card (still empty) stayed green. Test account and the test community report were deleted afterward.

### Follow-on: Reservoir Storage Levels restored, scoped to its own tab, with a two-level disclosure

The removal above turned out to be a misread of scope — the user wanted the panel gated to the Reservoirs tab specifically, not deleted. Re-added with two further refinements requested in the same pass: a clearer expand/collapse control, and (in a second follow-up message) hiding the Irrigation Department list behind an extra click by default, since 109 reservoirs rendering unconditionally is a lot of scroll for a panel most admins won't need open.

- **Tab-gated**: the whole panel is now wrapped in `{viewMode === "reservoirs" && (...)}`, so it only renders on the Reservoirs tab — re-adding `ReservoirListItem` and the `showAllReservoirs` state that had just been deleted, since both were needed again. Wrapped in a neutral `rounded-xl border border-gray-200 bg-gray-50/60` card, consistent with the DMC/Community Reports card treatment above (no color semantics here, since a mixed normal/elevated/spilling list has no single "is everything OK" signal the way an empty-vs-nonempty report list does).
- **Two-level disclosure, not one**: the top-level "Reservoir Storage Levels" caption was dropped (same "remove the wall of explanatory text" instruction as DMC/Community Reports got). Then, specifically for the Irrigation Department sub-list (109 reservoirs — the Hydropower sub-list is only 3 and was left always-visible, matching what was actually asked): the list itself is now hidden behind a **"View reservoirs" / "Hide reservoirs"** toggle (new `showIrrigationReservoirs` state, `ChevronDown`/`ChevronUp` icons matching the icon vocabulary already established by `InfoDisclosure.tsx`), and only once that's open does the existing **"Show all 109 reservoirs" / "Show less"** toggle (`showAllReservoirs`, unchanged logic) become visible. Both toggles are genuine two-way controls — expanding always has a matching way to collapse back, not a one-directional "view more" with no return path.
