## USGS earthquakes — regional tsunami-risk indicator

A new integration, not from the original feature list. The user did their own research first and brought findings rather than a request to investigate: confirmed the USGS earthquake API works and, with a broad Indian Ocean query, surfaces real events inside the Sumatra subduction zone — including one ~60km from Meulaboh, essentially the epicenter region of the 2004 Indian Ocean tsunami. Explicit instruction that came with it: frame this as a **regional tsunami-risk indicator**, not a "Sri Lanka earthquakes" feature — Sri Lanka itself sits on stable continental crust and barely registers seismic activity, so a Sri-Lanka-scoped version of this feed would look broken/empty almost every day.

- **Confirmed working by direct query**, same verify-before-building discipline as every other external source here: `https://earthquake.usgs.gov/fdsnws/event/1/query` (GeoJSON, no API key) tested directly with a real bounding box — returned 10 magnitude-4.0+ events over the last 30 days on the first real query, including the `tsunami` flag USGS itself attaches to potentially tsunami-generating events (a genuine bonus for this framing, not something added by this project).
- **Deliberately not scoped to Sri Lanka's own coordinates.** `server/src/routes/external.js`'s `USGS_BBOX` covers the wider Indian Ocean / Bay of Bengal / Sumatra subduction zone (`minLat: -10, maxLat: 20, minLng: 70, maxLng: 100`) — the zone that actually produced the 2004 tsunami — rather than a box drawn tightly around the island, which would be realistically empty on almost any given day.
- **`GET /api/external/earthquakes`** — public, cached 20 min (`USGS_CACHE_TTL_MS`), last 30 days, magnitude 4.0+, ordered newest-first. Each event is normalized to `{id, magnitude, place, time, depthKm, tsunami, url, lat, lng}`. Fails soft to `[]`, same pattern as every other feed here.
- **`PublicSeverityMap.tsx`** (public, translated — new `severityMap.earthquakes*` i18n keys in all three locale files) originally got a list panel only, on the reasoning that `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` only ever toggled Areas Affected/River Levels and had no multi-layer map to add a tab to — **since corrected, see "GDACS + earthquake map layers on the public severity map" below**, `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` now has GDACS and earthquake map tabs too, shared with this same list panel's data. Each list entry shows a magnitude badge (color-scaled gray/amber/red), a "Tsunami potential flagged" badge when USGS's own `tsunami` field is set, the place name linking to the USGS event page, and the time + depth.
- **`SituationMap.tsx` (admin) gets a proper 5th map tab, not just a list — corrected after initial pushback.** First version was list-only on this page too, reasoning that all these events are off Sri Lanka's coast anyway. The user pushed back directly ("isnt it better to have USGS earthquake tsunami-risk indicator as another tab like global alerts?") — right call: it's more consistent with the GDACS tab already on this page, reuses the same magnitude-color work, and the Sumatra subduction zone is actually close enough to Sri Lanka on a regional view to be visually meaningful, not just random pins in empty ocean. Added `"earthquakes"` as a 5th `ViewMode` (own toggle button "Tsunami Risk (Earthquakes)", own legend, own empty state, `CircleMarker`s sized/colored by magnitude with a popup matching the request/donation/GDACS popup pattern), keeping the list panel below the map too (same as the GDACS tab, which has both).
- **One real wrinkle: the default map view (zoom 8, centered on Sri Lanka) doesn't show the Sumatra region at all — no earthquake would ever be visible without recentering.** `react-leaflet`'s `MapContainer` only applies `center`/`zoom` on first mount, so switching tabs alone doesn't move the view. Added a small `MapViewController` component (`useMap()` + a `useEffect` keyed on `viewMode`) that calls `map.setView(...)` directly — zooms/pans out to `[8, 87]` zoom 5 (wide enough to show Sri Lanka and the whole Sumatra/Bay of Bengal region together) on the earthquakes tab, and back to the normal Sri-Lanka-only view on every other tab.
- Verified live on the public page (`/severity-map`, both English and Sinhala): real current events rendered correctly — e.g. "M 4.9 — 287 km SW of Banda Aceh, Indonesia" — with accurate magnitude badges, timestamps, and depths, and the tsunami-risk framing text renders as written in both languages (confirmed the Sinhala translation end-to-end, not just that the key exists).
- Verified live on `/admin/map` too, closing the earlier credentials gap: no admin account existed in this session, so registered a throwaway one through the actual `/register` UI (`Test Admin Verify`, role Administrator) rather than leaving it unverified. Clicked into the new "Tsunami Risk (Earthquakes)" tab and confirmed: the map recentered from the default Sri-Lanka zoom-8 view out to the wider region automatically, real gray/amber markers rendered scattered across the Bay of Bengal (correctly color-coded by magnitude), clicking one opened a popup with accurate real data ("M 4.5 — 188 km SE of Yārāda, India · 7/12/2026, 5:05:45 AM · 10 km deep" — matches the API response exactly), and switching back to the "Requests" tab correctly recentered the map to the normal Sri-Lanka-only view with no leftover zoom/pan state. Deleted the test admin account (both the Firestore `users` doc and the Firebase Auth user) via the Admin SDK immediately after, same cleanup convention used for other test data throughout this file.

### Follow-on: a Sri-Lanka-scoped default + "show all regional events" toggle, same as GDACS

The user asked directly for the earthquakes tab on `/admin/map` to work "like [GDACS's] Show all global events" toggle — i.e. default to a Sri-Lanka-only scope (usually empty, an honest reflection of real seismic activity) with an opt-in checkbox to reveal the wider, reliably-populated regional data, rather than always showing the wide region unconditionally.

- **`GET /api/external/earthquakes` gained `?scope=sri-lanka|regional`**, mirroring `/gdacs`'s existing scope param exactly. `sri-lanka` (the new default) queries a tight box around Sri Lanka and its immediate waters (`minLat: 5.5, maxLat: 10, minLng: 79, maxLng: 82.5`) — genuinely usually empty, same as `?scope=sri-lanka` already is for GDACS. `regional` is the original wide Indian Ocean/Sumatra box, unchanged. Cached separately per scope (`usgs-earthquakes-sri-lanka` / `usgs-earthquakes-regional`), same pattern as GDACS's per-scope cache keys.
- **This is a global default change, not additive** — every existing unscoped caller of this endpoint silently started getting the new (usually-empty) sri-lanka scope the moment this shipped. Caught and fixed deliberately rather than left as a silent regression: `SituationMap.tsx` (admin) is the one place that actually wants the new default-plus-toggle behavior (that's what was asked for), so it got the real toggle UI. `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (Landing's embedded map + `/severity-map`) and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s own separate list-panel fetch both have **no** scope toggle and were about to silently go blank by default — both were updated to explicitly pass `?scope=regional`, pinning them to their exact prior (already-verified, already-translated) behavior. Re-verified after the fix: the homepage's earthquake tab still renders all 14 real regional markers, unchanged.
- **`MapViewController` (admin map) now also takes `earthquakeScope`** — it only pans out to the wide `[8, 87]` zoom-5 regional view when `viewMode === "earthquakes" && earthquakeScope === "regional"`; the sri-lanka scope stays at the normal zoom-8 Sri-Lanka view every other tab uses, since that tight bbox roughly matches what's already on screen — no reason to zoom out for a box that's already visible.
- Verified live: default load of the Tsunami Risk tab on `/admin/map` shows the correct empty state ("expected most days; try 'Show all regional events' above") with zero API calls returning stale/wrong data (confirmed `GET /api/external/earthquakes` with no scope param returns `[]`, `?scope=regional` returns the real 14); clicking the toggle checkbox reveals all 14 real events with the map recentering to the Bay of Bengal; confirmed the homepage (`/`) still shows all 14 markers with no toggle present, unaffected by the admin-only change.

### Follow-on: the same Sri-Lanka-default + toggle extended to the public pages too

A direct reversal of the previous section's own "pin the public pages to `?scope=regional`, no toggle" decision — the user later asked for the public-facing earthquake section to work the same way admin's does: "add information about SL only if available if not lets add a view global events button." This supersedes that earlier restraint, not a bug fix.

- **`AreaSeverityMap.tsx`** (Landing's embedded map + `/severity-map`'s map) gained its own `earthquakeScope` state, independent of any other page's toggle — same self-contained-per-surface pattern already used for GDACS elsewhere in this app. Defaults to `sri-lanka`; when that comes back empty, a "View regional events" button appears (styled as a filled pill, matching this pass's other button-enhancement work below) and switches to `regional`; once viewing regional data, a small "Show Sri Lanka only" text link appears to switch back. `MapViewController` got the identical `earthquakeScope`-aware recentering logic already built for the admin map (only pans out to the wide Bay-of-Bengal view when scope is actually `regional`).
- **`PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s own separate list panel** (below the map, translated) got the identical treatment independently — its own `earthquakeScope` state, its own fetch, its own empty-state-with-button. It does **not** share state with `AreaSeverityMap`'s copy on the same page; each surface manages its own scope, consistent with how GDACS's admin/public toggles have always been independent of each other too.
- **Deliberately labeled "regional," not "global"** — the user said "global events," but the wider USGS scope is the Indian Ocean/Bay of Bengal/Sumatra subduction zone, not literally worldwide (that framing is GDACS's `?scope=global`, a genuinely different, much larger dataset). Kept the geographically accurate term already established for this feed rather than introduce an inaccurate "global" claim for regional data.
- New `severityMap.earthquakesNoEventsSriLanka` / `viewGlobalEvents` / `viewSriLankaOnly` i18n keys in all three locale files.
- Verified live: homepage's earthquake tab defaulted to the correct empty Sri-Lanka state with the reveal button present; clicking it rendered all 13 real regional markers with the "Show Sri Lanka only" link now showing; `/severity-map`'s separate list panel independently showed its own matching toggle and heading, confirmed present in Tamil after a language switch.

User-reported gap, found while looking at the homepage/`/severity-map`'s GDACS panel: the earthquake toggle above got built for both public surfaces, but the equivalent GDACS toggle never did — `AreaSeverityMap.tsx`'s GDACS tab and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s GDACS list panel were both still doing an unscoped `GET /api/external/gdacs` call (silently defaulting server-side to `sri-lanka`, usually empty) with no way to reveal global events, unlike their earthquake siblings sitting right next to them.

- **`AreaSeverityMap.tsx`** — added a `gdacsScope` state (`"sri-lanka" | "global"`, matching GDACS's own scope vocabulary, not "regional" — that word is reserved for the earthquake feed's genuinely different, smaller Indian-Ocean-only scope) with the identical empty-state-button / "Show Sri Lanka only" reset-link pattern already proven for earthquakes in this same file. Affects both the homepage's embedded map and `/severity-map`'s full map, since both render through this one shared component.
- **`PublicSeverityMap.tsx`**'s own separate GDACS list panel got the identical independent treatment — its own `gdacsScope` state, its own fetch, not sharing state with `AreaSeverityMap`'s copy on the same page (same independence convention as earthquakes' two copies).

### Reservoirs as a map layer, not just a list — for the 3 with coordinates

The user asked for reservoir/dam data to show on the map with graduated color shading, "like Major flood / Minor flood / Alert level... as low, increasing." Real constraint surfaced and confirmed with the user before building: 109 of the 112 reservoirs (the Irrigation Department ones) have **no coordinates at all** in their source bulletin — only the 3 CEB/Mahaweli ones (Kotmale, Victoria, Randenigala) do, since those came from Wikipedia lookups for district-attribution purposes (see "CEB / Mahaweli Complex hydropower reservoirs" above). Confirmed direction with the user rather than either silently building a partial map or overreaching with district-centroid approximations that would stack multiple reservoirs on one point and mislead: **add real markers for the 3 with coordinates, leave the other 109 list-only**, stated plainly in the UI rather than glossed over.

- **A real gap caught before this could work at all: `fetchCebMahaweliReservoirs()` computed `lat`/`lng` internally (to call `nearestDistrict()`) but never included them in the object it actually returned** — so `GET /api/external/reservoirs` had no coordinates for any reservoir, CEB included, until this was added. Easy to miss since the district-attribution feature (built earlier) never needed the raw coordinates downstream, only the derived district name.
- **Color scale reuses `GAUGE_STATUS_COLOR`'s exact hue progression** (`normal` blue → `elevated` amber → `high` orange → `spilling` red) re-keyed to reservoir risk levels instead of gauge flood status — same "how worried should I be" gradient, applied to a different metric (storage %, not flow), reading consistently on the same map per the user's own "different shades of Major flood/Minor flood/Alert level" framing.
- **New `"reservoirs"` tab** on both `SituationMap.tsx` (admin, between River Gauges and GDACS) and `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (public — Landing's embedded map + `/severity-map`, gated behind `extraLayers` like GDACS/Earthquakes). Deliberately its own tab, not merged into River Gauges — same "one hazard type per tab" convention already established for GDACS not bleeding into the Requests tab.
- Markers filter to `r.lat != null && r.lng != null` before rendering — the 3 CEB reservoirs pass, the 109 Irrigation Dept ones are silently excluded from the *map layer* (they're completely unaffected on the existing list panel below, still showing all 112 with their own color-coded badges). The tab's caption states this constraint explicitly rather than leaving an admin wondering where the other 109 pins are.
- Verified live: registered a throwaway admin, clicked the new "Reservoirs" tab on `/admin/map` — 3 real markers rendered, correct caption text present; clicked the Kotmale marker's popup and confirmed real live data ("Kotmale (hydropower), 63.3% capacity · 693.63 m MSL, Normal" — the % had genuinely drifted since an earlier check the same day, real live data, not a bug); confirmed the same 3 markers render on the homepage's embedded map. Test account deleted afterward.

### Follow-on: the other 109 reservoirs added too, at their district centroid, in light pink

Direct reversal of the "leave the other 109 list-only" restraint immediately above — the user asked for them on the map too, in a visually distinct color rather than the CEB markers' risk gradient.

- **`fetchReservoirSheet()` (Irrigation Dept parser) now looks up each reservoir's own `district` value against the existing `DISTRICTS` centroid list** (the same 25-district list `nearestDistrict()` already uses everywhere else in this app) and attaches that centroid's `lat`/`lng` — the sheet itself has no coordinates at all, only a district name, so this is the best available fallback, not a guess. A new `locationApproximate: true` flag distinguishes these from the 3 CEB reservoirs (`locationApproximate: false`, real coordinates) so the frontend can render and explain them differently. 108 of 109 got a resolved centroid; the one exception has a blank district cell in the source itself (a pre-existing, already-documented gap), correctly left uncoordinated rather than guessed.
- **Deliberately a single flat light-pink color (`#f9a8d4`), not risk-graduated** — unlike the CEB markers' blue/amber/orange/red gradient, these pins' position is only ever a district-level approximation, so color-coding them by risk the same way would visually imply a precision the location itself doesn't have. Pink signals "approximate — check the popup for the real number," a deliberately different visual language from "exact."
- **Popup content matches the existing list item's wording exactly** (risk badge, name, size, "→ district", capacity %, "As of {date}"), plus one extra line making the approximation explicit ("Approximate location — district centroid, not the real pin.") so a click never implies more precision than the pin actually has.
- Reservoirs sharing a district (e.g. every Badulla reservoir) stack on the exact same point — an honest reflection of the source data's real limits, not a rendering bug, and stated as such in code comments and the tab's caption.
- Verified live: `GET /api/external/reservoirs` confirmed 108 of 109 Irrigation Dept reservoirs now carry `lat`/`lng`; both `/admin/map` and the homepage's embedded map render 111 total markers (108 pink + 3 risk-colored) on the Reservoirs tab; clicked a pink marker (Ambewela) and confirmed the popup showed real data ("Ambewela — Badulla, 100% capacity, Spilling, Approximate location...") matching the list panel exactly.

### Fixed: river gauge popups missing their alert/flood-threshold line on the public map

User-reported: clicking a gauge marker on the homepage/`/severity-map` map showed station/level/status but not the "Alert X · Minor flood Y · Major flood Z" line admin's map already had — the `GaugeStation` data already carried `alertLevel`/`minorFloodLevel`/`majorFloodLevel`, `AreaSeverityMap.tsx`'s popup JSX just never rendered them. Added the same line (using the existing translated `severityMap.alertLevel`/`minorFlood`/`majorFlood` labels rather than admin's plain-English hardcoded ones, since this component is translated and admin's isn't). Verified live: clicking a real gauge marker on the homepage now shows "Alert level 3.5 · Minor flood 4 · Major flood 5" matching real API data.

### Fixed: homepage map popups weren't showing at all

While investigating the gauge-popup report above, found the real root cause of a related complaint: `Landing.tsx`'s `<AreaSeverityMap>` was passed `showPopups={false}` — a deliberate choice from the original visual-design pass ("cleaner glanceable view" for the homepage teaser) that predates GDACS/earthquakes/reservoirs existing as map layers at all. This meant **no marker on the homepage map ever showed a popup on click, for any layer** — not a gauge-specific bug. Removed the `showPopups={false}` override (the prop now defaults to `true`, matching `/severity-map`'s behavior) — another instance of this session's recurring pattern where an earlier "keep the homepage restrained" decision gets explicitly reversed once the user asks for parity with the full experience. Verified live: clicking markers on the homepage map now opens popups across every layer (gauges, reservoirs, confirmed directly).

## Country search + zoom-to-outline (GDACS + Earthquakes tabs)

Feedback from the user's dissertation supervisor, relayed directly: "the map is not user friendly yet, for example a person might not know where nepal is" — GDACS events and regional earthquakes name countries (Nepal, Indonesia, Myanmar, ...) but nothing on the map helped a viewer actually find one. Confirmed scope with the user before building, since this touches a genuinely new kind of data this app had never needed before (world country boundaries, as opposed to Sri Lanka's own 25 districts): applies to **both** the GDACS and Earthquakes tabs, on **both** admin's `/admin/map` and the public map (homepage + `/severity-map`).

- **Deliberately not a live external fetch, unlike every other feed in `external.js`.** Country borders don't change, so there's no reason to add a network dependency (and its attendant outage risk) for this. Instead, `server/src/routes/external.js` reads `world-atlas`'s bundled `countries-110m.json` (Natural Earth data at 110m resolution, public domain, ~105KB, new dependency alongside `topojson-client` to convert its TopoJSON to GeoJSON), computed to a trimmed `{name, geometry}` FeatureCollection once and memoized in memory for the life of the server process — no cache TTL logic needed at all, since there's nothing to invalidate. **`GET /api/external/world-countries`** — 176 countries (177 minus Antarctica, filtered out as irrelevant to a disaster search). Confirmed Nepal is present before writing any frontend code, given it was the supervisor's own example.
- **`web/src/components/CountrySearchBox.tsx`** (new, shared) — a pure search-and-select UI with no Leaflet dependency: a text input filters the 176 countries by substring match as you type, showing up to 8 results in a dropdown; selecting one calls the caller's `onSelect`, an "✕" clears it. Kept map-independent deliberately — the actual zoom/outline logic needs react-leaflet's `useMap()` hook, which only works inside that page's own `<MapContainer>` tree, so each map file wires the callback into its own logic rather than this component reaching into Leaflet itself.
- **Zoom-to-country reuses the `MapViewController` pattern already in both map files** (the same component that already handles `earthquakeScope`-driven recentering) — extended to take `selectedCountry` and, when set, compute its bounds via Leaflet's own `L.geoJSON(feature).getBounds()` (no need for a turf bbox helper) and call `map.fitBounds(bounds, {padding: [24,24]})`, taking priority over the tab's own default scope-based view. Clearing the selection falls back to whatever the tab would normally show.
- **Outline styling**: a single violet dashed `<GeoJSON>` layer (`#7c3aed`, low fill opacity) — deliberately a color not already used by any risk/alert-level palette on this map (green/amber/orange/red are all taken by GDACS alert levels and earthquake magnitude), so a searched country's highlight is never confused for a hazard-severity indicator.
- **Both tabs share one search box and one `countries` fetch** (lazy-loaded the same way gauges/GDACS/earthquakes already are, on first opening either tab) rather than two separate copies — the underlying world-boundaries data is identical regardless of which tab it's used from.
- Verified live on all three surfaces: searched "Nepal" on the homepage's GDACS tab — exactly one match, selecting it rendered the violet outline and zoomed the map in tight enough that Nepal's outline filled most of the visible map area (confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` comparison, not just "a marker exists somewhere"); the "✕" correctly cleared it. Searched "Indonesia" on `/admin/map`'s Earthquakes tab (a throwaway admin account) — same result, outline + zoom confirmed. Confirmed the search box also renders correctly on `/severity-map`, since it shares the exact same `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` component as the homepage. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across the whole web project after wiring both files.

### Follow-on: card grouping for the map controls, and the search box moved to the right

User feedback after the feature above shipped, pasted as a raw text dump of the homepage's "Affected Areas" section: "feels like all of this together, has no proper division/separation," followed immediately by "also move the search tab to the right." Two related but distinct problems, both purely visual/layout, no logic changes.

- **The division/separation problem**: on both `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (homepage + `/severity-map`) and `SituationMap.tsx` (`/admin/map`), the tab-toggle buttons, the country search box, the legend dots, the caption text, and the empty-state message were all sitting as bare, unbordered elements directly in the page flow — nothing visually grouped them as "one control panel for the map below." Fixed by wrapping the tab-buttons row through the empty-state into a single `rounded-xl border border-gray-200 bg-white p-4` card, with a `border-t border-gray-100` divider between the tab-row and the legend/caption section beneath it — so it reads as one panel with two sections, not a loose pile of controls. `SituationMap.tsx` has a structural quirk `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` doesn't (the tab-row renders outside the page's `{loading ? ... : (...)}` conditional, legend/captions render inside it), so instead of restructuring that conditional, the two adjacent divs are styled as a visually-seamless two-part card via `rounded-t-xl border border-b-0` (top) + `rounded-b-xl border border-t-0` (bottom) — same trick, no logic moved.
- **`Landing.tsx`'s weather strip got the same treatment independently**: it was a bare `flex flex-wrap` row of chips sitting directly above the map card with zero separation from it. Wrapped in its own `rounded-xl border border-gray-200 bg-gray-50 p-4` card with a small uppercase "Current Conditions" heading (new `landing.currentConditions` i18n key, all three locales) — now reads as two distinct cards (current weather, then the map) rather than one undifferentiated block.
- **The "move search to the right" problem had a real root cause, not just a missing class**: `CountrySearchBox.tsx` sat inside a `flex flex-wrap items-center justify-between` row alongside the tab buttons, and `justify-content: space-between` only distributes space between items sharing the same flex line — with 5 tab buttons (including the long "Global Disaster Alerts (GDACS)" and "Tsunami Risk (Earthquakes)" labels) plus a 256px search box exceeding the row's available width inside the page's `max-w-4xl` container, the search box wraps onto its own line, where a single lone item has nothing to space against and sits at the line's start (left) by default. Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` at both narrow and desktop widths before concluding this, not just from the screenshot. Fixed with one line: added `ml-auto` directly to `CountrySearchBox.tsx`'s own root div className (alongside its existing `relative w-full sm:w-64 sm:shrink-0`) — this pushes the box to the right edge of whichever flex line it ends up on, whether that's shared with the tabs or its own wrapped line, so the fix holds regardless of viewport width or how many tabs are showing.
- Verified live on both surfaces: homepage (`/`) — `getBoundingClientRect()` showed the search box's wrapper sitting 16.8px from the card's right edge (exactly the card's own `p-4` padding, i.e. flush-right), confirmed visually via screenshot showing the weather card and map-controls card as clearly separated blocks. `/admin/map` (throwaway admin account) — identical 16.8px-from-edge result on the GDACS tab, confirmed visually via screenshot showing the tab row + search box sharing one line with the search flush right, and the legend/caption panel below it. Test admin account deleted afterward.

## Public map accessibility pass (plain language, icons, onboarding, text-size/contrast)

The user's supervisor called the map "not user-friendly enough... optimized" for a site meant to be used by people of all knowledge levels nationwide. Asked for suggestions first (not a decided plan), gave 7 concrete options, and the user picked 4 to build: plain-language captions, icon+color+text (never color alone), a one-time guided walkthrough, and a text-size/high-contrast toggle. Scoped to the **public-facing surfaces only** (`AreaSeverityMap.tsx` — the homepage embed and `/severity-map`'s full map — and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s own list panels) — consistent with this project's existing "admin stays technical/English, public gets simplified" line already drawn for i18n; `SituationMap.tsx` (admin) was not touched.

- **`web/src/components/InfoDisclosure.tsx`** (new, shared) — always shows a short plain-language `summary` first; the existing jargon-heavy `details` text (acronyms, model internals, source citations — appropriate for a dissertation examiner, wrong as the first thing a general visitor sees) collapses behind a "How is this calculated?" toggle. Wired into every caption that was previously a wall of technical text: GDACS (`gdacsCaption` → new `gdacsSimpleCaption`: "Alerts about major disasters happening around the world..."), Earthquakes (`earthquakesSimpleCaption`), Flood Risk Forecast (`floodRiskSimpleCaption` — the worst offender, the old caption led with "A trained model (logistic regression)..."), and Reservoirs (`reservoirsSimpleCaption`, reused for both `AreaSeverityMap.tsx`'s pin-location caption and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s two-operators caption, since both boil down to the same plain sentence). New keys added to all three locale files — Sinhala/Tamil AI-translated, same not-yet-native-reviewed caveat as the rest of this app's translations.
- **Color is never the only signal, on the map or in the list panels.** `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` gained a shared `SeverityTier` vocabulary (`safe`/`caution`/`danger`/`unknown`) mapped from every layer's own status values (`LEVEL_TIER`, `GAUGE_STATUS_TIER`, `RESERVOIR_RISK_TIER`, `FLOOD_RISK_TIER`, `GDACS_TIER`, plus a `magnitudeTier()` function for earthquakes), each tier rendered as one of 4 `lucide-react` icons (`CheckCircle2`/`AlertTriangle`/`AlertOctagon`/`HelpCircle`) via two small helper components: `LegendItem` (colored dot + icon + label, used in every legend row) and `TierLabel` (icon + text, used inside every JSX `<Popup>`). The two GeoJSON boundary layers (`areas`, `floodRisk`) bind raw HTML popup strings rather than JSX, so they get the same 4 tiers as plain Unicode symbols instead (`TIER_SYMBOL`: ✓/⚠/❗/?) prefixed onto the risk-level line.
  - **A real accessibility gap this surfaced, not just a nice-to-have**: the GDACS popup had no text statement of the alert level at all — Green/Orange/Red was color-only information before this pass. Fixed by adding a `TierLabel`-wrapped alert-level line to the popup. Earthquake popups also gained a `TierLabel` wrapping the magnitude line, and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s own separate reservoir-risk/GDACS-alert/magnitude badges (colored pills with text, in its list panels) each gained a matching icon prefix via local `RESERVOIR_RISK_ICON`/`GDACS_ALERT_ICON`/`MagnitudeIcon` lookups, so the exact same icon vocabulary reads consistently whether you're looking at the map or the list below it.
- **`web/src/hooks/useMapWalkthrough.ts` + `web/src/components/MapWalkthroughModal.tsx`** (new) — a 3-step onboarding modal, auto-opened once per browser (localStorage `map_walkthrough_dismissed`, not sessionStorage — meant to be seen once ever, not once per tab) covering: (1) use the tabs to switch layers, (2) colors+icons show risk (with the same ✓/⚠/❗ vocabulary spelled out in the copy), (3) tap the map for details. **Deliberately a centered modal carousel, not a spotlight tooltip pointing at specific map controls** — spotlight positioning is fragile across viewport sizes and tab states (this app's tab row alone reflows between 2 and 6 buttons depending on `extraLayers`), while a modal works identically everywhere with no per-element coordinate math. A "Help / Tour" button in the new accessibility control (see below) reopens it any time. Mounted on `Landing.tsx` and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`.
- **`web/src/lib/accessibility.ts` + `web/src/components/AccessibilityControls.tsx`** (new) — a small "Aa" control (text-size: normal/large/xlarge, and a high-contrast checkbox), deliberately **not** React context/state — `setFontScale()` sets the **root `<html>` element's** `font-size` directly (not a wrapping div), because Tailwind's `text-*` utilities are rem-based and rem is always relative to the document root, not the nearest ancestor — a scoped wrapper's own `fontSize` style would never have cascaded to them. This means the control only ever needs to be *mounted* on the two public pages (matching the specific supervisor feedback), but its *effect* is genuinely app-wide and outlives navigating away, since `applyStoredAccessibilityPrefs()` runs once at app boot (`main.tsx`) to restore whatever was saved last time — an accessibility preference shouldn't silently reset just because the user moved to `/login` or a dashboard page.
  - High contrast is a **targeted CSS override, not a certified WCAG-AAA mode**: `index.css`'s `html.high-contrast` rules force this app's specific low-contrast utility classes (`text-gray-400/500/600`, `border-gray-100/200/300`) to pure black and underline every link — chosen because those are the exact classes used for secondary text/borders throughout this codebase, not a generic contrast filter (a `filter: contrast()` approach was considered and rejected — it visibly muddies flat UI colors rather than sharpening them).
- Verified live end-to-end in the browser: fresh page load correctly auto-opened the walkthrough at "STEP 1 OF 3"; clicked through all 3 steps (dispatched-event clicks, since a couple of attempts hit this session's known ref-staleness/timing quirks with rapid repeated clicks — resolved by using real coordinate clicks and re-confirming via direct DOM/localStorage reads rather than trusting a single screenshot) and confirmed `localStorage.map_walkthrough_dismissed === "1"` after "Got it," and confirmed a reload did **not** reopen it. Reopened it via the accessibility popover's "Help / Tour" button. Toggled text size to "A++" and confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that the root font-size changed from 16px to 20px (the 125% `xlarge` step) — visibly reflowed the hero heading from 2 lines to 3 and "Sign in" to wrap, proving it's a real app-wide scale, not a cosmetic no-op. Toggled high contrast and confirmed `document.documentElement.classList.contains("high-contrast")` plus a visible before/after: every link gained an underline, and every previously light-gray caption/legend/border turned black. Screenshotted the Earthquakes tab with high contrast active and confirmed, in one frame: the plain-language caption, the collapsed-by-default "How is this calculated?" disclosure (expanded to show it works), and all 3 magnitude-band legend rows each showing colored dot + correct icon (danger/caution/unknown) + label. Cross-checked the "Areas Affected" default tab separately and confirmed all 4 tiers render correctly (red ❗ High need, orange ⚠ Moderate need, green ✓ Low need, gray ? No activity), and confirmed the reservoir/GDACS/earthquake list panels on `/severity-map` show the same plain-caption-plus-disclosure and icon-badge treatment independently of the map component. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Reset the test browser's accessibility localStorage keys back to defaults afterward.

### Follow-on: app-wide screen-reader/dialog-semantics pass on web — started, then redirected to mobile-only

The user asked to build on screen-reader accessibility generally (not scoped to the public map this time) and confirmed starting with dialog/screen-reader labels. This was built on **web first** — deliberately, since the browser's own accessibility tree (`read_page` / direct DOM inspection) makes every fix here directly, mechanically verifiable in a way mobile's native accessibility services can't be from this session — then the user redirected mid-build: **"i need this disability update to my mobile version only."** These web changes were **kept, not reverted** (they're real, verified bug fixes with no downside), but all further accessibility work moved to mobile-only from that point on — see "Follow-on: screen-reader accessibility pass — mobile only" under "Mobile app (Expo / React Native)" below for what came after the redirect.

- **`web/src/hooks/useDialogA11y.ts`** (new, shared) — this app had **5 separate "fixed inset-0" modal-style overlays** (`SosButton.tsx`, `QrScanModal.tsx`, `CaseNotesModal.tsx`, `ChatModal.tsx` (3 return branches), `MapWalkthroughModal.tsx`) and **none of them had dialog semantics, focus management, or Escape-to-close** — confirmed by grep before assuming (only 9 `aria-label` occurrences existed across the entire web app, and zero `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal` anywhere). A single hook was justified here rather than 5 near-identical inline implementations: given `{open, onClose}`, it returns a ref for the dialog panel that (1) moves focus into the panel the instant it opens (`tabIndex={-1}` makes an otherwise-inert container programmatically focusable), (2) traps Tab/Shift+Tab within the dialog's own focusable elements so keyboard focus can't escape to the page behind the overlay, (3) closes on Escape, and (4) restores focus to whatever was focused before the dialog opened, on close.
- Applied to all 5 modals with `role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-labelledby={a heading id}`. `MapWalkthroughModal.tsx`'s step content also gained `aria-live="polite"` so its heading/body text is re-announced on every "Next" click, since the dialog's own `aria-labelledby` target changing text mid-session doesn't automatically get re-announced unless focus moves or a live region says so.
- **Real, additional gaps found and fixed while in these components**: `aria-pressed` on the SOS modal's type-selector buttons and `AccessibilityControls.tsx`'s text-size buttons (selection state was color-only before); `aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup` on the "Aa" accessibility popover's own trigger button; `aria-pressed` on `LanguageSwitcher.tsx`'s three language buttons (same color-only-selection gap); `aria-label` on several `placeholder`-only inputs (SOS people-count/description, the case-notes textarea, the chat message input, `CountrySearchBox.tsx`/`DistrictSearchBox.tsx`'s search inputs and their "✕" clear buttons, which had a `title` attribute but nothing screen-reader-reliable).
- **`aria-live` added to every genuinely dynamic, must-be-heard-without-looking element in the app**: `NotificationSetup.tsx`'s foreground-message toast (`role="status" aria-live="polite"`), `EmergencyBanner.tsx`'s two independent bars (the active-districts bar `role="status"`/polite, the manual broadcast `role="alert"`/assertive — a genuine emergency broadcast should interrupt, an informational district list shouldn't), and — the single highest-value fix in this whole web pass — `SosStatusBanner.tsx`, whose own file comment already calls it "the one place in the app where 'live' matters more than anywhere else," yet had zero accessibility markup before this (`role="status" aria-live="assertive"` added).
- Verified live in the browser, not just by reading the code: opened `MapWalkthroughModal.tsx` on the real running app and confirmed via direct DOM query that `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, and `aria-labelledby` all resolved correctly (`aria-labelledby` → the actual heading text, "Welcome to the risk map") and that **focus had genuinely moved into the dialog** (`document.activeElement === dialog`, confirmed true) — not just that the attributes were present in markup. Pressed real Tab keys 3 times in sequence and confirmed focus moved Close → Skip → Next in order, proving the panel's own focusable elements are reachable in the expected sequence (the 4th-Tab wrap-back-to-Close check was interrupted by the mobile-only redirect before it completed, but the forward sequence through all 3 focusable elements was directly confirmed). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across every edited file.
- **Not reverted, not extended further on web after the redirect** — these are the only web accessibility changes from this pass; anything more (a full app-wide sweep of every icon-only button, every form across every dashboard page, etc.) was not attempted here and would be a reasonable follow-on if the user ever wants web accessibility work resumed.

## District search + zoom on the admin Situation Map

A follow-on request naming the admin map's own tabs directly: "Requests / Areas Affected / River Gauges / Reservoirs / Flood Risk Forecast... let these have search button including all districts and when searched zoom into the specific district." Scoped to exactly those 5 Sri-Lanka-focused tabs on `SituationMap.tsx` (admin) — GDACS and Earthquakes already have `CountrySearchBox` for a different data domain (world countries), and this request named the other 5 tabs specifically, not those two.

- **`web/src/components/DistrictSearchBox.tsx`** (new) — same search-input-plus-dropdown UX as `CountrySearchBox.tsx`, but deliberately a separate, simpler component rather than generalizing that one: `CountrySearchBox` is typed around a GeoJSON `Feature` (for `fitBounds`-to-polygon), while a district only ever needs a name + centroid (`{name, lat, lng}`, straight from the already-existing static `DISTRICTS` list in `web/src/lib/districts.ts`) — no API fetch needed at all, unlike the world-countries search which needed a server endpoint.
- **Zoom is a fixed-zoom pan to the centroid (`map.setView([lat, lng], 10)`), not a `fitBounds` to a real polygon** — unlike the public map's "Areas Affected"/"Flood Risk Forecast" tabs (`AreaSeverityMap.tsx`), `SituationMap.tsx` was never built with real district boundary polygons in the first place (it plots `CircleMarker`s at centroids, sized/colored by data, for every layer) — so there was no boundary data already in this file to reuse, and fetching one just for this feature would be inconsistent with the page's own established centroid-only approach. Zoom level 10 was picked empirically (clearly shows a district's own towns without needing to also see the whole country).
- **`MapViewController`** (already existing, handles the `selectedCountry`-driven zoom for GDACS/Earthquakes) gained a `selectedDistrict` prop with the same priority pattern: a searched district takes over the view for whichever of the 5 local tabs is active, same as a searched country already did for GDACS/Earthquakes.
- **The search box and the selected district persist across tab switches** within the 5 local tabs (e.g. search "Ratnapura" on Requests, switch to Reservoirs — still zoomed to Ratnapura) — a deliberate, low-effort consequence of keeping `selectedDistrict` in the parent page's own state rather than resetting it per-tab, and matches what an admin would actually want (comparing the same district across different data layers).
- Verified live (throwaway admin account): searched "Ratna" on the default Requests tab, selected "Ratnapura" from the one matching result, confirmed the map recentered to show real Ratnapura-district towns (Ruwanwella, Karawanella, Ginigathhena, Deraniyagala) rather than the default island-wide view; switched to the Reservoirs tab and confirmed the same Ratnapura-zoomed view persisted with no re-search needed; confirmed the "✕" clear button is present. Test account deleted afterward.

### Follow-on: the same district search added to the public map's Flood Risk Forecast tab

Direct user ask, scoped narrowly to just this one tab ("let the flood risk forecast also have the search box to search by district") — the district search above was built only for admin's `SituationMap.tsx`; the public map (`AreaSeverityMap.tsx`, shared by the homepage embed and `/severity-map`) never got one on any tab, confirmed by checking the code rather than assumed.

- **Reuses the existing `DistrictSearchBox.tsx` component as-is** — no new component needed. `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` gained a `selectedDistrict` state and renders the search box only when `viewMode === "floodRisk"` (deliberately not generalized to the page's other Sri-Lanka tabs — Areas Affected, River Levels, Reservoirs — since that wasn't what was asked; a reasonable follow-on later if wanted, not assumed here).
- **A real, free improvement over admin's version**: admin's `SituationMap.tsx` never had real district boundary polygons to work with (it's `CircleMarker`s only), so its district search does a fixed-zoom pan to the centroid. This public component *already* fetches and renders real HDX boundary polygons for the Flood Risk Forecast tab (see "Real district boundary polygons" above) — so `MapViewController` looks up the selected district's actual `boundaries.features` entry and does a real `fitBounds` to its polygon (same technique the country search already uses for GDACS/Earthquakes), a sharper zoom than a fixed centroid pan, with zero new data fetches. Falls back to a fixed-zoom centroid pan if boundaries haven't loaded yet or no matching feature is found, so the search never silently does nothing.
- Verified live in the browser (`/severity-map`): searched "Ratna," selected Ratnapura, confirmed "Showing: Ratnapura" appears and the map visibly zoomed into the Ratnapura region (real town/province labels — Avissawella, Nuwara Eliya, Uva Province, Monaragala — replacing the default island-wide view); clicked the "✕" clear button and confirmed the map correctly reverted to the default zoomed-out view. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No console errors introduced by this change.

## Project quality tooling (dependency audit, ESLint, automated tests, CI, Firestore backup, structured logging)

The user's supervisor asking about CI/CD (already built, see below) prompted the direct question "what other such things should I include to improve the quality of the project." Investigated rather than answered from generic advice — ran real `npm audit`, checked for existing lint/test tooling (there was none, confirmed by grepping both `package.json` files, not assumed), and checked `.gitignore` coverage of real secrets (confirmed already correct: `.env` files, the Firebase service account key — which lives outside the repo entirely — and `mobile/google-services.json` are all genuinely excluded, no gap there). The user asked to act on all five findings.

- **Dependency vulnerabilities**: `npm audit fix` (no `--force`) applied everywhere a non-breaking fix existed — confirmed by `git diff --stat` on both lockfiles, and both `tsc --noEmit`/`vite build` (web) and an app-load smoke test (server) still passed clean afterward. **Deliberately did NOT run `--force`**: a dry run showed it would bump `firebase-admin` 12→14 (server, 2 major versions) and, on the web side, cascade into **Vite 5→8 (3 major versions) and react-router-dom 6→7** — a real risk of breaking the entire build/routing setup days before a deadline, to fix a moderate-severity issue in transitive HTTP-client internals (`undici`, pulled in by the Firebase SDK) that's low real-world risk for a localhost-only, never-deployed app. Remaining count: 11 moderate (server, all `@google-cloud/*` transitive deps of `firebase-admin`) and 13 (web, 1 high — `undici` — plus `dompurify`/`react-router` moderates). Documented here as a deliberate risk/effort tradeoff, not an oversight.
- **ESLint**, added fresh to both packages (neither had any linter configured at all, confirmed by checking for `eslint`/`.eslintrc*`/`eslint.config.*` before assuming):
  - `web/eslint.config.js` — flat config, `typescript-eslint` recommended + React Hooks + React Refresh. **Deliberately does NOT spread `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`' full "recommended" config** — v7+ ships a React-Compiler-oriented ruleset (`set-state-in-effect`, `purity`) calibrated for React 19 + the compiler, not this project's plain React 18. Turning it on flagged 20 "errors" that were really just this codebase's own correct, intentional patterns: the standard "`setLoading(true)` then fetch in a `useEffect`" pattern used in nearly every page, and — the two genuinely interesting false positives — `AdminRequests.tsx`'s `isStale()`/`staleAgeLabel()` deliberately calling `Date.now()` during render (refreshed via an intentional `setInterval` re-render tick, see "Stale request flags" above), which the new `purity` rule can't distinguish from an actual bug. Kept only the two long-standing, universally-useful rules (`rules-of-hooks` as an error, `exhaustive-deps` as a warning) instead of either mass-"fixing" correct code or drowning real signal in disable-comments. Result: 0 errors, 30 warnings (`@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` in a handful of `catch (err: any)` blocks and Leaflet/GeoJSON typing edge cases — left as warnings, not worth the churn to eliminate).
  - `server/eslint.config.js` — plain `@eslint/js` recommended + Node globals. Found exactly 4 real issues, all reviewed individually rather than blindly auto-fixed: 2 were genuinely dead code (`load-test.js`'s `status`/`body` initializers were always overwritten before being read on every code path, including the catch branch — removed the dead initial values), and 2 were **intentional patterns the linter can't tell apart from bugs without a rename**: `app.js`'s central error handler's unused `next` param (Express requires the exact 4-arg `(err, req, res, next)` signature to recognize error-handling middleware, even when `next` is never called) and `deliveries.js`'s `const { confirmToken, ...rest } = doc.data()` (deliberately destructuring `confirmToken` OUT to exclude it from the victim's own delivery list response — see "QR-code delivery confirmation" above). Both renamed with a leading underscore (`_next`, `_confirmToken`) — the project-wide "intentionally unused" convention set in both configs — rather than disabling the rule. Result: 0 errors, 0 warnings.
- **Automated tests (Vitest, both packages)** — this project had zero automated tests of any kind before this pass; CI could only ever catch a type/syntax error, never a logic regression. Scoped deliberately to the highest-value **pure, already-existing logic** rather than attempting UI/integration coverage in one pass:
  - Server (`server/src/utils/__tests__/`, 39 tests): `geo.js` (Haversine distance — symmetry, known city-to-city sanity range, missing-input handling), `phone.js` (every input format `normalizeSriLankanPhone` claims to accept/reject, including the landline-rejection rule), `requestItemStatus.js` (all 3 `computeOverallStatus` branches), `priorityScore.js` (each of the 4 weighted criteria independently, including the two documented caps at 30/25 points and the 20-point wait-score cap), `districts.js` (all 25 real districts present with plausible Sri Lankan coordinates, `nearestDistrict()` centroid-matching), and **`floodPrediction.js`'s pure feature-engineering helpers** (`riskLevelFor`'s 4 threshold boundaries, `sigmoid`, and — the most valuable ones — `currentMonthProjectedTotal`/`recentAverageDailyRate`/`prior30dTotal`'s date-window math, including a regression test for the real early-month NASA-POWER-lag bug this project already found and fixed once, see "Flood risk forecast (ML)" above). These 5 helper functions were **exported for the first time specifically for testability** (`module.exports` widened, zero behavior change) — they were already pure and well-isolated, just previously module-private.
  - Web (`web/src/lib/__tests__/`, 6 tests): `districts.ts` and `phone.ts` — the client-side mirrors of the server logic above, testing that both copies genuinely agree (a real risk category for this project, since these are hand-duplicated, not shared, per their own file-header comments).
  - **Deliberately not covered in this pass**: anything requiring a live Firestore/Firebase connection (every route handler, `categories.js`'s `getCategoryLimits()`) or a DOM (`accessibility.ts`, any component). Testing those would need either the Firebase Local Emulator Suite or `jsdom` — real, reasonable follow-ups, not attempted here to keep this pass scoped to what could be verified quickly and safely.
  - Both `vitest.config.js` (server) and `vite.config.ts`'s new `test` block (web) use `globals: true` specifically so plain CommonJS test files (server) don't need an ESM `import` just for `describe`/`it`/`expect` (Vitest itself can only be imported via `import`, not `require` — hit this directly, fixed by enabling globals instead of rewriting every test file's module style).
  - `npm run lint`/`npm test` added to both `package.json`s, and both ESLint configs gained a `globals.vitest` override scoped to `**/__tests__/**` so the linter doesn't flag `describe`/`it`/`expect` as undefined globals.
- **CI wiring** — `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s server and web jobs (originally type-check/build/syntax-check only, see below) each gained a `Lint` and `Unit tests` step, renamed accordingly ("Server (lint + test)", "Web (lint + test + type-check + build)"). The pipeline now actually verifies something beyond "does it compile."
- **`server/scripts/backup-firestore.js`** (new) — dumps every one of this project's 17 top-level Firestore collections to timestamped local JSON files (`server/backups/<timestamp>/<collection>.json`, gitignored — this data includes real NIC numbers, phone numbers, and home addresses). Deliberately a plain Admin-SDK read-and-write-to-JSON, not `gcloud firestore export` (which needs a GCS bucket and the gcloud CLI, neither otherwise needed by this project) — protects the real demo dataset (used throughout this project's entire development history, including a lot of live create/delete testing) against being lost right before the viva. `npm run backup-firestore`. Verified live: backed up 539 real documents across all 17 collections on the first run.
- **Structured logging (`pino`)** — `server/src/utils/logger.js` (pretty-printed in dev, plain JSON if `NODE_ENV=production`), wired at the **framework level**: `pino-http` middleware in `app.js` for automatic structured request logging (method/path/status/response-time — a genuinely new capability, this project had no request logging at all before, not just unstructured `console.log` calls), the central error handler, and `server.js`'s startup message. **Deliberately not a full mechanical sweep of the ~90 existing `console.*` calls scattered across ~30 route/utility files** — converting those is safe and low-risk (identical call shape, `logger.error(...)` instead of `console.error(...)`) but doing it correctly means actually reading each call site, which this pass didn't have room for; flagged here explicitly as a real follow-up rather than silently leaving the impression everything was converted. Verified live: restarted the dev server and confirmed real colorized structured log lines for both a 200 and a 404 request, including method/path/status/response time.

### Follow-on: ML-specific testing + saved test results (not just pass/fail)

The user's supervisor requires the actual **test results** as evidence, not just "tests were written" — directly shaped how this was built: a dedicated evaluation script that computes and saves real numbers (a confusion matrix, precision/recall/F1, domain-knowledge sanity checks), on top of fast repeatable unit tests, rather than only reporting a Vitest pass count.

- **`server/scripts/evaluate-flood-model.js`** (new) — re-runs the training pipeline once (same ~30-60s, 25 NASA POWER calls as a normal retrain; training is deterministic — fixed epochs/lr/l2, no randomness — so this evaluates the same model currently being served, not a different one) to get fresh in-sample predictions, then computes and writes two files: **`server/ml-evaluation-report.md`** (human-readable, dissertation-ready — tables for data integrity, model summary, confusion matrix, sanity checks, and a qualitative "riskiest/safest district-months" list) and **`server/ml-evaluation-report.json`** (the same numbers, raw). `npm run evaluate-flood-model`. Neither file contains any PII (unlike `backup-firestore.js`'s output), so both are safe to commit and hand to a supervisor directly.
- **`trainFloodRiskModel()` was extended (backward-compatible) to also return per-sample `evaluationRecords`** (`{district, year, month, label, probability}` for all 12,000 training samples) — attached to the in-memory return value only, never written into the persisted `flood-risk-model.json` (which stays lean for request-time loading). This is what makes a real confusion matrix possible without a second, separate re-derivation of the feature set. Confirmed both existing callers (the CLI script, which ignores the return value, and `external.js`'s retrain route, which only reads specific named fields) are unaffected by the wider return shape.
- **Real results from the first run** (also saved in `ml-evaluation-report.md`, not just quoted here): confusion matrix at the 0.5 threshold — TP=146, FP=138, TN=10687, FN=1029 → **precision 51.4%, recall 12.4%, F1 20.0%, accuracy 90.3%** (vs. a 90.2% "always predict no flood" baseline — expected and already-documented given the ~90/10 class imbalance; the model's real demonstrated skill is in *ranking* risk, not a binary call at 0.5, which is exactly what the existing top-decile-precision metric was always about). All 3 domain-sanity checks passed, including a new one beyond the original Ratnapura/Mannar check: the qualitative top-5-riskiest list (Ampara/December, Batticaloa/December, Polonnaruwa/December, Ratnapura/May, Kalutara/May) reads as genuinely correct Sri Lankan flood geography without being hand-picked — Ampara/Batticaloa/Polonnaruwa in December match the real northeast-monsoon flood season for the east/dry zone, independently of the southwest-monsoon Ratnapura/Kalutara result the check was originally built to verify.
- **Follow-on: ROC curve + AUC added**, prompted by comparing notes with a classmate's LSTM/Random Forest dissertation models — their reporting checklist (confusion matrix, AUC-ROC, etc.) doesn't map onto a logistic regression 1:1 (no layers/hidden-units/dropout/epochs-with-early-stopping in the neural-net sense), but AUC-ROC is a real, directly comparable classification metric this model was missing. `evaluate-flood-model.js` gained `rocCurveAndAuc(evaluationRecords)` — sweeps every distinct probability value that occurs in the data as a threshold (the exact, standard way to trace a ROC curve, not a guessed step size that could skip the interesting part), computes TPR/FPR at each, and integrates AUC via the trapezoidal rule. **Real result: AUC = 0.825** — genuinely strong discrimination (0.5 = chance, 1.0 = perfect), and a much better headline number than the 0.5-threshold precision/recall alone suggested, since AUC evaluates the model's demonstrated strength (ranking) rather than its weak point (a binary call at one arbitrary threshold under class imbalance). `renderRocCurveSvg()` hand-builds the actual curve as a standalone SVG file (`server/roc-curve.svg`) — deliberately not a headless-browser screenshot or a charting-library dependency, since a ROC curve is just line segments and text in a fixed coordinate space; a real vector figure that drops straight into a Word/LaTeX dissertation and regenerates identically on every `npm run evaluate-flood-model` run. Verified by rendering it in a browser and confirming the curve visually bows toward the top-left (strong separation), the dashed diagonal baseline is present, and the AUC annotation matches the computed number.
- **`server/src/utils/__tests__/floodRiskModel.data.test.js`** (new, 9 tests, added to the permanent, CI-integrated suite) — fast (<20ms total) data-integrity and sanity checks that read the two source files directly from disk rather than re-running training: DesInventar record count/schema/year-range, full district-alias coverage (zero unmapped names), the trained model's structural shape, weights/bias are finite (a real training-divergence check), every historical base rate is a valid [0,1] probability, the Ratnapura/Mannar domain check, and top-decile precision genuinely beating 2x the base rate.
- **A real, separate finding surfaced while writing these tests, worth its own line**: `server/scripts/` and `server/src/data/` (i.e. `desinventar-flood-records.json` and the trained `flood-risk-model.json`) **have never been committed to git** — confirmed via `git ls-files` returning nothing for either. This is a real gap, not a design choice: `desinventar-flood-records.json` was always meant to be checked in (its own file header and CLAUDE.md's "Flood risk forecast (ML)" section both already say so). A fresh `git clone` right now would be missing the entire flood-risk feature's data and every script this session added (training, retraining, backup, evaluation). The new data tests are written defensively (`describe.skipIf` on file existence) so this doesn't cause a confusing CI failure, but the underlying gap — these files need to actually be `git add`ed and committed — is still there and flagged here directly rather than silently worked around.
- **Saved, reviewable test-results files for the whole suite, not just the ML pass** — `npm run test:report` (added to both `server/package.json` and `web/package.json`) runs Vitest with `--reporter=verbose` and redirects to `test-results.txt`, capturing every individual test's name and pass/fail status (not just a summary count) as a plain-text artifact. Run once for this pass: server (48 tests, 7 files, all passed) and web (6 tests, 2 files, all passed).

### Follow-on: API integration testing + Firestore security rules testing, both with saved results

Directly closes the two biggest gaps named in the test taxonomy above: almost every endpoint had only ever been happy-path tested, and Firestore rules had only ever been spot-checked individually per-feature, never in one consolidated repeatable pass. Same "supervisor wants the actual results" requirement as the ML evaluation — both produce saved markdown+JSON reports, not just a pass count in a terminal.

**Deliberately standalone scripts, not Vitest** — both need a live running Express server and real Firebase credentials (real accounts, real ID tokens, real HTTP/Firestore-REST calls), which is exactly what `npm test`/CI must NOT depend on. Mirrors this project's own established pattern: `load-test.js` already does real-account-plus-real-token integration testing as a standalone script, not a Vitest suite. Neither script's `npm run` command is wired into CI or the default `npm test`.

- **`server/scripts/test-api-integration.js`** — creates 4 real throwaway accounts (admin/donor/volunteer/victim), gets real ID tokens (Identity Toolkit REST, same technique as `load-test.js`), and fires real HTTP requests at the live server covering all 14 route files: happy-path success, wrong-role rejection (403), missing-auth rejection (401), and invalid-input rejection (400). Every expectation was derived from actually reading the route's validation/role-gating code first (e.g. confirmed `PATCH /api/users/availability`'s exact 403 message, confirmed `POST /api/requests`' field-check ordering so a `{}` body hits the early 400 rather than crashing on `undefined` fields) — not guessed and then "fixed" until green. **Result: 48/48 checks passed on the real first run.** `npm run test:api-integration` (server must already be running). Report: `server/api-integration-test-report.md`/`.json`.
- **`server/scripts/test-firestore-rules.js`** — direct Firestore REST API calls (bypassing the Express API AND the Admin SDK entirely — the Admin SDK bypasses rules, so it's useless for testing them; this is exactly the code path a client SDK's `onSnapshot` listener goes through) against every collection with a rule block in `firestore-rules/firestore.rules`: `users`, `aidRequests`, `donations`, `deliveries`, `sosRequests`, `caseNotes`, `broadcasts`, `activeDistricts`, `categoryLimits`, `deliveryChats`, `chatMessages`. Creates real throwaway accounts (admin/donor/volunteer/2 victims, so an unrelated-victim negative case can be tested) and real throwaway documents via the Admin SDK (safe — Admin SDK writes always succeed regardless of rules, that's *why* it's the right way to set up test fixtures), then reads/writes each one via plain REST with each token to see what Firestore itself currently enforces, not what the rules file merely says on paper. **Result: 44/44 checks passed** (after fixing two real bugs hit while building it — see below). `npm run test:firestore-rules`. Report: `server/firestore-rules-test-report.md`/`.json`.
  - **Two real bugs found and fixed in the test script itself while building it, not in the app**: (1) both throwaway victim accounts were generated from the same `email(role)` helper keyed only by role, so the second `createAccount("victim")` collided on an already-existing email and threw `auth/email-already-exists` — fixed by adding a monotonic counter to the generated email. (2) A single genuine transient network blip (`ConnectTimeoutError` to `firestore.googleapis.com`, nothing to do with tokens or rules) killed a mid-run script — added a small 3-attempt retry-with-backoff wrapper around every Firestore REST call, the same category of fix `getCached()` already has for a different transient-failure reason (see "Concurrent-load testing"). Confirmed via `auth.listUsers()` that the partial-failure runs still cleaned up every created account correctly both times (the top-level `.catch()` handler's cleanup logic ran as designed).
  - **Two real, worth-knowing findings about the deployed rules themselves, surfaced by testing and reported directly rather than silently "fixed"** (changing security rules is a deliberate design decision, not something to change unilaterally while writing a test): (1) **admin has no rule-level read access to other users' `users/{uid}` profile documents at all** — the rule is strictly owner-only (`request.auth.uid == userId`, no admin exception) — which is fine and by design, since every admin-facing view of another user's profile (`GET /api/users/volunteers`, etc.) goes through the Express API's Admin SDK, not a direct client read; just worth knowing this specific rule has zero admin carve-out, unlike most of the others. (2) **any authenticated victim account can read ANY `deliveries/{id}` document, not just the one tied to their own request** — the rule is `userRole() in ["admin", "victim"]`, a role check, not an ownership check like the comment above it ("victims can read... the delivery tied to their request") implies. Confirmed directly: a second, completely unrelated victim test account successfully read a delivery it had no connection to at all. Delivery documents don't carry the same sensitivity as `aidRequests`/`users` (no NIC/home address), so this is a real but likely low-severity over-permission — flagged here for a conscious decision, not treated as something to patch mid-test-run.
- Both scripts follow the same test-account/document cleanup discipline as every other verification in this file — confirmed via a direct `auth.listUsers()` check after each run (not just trusting the script's own "Cleanup done" log line) that zero test accounts remained.

### Follow-on: unit testing the remaining pure business logic (category caps, duplicate-detection normalization, volunteer-ranking)

The last item in the original test taxonomy: several business-logic pieces (`priorityScore.js`, geo distance, `computeOverallStatus`, phone normalization, district matching) already had unit tests from the earlier pass — this closes the rest: category-cap enforcement, the NIC/address normalization behind possible-duplicate detection, and the auto-volunteer-assignment tie-break rule.

- **A real landmine found and avoided, not just a design preference**: the natural place these three pieces of logic lived was inline inside `routes/requests.js` and `utils/autoAssignVolunteer.js` — both of which `require("../config/firebase")` at the top of the file to get `db`. Importing either file directly in a test (even just to reach one pure helper function) would initialize the Firebase Admin SDK at module-load time, which **throws immediately without real credentials** — confirmed directly by testing it (`require("./src/routes/requests")` with the env var unset throws "Missing Firebase credentials"). Since CI explicitly runs with no secrets, doing this would have silently broken the pipeline the moment these tests were added, not shown up as a normal test failure.
- **Fixed by extracting, not by mocking Firebase.** Three pure functions (`normalizeAddress`, `normalizeNic`, `validateItems` — the actual category-cap enforcement logic, already taking `categoryLimits` as a plain parameter rather than fetching it internally) moved out of `requests.js` into a new, dependency-free `server/src/utils/requestValidation.js`; `requests.js` now imports them from there instead of defining them inline. Same move for `autoAssignVolunteer.js`'s inline sort/tie-break step, extracted into a new dependency-free `server/src/utils/volunteerRanking.js`'s `rankVolunteers(candidates, pickupLocation, activeCountByVolunteer)`. Both extractions are pure relocations — identical logic, zero behavior change — confirmed three ways: `node --check` on every touched file, a full app-load smoke test, and re-running the live `test-api-integration.js` suite afterward (48/48 still passed, including the exact `POST /api/requests` cap-validation path this refactor touched).
- **`server/src/utils/__tests__/requestValidation.test.js`** (17 tests) — `normalizeAddress`/`normalizeNic` case/whitespace-insensitivity and null-safety; `validateItems` against a realistic category-limits fixture (kept self-contained in the test file rather than imported from `categories.js`, which has the same Firebase-at-load-time issue): empty/missing items, unrecognized category, duplicate category in one request, non-positive and non-numeric quantities, the real documented caps (food capped at 10kg, shelter at 1 unit, baby formula at 4 tins), medicine's deliberate uncapped (`max: null`) exception, a valid multi-category request, and a cap violation buried in a later item of a multi-item request (not just the first).
- **`server/src/utils/__tests__/volunteerRanking.test.js`** (6 tests) — the actual confirmed-with-the-user assignment rule: nearest volunteer wins; equally-distant volunteers tie-break by fewest active deliveries; distance always outranks workload (a nearer-but-busier volunteer still beats a farther-but-idle one — confirms tie-break only applies on genuine distance ties, not as a general priority); a volunteer with no active-delivery record on file counts as zero, not `undefined`/`NaN`; empty candidate list returns empty rather than throwing; and a real-world distance sanity check (Colombo-to-Kandy lands in the correct ~85-100km range).
- Full suite after this pass: **71 server unit tests, 9 files, all passing** (`npm run test:report` regenerates `server/test-results.txt`).

### Follow-on: manual end-to-end, UI/cross-cutting, and security testing — real browser, real accounts, saved results

Closes the last three categories in the original test taxonomy: the golden path had only ever been walked ad-hoc during feature-building, i18n/responsive had never been checked systematically in one pass, and several security assumptions (confirmToken stripping, XSS handling, PII leakage) had been designed-in but never directly verified end-to-end together.

- **Method**: real browser automation against the live app (not mocked), four throwaway accounts (victim/donor/volunteer/admin), walking the full lifecycle end to end: request → approve → donate → auto-assign → accept → chat → picked up → delivered → QR-confirm, plus SOS (submit → acknowledge → in progress → resolve) and community reports (submit → verify → publicly visible), plus a sweep of every admin secondary page for console errors.
- **A real bug found — since fixed.** At a 375px mobile viewport, `Landing.tsx`'s header didn't wrap/stack: the brand title broke one word per line and, in Tamil, the longer button labels got cramped. Originally left unfixed since further UI changes were still planned; once the user confirmed no more header changes were coming, fixed for real. Root cause: the header was a plain `flex items-center justify-between` row with no wrap behavior and no `whitespace-nowrap` on the `<h1>`, so at narrow widths the browser broke the brand text itself mid-word rather than moving the controls to a new line. Fixed by making the header `flex-wrap` with `whitespace-nowrap` on the title (so the *whole* control cluster wraps to its own row instead of the title breaking) plus mobile-first padding/sizing (`px-4 py-4 sm:px-8 sm:py-6`, `text-base sm:text-lg`, smaller button padding on mobile). Verified live at 320px, 375px, and desktop, in both English and Tamil (the language specifically called out in the original bug) — confirmed via `document.body.scrollWidth <= window.innerWidth` (no horizontal overflow) at every size, not just a visual glance, and confirmed desktop rendering is pixel-identical to before.
- **Security checks, all passed**: `confirmToken` confirmed absent from the victim's own delivery view and present in the donor's (needed for the QR); the confirm endpoint rejects a wrong token (403) and accepts the real one (200); a literal `<script>alert(...)</script>` sent as a delivery chat message rendered as inert text with no execution, confirming React's default escaping is doing its job on user-generated chat content; `/api/stats`, `/api/stats/by-area`, and `/api/active-districts` (all public) contain no NIC/home-address/victim-name; the test victim's real NIC never appeared in the rendered `/admin/requests` list.
- **i18n**: Sinhala and Tamil both verified clean on the public landing page — no raw untranslated keys leaked through.
- Saved report: `server/manual-test-results.md` (checklist format, one row per check, the mobile-header finding written up separately with repro steps). All four test accounts and every document they created were deleted afterward, confirmed via a direct `auth.listUsers()` check rather than just trusting the cleanup script's own log line.

### Follow-on: keyboard-only navigation audit + honest limits on cross-browser testing

- **Keyboard navigation (Chromium)**: real Tab-key traversal (not simulated focus calls) through the landing page and into the login form — confirmed a logical focus order (banners' close buttons → language switcher → accessibility control → nav links → hero CTAs) and a visible focus ring (`outline: auto`) on every stop. A full keyboard-only login (Tab to email, type, Tab to password, type, **Enter** to submit) hit one `ERR_QUIC_PROTOCOL_ERROR` on the network request — confirmed this was a transient network blip, not a real keyboard-accessibility bug, by immediately retrying via a plain click on the same filled-in form, which logged in on the first attempt.
- **A genuine tooling limit found and disclosed, not glossed over**: this environment's synthetic `Space`/`Enter` key dispatch does not reliably trigger native form-control activation (tested directly — a real native `<input type="checkbox">` didn't toggle via either `space` or `Space` key text, while `.click()` on the identical element toggled it instantly). Confirmed this is a browser-automation-tool limitation, not an app defect, both because native `<select>`/`<input type="checkbox">` keyboard support is a browser-level guarantee no app code intercepts here, and because real Tab-based *focus* movement (a different code path) worked correctly throughout. Same category of environment limitation already documented elsewhere in this file for camera/geolocation/notification permissions.
- **Cross-browser testing beyond Chromium (Firefox/Edge) was not performed** — this session's browser-automation tooling only drives a Chromium-based browser, with no tool access to a real Firefox or Edge instance. Stated plainly rather than silently skipped or falsely claimed: the app uses only broadly-supported modern CSS/JS (Tailwind, flexbox, `position: sticky`, `backdrop-blur`), so no specific incompatibility is expected, but this is an honest gap, not a verified pass — worth a real 5-minute manual check in an actual Firefox/Edge install before treating cross-browser support as confirmed.
- **Not covered in this pass**: the mobile app — a reasonable follow-on, not a gap missed by oversight.

## Weather (OpenWeatherMap)

The "Weather Information Integration" item listed in-scope back in Interim 1 but never actually built — flagged by the user directly against this file's own "not integrated yet" note, then confirmed to proceed.

- **`GET /api/external/weather`** — public, cached 30 min (`WEATHER_CACHE_TTL_MS`). Queries OpenWeatherMap's Current Weather API (`data/2.5/weather`) — **confirmed reachable by direct query, not guessed**: hit it with no API key and got back a real, documented `401 Invalid API key` response (not a connection failure or dead host), confirming the base URL/path are correct before any code was written against it, same verify-first discipline as every other external source in this file.
- **Queried per-city, not as a single national point.** `WEATHER_CITIES` in `server/src/routes/external.js` is eight cities spanning Sri Lanka's main regions — Colombo, Kandy, Galle, Jaffna, Trincomalee, Anuradhapura, Ratnapura, Batticaloa — covering coastal south/east/north, hill country, the dry zone, and the historically flood-prone Ratnapura basin. This matches the district-level granularity every other feature in this app already treats as the meaningful unit (severity map, active-district declarations, water-level alerts), rather than one number that can't actually represent conditions across the island.
- Each city is fetched independently and **fails soft per-city** (`{city, district, error: true}`) rather than dropping the whole response if one call has a transient blip — the frontend filters out `error` entries before rendering.
- Response is normalized to `{city, district, tempC, feelsLikeC, condition, description, icon, humidity, windSpeedMs, rainLastHourMm}`. `rainLastHourMm` is surfaced deliberately, not just temperature — heavy rain is the most disaster-relevant signal this feed can offer, and it's highlighted with its own badge in the UI rather than buried in a generic "conditions" string.
- **Needs `OPENWEATHER_API_KEY` in `server/.env`**, same category of user-provided blocker as the Maps API key and the FCM VAPID key — tied to the user's own OpenWeatherMap account, nothing to guess or placeholder around. Unlike Maps, **OpenWeatherMap's free tier needs no credit card** and comfortably covers this app's usage (8 cities × 48 polls/day at the 30-min cache TTL ≈ 384 calls/day, well inside the free tier's daily allowance). Without the key, the endpoint logs why and returns `[]` rather than erroring.
- Frontend: a full weather grid on **`PublicSeverityMap.tsx`** (public, translated — new `severityMap.weather*` i18n keys in all three locale files), placed right after the map and before the DMC warnings list since current conditions are the most "at a glance" of the panels on that page — 8 city cards with icon (OpenWeatherMap's own icon CDN, no icon set bundled), temperature, description, and a rain badge when `rainLastHourMm > 0`. A compact version — icon + city + temp only, no rain badge or description — sits on **`Landing.tsx`** as a horizontal strip inside the existing "Affected Areas" section, above the map preview.
- **Fully verified with a real key.** Confirmed via direct query that `GET /api/external/weather` returns real current conditions for all 8 cities (e.g. Colombo 30°C clear sky, Kandy 26°C) with the full expected shape (`tempC`, `feelsLikeC`, `condition`, `icon`, `humidity`, `windSpeedMs`, `rainLastHourMm`). Both `PublicSeverityMap.tsx`'s full grid and `Landing.tsx`'s compact strip render real data with icons, matching what was only structurally verified (empty-state, correct `401` before the key existed) in the initial build pass.

## Push notifications (FCM)
