This isn't only needed inside the Fellow Travellers panel: **every existing chat-button call site that constructs a donor↔volunteer or volunteer↔victim chat id** (`VolunteerDeliveries.tsx`, `DonorMyDonations.tsx`, `VictimMyRequests.tsx` on web; `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`, `MyDonationsScreen.tsx`, `MyRequestsScreen.tsx` on mobile) had its hardcoded `${d.id}_donor_volunteer`-style construction replaced with this helper — otherwise a donor or victim would keep trying to open the old, now-locked chat after a handoff instead of the fresh one. (`donor_victim`, the self-delivery pair, is untouched by this — self-delivery has no volunteer, so it's never versioned.) `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx`/`MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` additionally gained: a "Hand off" button next to each fellow-traveller's "Message" button; an amber "Handoff request sent" state with a Cancel button once one is pending (replacing the fellow-travellers list until it's resolved, since only one pending handoff is allowed per delivery); and a live "Delivery handoff requests for you" panel (two merged `onSnapshot` queries, same pattern Phase 1 already used for its own live handoff-adjacent state) with Accept/Decline buttons. New i18n keys (`handoffButton`/`handoffConfirm`/`handoffPending`/`handoffCancel`/`handoffIncomingTitle`/`handoffAccept`/`handoffDecline`) added to the existing `volunteerDeliveries` namespace, all three locales, both platforms.

**Verified end-to-end against the real backend** (throwaway accounts — 1 admin, 1 donor, 3 victims, 3 volunteers — real Firebase ID tokens, real HTTP calls, a one-off script written for this verification and deleted afterward): built two Kandy→Colombo deliveries (volunteer1, volunteer2) and a Jaffna→Jaffna distractor (volunteer3). Confirmed, in order: the two baseline chats exist at their original un-versioned ids right after accept; a handoff attempt across the mismatched-destination distractor is rejected (400); a volunteer can't propose a handoff for someone else's delivery (403); a real handoff can be proposed, shows up in `GET /handoffs/mine` for both sender and recipient, and a second simultaneous one on the same delivery is rejected (409); cancelling and re-cancelling behave correctly (200, then 400); declining leaves the delivery's volunteer completely unchanged; only the named recipient can accept (403 for an uninvolved third volunteer); **accepting actually transfers ownership** — `volunteerId` changes, `handoffVersion` becomes `1`, the **old** donor↔volunteer1 and volunteer1↔victim chats are confirmed `locked`, **fresh** donor↔volunteer2 and volunteer2↔victim chats exist at the versioned id with `status: "active"` and the correct new party, `GET /mine` now includes the delivery for volunteer2 and no longer for volunteer1, and a second accept attempt is correctly rejected (400); advancing the *other* delivery to `delivered` and then attempting a handoff on it correctly fails (400), confirming the QR-code boundary holds; and the audit log recorded `delivery.handoff` with the correct previous/new volunteer ids. **25/25 checks passed.** All 8 test accounts and every document/chat/handoff created were confirmed deleted afterward via a direct `auth.listUsers()` call, not just the script's own log line. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` (0 errors, same pre-existing warning count) clean on both `web` and `mobile`; `node --check` clean on every modified backend file.

**The `deliveryHandoffs` Firestore rules are now deployed**, confirmed with the user first (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules`), then verified directly against the live rule via real Firestore REST calls with real ID tokens (bypassing both the Admin SDK and the Express API, same wire-level proof technique already used for `sosRequests`/`deliveryChats`): the handoff's own `fromVolunteerId` and `toVolunteerId` both got `200` reading it, an unrelated third volunteer got `403`, and a direct client write attempt by the owner was correctly rejected with `403` (writes go through the API only). Test accounts and the test doc deleted afterward.

**Also verified live in a real browser**, not just via API/REST calls — this is what actually exercises the live-listener UI decision end-to-end, which the API-level test couldn't (that hit the routes directly with the Admin SDK bypassing rules, so it never proved the `onSnapshot` listeners a real signed-in browser session relies on would actually work). Seeded two volunteers with real accepted deliveries (Kandy→Colombo each) via the API, then drove the actual running web app: logged in as volunteer1, opened My Deliveries, clicked "Find" in the Fellow Travellers panel — both a real unrelated pre-existing Colombo-bound delivery and the seeded match appeared (destination-only mode); toggling "Also match origin district" correctly narrowed it to just the Kandy-origin match. Clicked "Hand off" — hit this environment's one real, already-documented limitation (native `window.confirm()` dialogs are disabled in this browser-automation tool and auto-return `false`, the same category of thing already noted elsewhere in this file for `window.prompt`), worked around by overriding `window.confirm` to `true` for this one test click, matching how this exact class of limitation has been handled before. The request fired for real, and volunteer1's UI reactively switched to "Handoff request sent — waiting for a response." **entirely via the live Firestore listener**, no reload. Logged out, logged in as volunteer2: the "Delivery handoff requests for you" card appeared live with the real request, no reload needed to see it either. Clicked Accept — the delivery immediately appeared in volunteer2's own delivery list alongside their original one, both fully interactive (chat, Navigate, Mark as picked up). Clicked the handed-off delivery's "💬 Donor" button and confirmed it opened a genuinely fresh, active thread ("No messages yet," a live compose box) — direct proof `deliveryChatId()`'s versioned-id routing worked correctly in the real app, not just in the unit-level chat-id math. All seed accounts and documents deleted afterward, confirmed via a direct `auth.listUsers()` re-check (zero remaining).

## QR-code delivery confirmation

Replaces a same-device "Confirm receipt" button tap — the victim now has to physically scan a code shown by whoever has the goods, which is harder to fake.

- When a delivery reaches `delivered` (via `PATCH /:id/status` or `PATCH /:id/self-deliver`), the backend generates a random `confirmToken` (`crypto.randomBytes(16)`) and stores it on the delivery.
- `DeliveryQrCode.tsx` renders that token + the delivery id as a QR (`qrcode.react`) on `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` and `DonorMyDonations.tsx` (self-delivery) — shown to whoever has physical custody of the goods.
- `QrScanModal.tsx` opens the victim's camera (`html5-qrcode`) from `VictimMyRequests.tsx`'s "Scan to confirm" button, decodes `{deliveryId, token}`, and calls `POST /:id/confirm` with that token.
- **`POST /api/deliveries/:id/confirm` now requires the body's `token` to match `delivery.confirmToken`** in addition to the existing victim-ownership check — a wrong or missing token gets a 403, verified live.
- `GET /api/deliveries/by-request/:requestId` (the victim's own delivery list) deliberately **strips `confirmToken` out of the response** — the victim's client is never handed the token directly, only whoever generates the QR sees it. Don't add it back there even if it seems convenient.
- Camera access needs a secure context (HTTPS) in production — works on `localhost` for dev without issue, but note this when deployment planning starts.

**Note on live location:** `currentLocation` today is only a snapshot captured once per status-change button click, not continuous tracking. True "PickMe-style" live tracking (needed for turn-by-turn navigation) needs `navigator.geolocation.watchPosition` streaming into Firestore periodically, with the victim/admin side using a real-time Firestore listener instead of the current polling `apiFetch` pattern. Bigger lift than it sounds — its own task when navigation gets built, not a small addition.

### Follow-on: delivery details (category + delivery ID) were missing from both the volunteer's list and the QR card itself; the "fake QR" concern checked directly against live code

Two related user reports in one message: (1) neither `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` (web) nor `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` (mobile) showed what was actually *in* a delivery — their `Delivery` interfaces only ever declared `id`/`requestId`/`donationId`, never `category`, even though the backend (`GET /api/deliveries/mine`) already returns the full document including it; the field was just never wired into either UI. (2) a worry that "any QR" — i.e. a forged one — might be accepted by the scan-to-confirm flow.

- **Real gap, fixed on both platforms**: added `category` (and, where needed, `method`) to the `Delivery` interfaces in `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` and mobile's `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`, and rendered it in the card header ("Water — Delivery #a3f9d2"). Mobile's `MyDonationsScreen.tsx` and web's `DonorMyDonations.tsx` already showed category (no gap there) but had no way to cross-reference the linked delivery's own short id — added a `Delivery #xxxxxx` caption there too.
- **`DeliveryQrCode.tsx` (both platforms) gained an optional `details` prop**, rendered directly on the QR card itself above the code (category name + `Delivery #xxxxxx`) — so whoever is looking at the physical/on-screen code, on either side of the handoff, sees a self-describing label regardless of what else is visible on the surrounding page at that moment. Wired at every call site (`VolunteerDeliveries.tsx`, `DonorMyDonations.tsx`, mobile's `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`/`MyDonationsScreen.tsx`).
- **The victim's own request view** (`VictimMyRequests.tsx` web, `MyRequestsScreen.tsx` mobile) gained the same short delivery-id caption under each item, so a victim and the volunteer/donor delivering to them can verbally cross-reference "delivery #a3f9d2" during a handoff — this is also what made wiring the new per-item chat buttons (see the mobile chat follow-on below) natural, since the chat id is derived from the same delivery id.
- **The "fake QR" concern — checked directly against the live `server/src/routes/deliveries.js` code, not from memory, and it's unfounded.** `generateConfirmToken()` is `crypto.randomBytes(16).toString("hex")` — 128 bits of cryptographically-secure randomness (32 hex characters), stored on the delivery document the moment it's marked `delivered`. `POST /:id/confirm` requires `req.body.token === delivery.confirmToken` **exactly** (`deliveries.js:428`) — any QR encoding a wrong or fabricated token gets a 403 `"Invalid or missing confirmation code"`, already directly verified live in an earlier pass (see "Manual end-to-end, UI/cross-cutting, and security testing" above: "the confirm endpoint rejects a wrong token (403) and accepts the real one (200)"). A "fake QR" would need to guess both a real, currently-`delivered` deliveryId (a Firestore auto-ID) **and** its exact 32-character random token — computationally infeasible, not a gap in the design.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `web` and `mobile`; `npx vite build` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via `curl` returned `200` with the delivery-id/category-caption code confirmed present in the compiled output.

## Maps — built

Three map surfaces, all live:
- `SituationMap.tsx` (`/admin/map`, admin-only) — three-way toggle: "Requests" (exact pinpoint markers for every aidRequest colored by severity + donation pickup points, from the existing admin list endpoints), "Areas Affected" (district aggregates, same data as the public map), "River Gauges" (live gauge stations colored by flood-threshold status). Also lists recent DMC emergency warnings below the map.
- `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (shared component, `web/src/components/`) — toggle between "Areas Affected" (district-level circles, count + avg severity → none/low/moderate/high, from `GET /api/stats/by-area`) and "River Levels" (live gauge stations from `GET /api/external/water-levels`, colored normal/alert/minor flood/major flood). An `extraLayers` prop (default off) adds two more tabs, "Global Alerts (GDACS)" and "Tsunami Risk (Earthquakes)" — on for `/severity-map`, off for the Landing preview, see "GDACS + earthquake map layers on the public severity map" above. Never receives or renders a per-victim coordinate — everything here is either aggregated or public infrastructure data. Used by both:
  - `Landing.tsx` — embedded preview (420px), popups disabled (`showPopups={false}`) for a cleaner glanceable view, links to the full page
  - `PublicSeverityMap.tsx` (`/severity-map`, public, no login) — full-size (600px), popups enabled, plus the DMC emergency warnings list below it
- District attribution (for "Areas Affected") uses **nearest-centroid matching** against a hardcoded list of Sri Lanka's 25 district centroids (`server/src/utils/districts.js`), not real polygon boundaries — a deliberate simplification since no boundary GeoJSON was available and an external geocoding API would add a dependency/cost for something free-tier OSM tiles don't need. Points near a district border can get attributed to the neighboring district. Swap for real boundaries if precision ever matters.

### Real district boundary polygons (HDX COD-AB)

A follow-on request: replace the centroid-circle approximation on the public severity map with real district polygons, from HDX's official COD-AB (Common Operational Dataset — Administrative Boundaries) dataset — Survey-Department-of-Sri-Lanka-sourced, OCHA-vetted, admin levels 0-4 (country → 9 provinces → 25 districts → 339 DS divisions → 14,043 GN divisions). Admin-2 (25 districts) is what matches this project's existing district granularity.

- **The specific "live ArcGIS geoservice" the user asked for is genuinely dead — verified, not assumed.** `codgis.itos.uga.edu` and `gistmaps.itos.uga.edu` (the documented COD_External ArcGIS REST hosts) return **NXDOMAIN from both Google's (8.8.8.8) and Cloudflare's (1.1.1.1) public DNS** — not a local network fluke, a real global DNS failure. ITOS (Information Technology Outreach Services, University of Georgia — who ran this service) has itself been reorganized: their old site now 301-redirects into UGA's Carl Vinson Institute of Government. Flagged to the user directly (via AskUserQuestion) rather than silently substituting, same pattern as the DMC and GDACS findings above.
- **What's actually reachable: HDX's own file CDN.** `data.humdata.org`'s CKAN API (confirmed working, `curl`-tested directly) serves the same official COD-AB dataset as a downloadable resource, redirecting to an S3-backed URL. Downloaded and inspected directly: `lka_admin2.geojson` has exactly 25 features, real Survey-Department polygons, and — a genuine bonus — English/Sinhala/Tamil district name fields already included (`adm2_name`/`adm2_name1`/`adm2_name2`), on top of province, p-code, and area.
- **The raw file is too heavy to serve to a browser as-is** (~20MB for just the admin-2 layer — precision-grade GIS data, not web-map-ready) and the full zip (all 5 admin levels + lines/points) is ~130MB. `server/src/routes/external.js`'s `fetchDistrictBoundaries()` downloads the zip, extracts only `lka_admin2.geojson` via `adm-zip`, and simplifies it with `@turf/simplify` (Douglas-Peucker, `tolerance: 0.002`, `highQuality: true`) — cuts it from ~20MB / hundreds of thousands of vertices down to **~280KB / 6,749 points** across all 25 districts, visually indistinguishable at web-map zoom levels. Response fields are trimmed/renamed to `{district, districtSi, districtTa, province, pcode, areaSqKm}` per feature.
- **`GET /api/external/district-boundaries`** — public, cached 24h (`DISTRICT_BOUNDARIES_CACHE_TTL_MS`, the longest TTL of any external feed here — district boundaries don't change during a dissertation's lifetime). Fails soft to `null`, same as every other external feed.
- **The cold-cache download alone takes 1-2.5+ minutes** (133MB over a real network) — far too slow to happen inside a page load. Two mitigations, both in `external.js`: (1) the cache is **warmed once at server startup** — a fire-and-forget `getCached(...)` call at module load, so by the time a real user (or a viva examiner) opens the map, the fetch has almost certainly already completed in the background; (2) the frontend (`AreaSeverityMap.tsx`) fetches boundaries in a **separate, non-blocking `useEffect`** that never gates the map's initial render — if `boundaries` is still `null` (cold cache mid-warm-up, or the fetch failed), the map **falls back to the original centroid-circle rendering** exactly as before, so the feature can only improve the map, never break it.
- `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` renders district polygons via react-leaflet's `<GeoJSON>` (colored via a `style` function that looks up each polygon's district name against the existing `/api/stats/by-area` response — same severity levels, same color values, just filled into a real shape instead of a circle) with `onEachFeature` binding the same popup content as before. Districts with **no active requests get a distinct "No activity" gray** (`#9ca3af`, low fill opacity) rather than being invisible or reusing the "low" green — added a 4th legend entry and a new `severityMap.noActivity` i18n key (EN/SI/TA) for it, since without it a mostly-quiet demo dataset would render 24 of 25 districts as blank space.
- Verified live: `curl`'d the endpoint directly (25 features, correct trimmed properties, Sinhala/Tamil names intact); confirmed in-browser via DOM inspection that all 25 `<path>` elements render with the correct fill (1 district colored by its real active-request level, 24 in the "no activity" gray); clicked the colored district's popup and confirmed accurate content ("Colombo — 3 active requests — High need") rendered over the district's actual non-circular shape, not a circle; confirmed the same shared component still works correctly on both the Landing page's embedded preview and the full `/severity-map` page, and that the "River Levels" tab (still `CircleMarker`-based, unaffected by this change) continues to work.

### Manual pickup-location picker on the donation form

Prompted by the user's own question: every location capture in this app so far was GPS-only (`navigator.geolocation`) — fine when the donor is standing next to the item, wrong when they've ordered it online for a volunteer to collect from a shop, since the donor's *current* location isn't the pickup point in that case. First time this app has needed "click a point on a map" rather than "use my current position" — no existing pattern to reuse, built fresh.

- **`DonorDonationForm.tsx`** — the "Pickup location" field now has two modes, toggled by a small pill pair matching the delivery-method picker's existing style: "Capture my current location" (unchanged GPS flow) and "Choose a location on the map" (new). The map mode renders a small (260px) Leaflet map centered on Sri Lanka; a new `LocationPicker` helper component uses react-leaflet's `useMapEvents` (the only way to get click coordinates — `MapContainer` itself has no `onClick` prop) to set `location` on click, dropping a `<Marker>` at the chosen point. Both modes feed the exact same `location` state and `locationStatus` flow the form already validates on submit — no branching needed anywhere else in the component.
- **Applies to both delivery methods, not just volunteer-pickup** — a self-delivering donor could just as easily be dropping off something ordered from a shop rather than from their own location, so the toggle isn't conditioned on `deliveryMethod`.
- Verified live: registered a throwaway donor, switched to map mode, dispatched a real click at the map's center (a synthetic `MouseEvent` with `clientX`/`clientY`, since coordinate-based clicks needed a screenshot this environment couldn't take — confirmed this reaches Leaflet's own event handling, not just React's), confirmed a marker appeared and the "Pin placed ✓" text updated, then submitted the full form and confirmed via direct Firestore read that the donation's `location` field held the exact clicked coordinates (`{lat: 7.874, lng: 80.766}`, Sri Lanka's centroid — the map's default click target). Test donation and account deleted afterward.

## Internationalization (English / Sinhala / Tamil)

`i18next` + `react-i18next`, language auto-detected then persisted to `localStorage` (`i18nextLng` key), switchable via `LanguageSwitcher.tsx` (shown on Landing, Login, Register, TransparencyDashboard, PublicSeverityMap, and in the DashboardLayout sidebar for logged-in pages).

**Coverage as of this handoff:** Landing, Login, Register, DashboardLayout nav, VictimRequestForm, VictimMyRequests, TransparencyDashboard, PublicSeverityMap, AreaSeverityMap, StatusBadge, Footer, **and now DonorDonationForm, DonorMyDonations, VolunteerDeliveries, VolunteerNavigation, and the shared ChatModal** — every non-admin logged-in page is translated. **Admin pages (AdminRequests, AdminDonations, and the rest of the admin surface) are deliberately still English-only** — the user's explicit call: keep admin English, translate donor and volunteer. Extend the same way for anything new: `useTranslation()`, `t("namespace.key")`, add the key to all three `locales/*.json` files.

**Translation quality — read before treating these as final:** the Sinhala and Tamil strings are AI-generated best-effort, not reviewed by a native speaker. Reasonable for a dissertation demo of the capability and for the equity/accessibility argument in the research questions, but **get them checked by a native Sinhala/Tamil speaker before any real deployment** — mistranslation in disaster-relief communication is a real harm, not a cosmetic issue. This caveat is also in `i18n.ts`'s file comment and `LanguageSwitcher.tsx`'s comment; keep it there.

### Follow-on: donor + volunteer pages translated, admin deliberately left out

The user explicitly drew the line here: "keep admin as english only and add sinhala and tamil for the rest two" (donor and volunteer). This closes the last i18n gap on the web app's logged-in surfaces other than admin, which stays English-only by the same explicit instruction.

- **`DonorDonationForm.tsx`, `DonorMyDonations.tsx`, `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx`, `VolunteerNavigation.tsx`** all gained `useTranslation()` and new i18n namespaces (`donorDonationForm`, `donorMyDonations`, `volunteerDeliveries`, `volunteerNavigation`) in all three locale files. Category names use the same `t(\`categories.${key}\`, rawLabel)` fallback pattern already established for `VictimRequestForm.tsx`/`VictimMyRequests.tsx` — a category added via the admin UI shows in English until someone adds translated strings, never a raw i18n key.
- **`ChatModal.tsx` (shared component, built for the delivery-chat feature) also gained a `chatModal` namespace** — it's rendered on `VictimMyRequests.tsx` (already translated) as well as the two donor/volunteer pages just translated, so leaving it hardcoded English would have been a visible seam the instant a non-admin user with a non-English locale opened a chat. Role labels shown inside the modal (e.g. "Share my name & phone with the donor") use a small `chatModal.role.{donor,volunteer,victim}` map so the sentence reads naturally in each language rather than interpolating an untranslated English role string into a translated sentence.
- **`VolunteerNavigation.tsx` needed `TFunction` imported from `i18next`, not `react-i18next`** (the latter doesn't export it) — hit and fixed immediately via `tsc --noEmit`, worth remembering if this pattern is reused (`computeRoute()` and `formatDuration()` are plain functions outside the component, so they take `t` as a parameter rather than calling `useTranslation()` themselves).
- Verified live: registered throwaway donor and volunteer accounts, switched language mid-session (Sinhala for the donor pages, Tamil for the volunteer page + its community-report form), and confirmed every visible string — including the category dropdown, delivery-method cards, the map-vs-GPS pickup-location toggle, and the road-closure report form — rendered correctly translated with no leftover English or raw i18n keys. Admin pages were not touched and remain English-only by construction. Test accounts deleted afterward.

### Follow-on: mobile's donor + volunteer screens translated too, admin stays English (confirmed with the user first)

Mobile had never had any of this — only victim-facing screens + auth were translated on mobile (web's donor/volunteer translation pass above never crossed over). Before committing to translating mobile's 14 admin screens as well, asked the user directly whether admin should follow web's "English-only" rule or go further — **confirmed: admin stays English-only on mobile too**, keeping the app-wide rule consistent (admin = English everywhere, victim/donor/volunteer = translated everywhere) rather than mobile drifting from web's own precedent.

- **Scope actually covered, beyond just "donor + volunteer"**: since victim was already an established-translated role on web but two of its own mobile screens had never been touched, those were folded in opportunistically rather than left as a lingering gap — `VictimHomeScreen.tsx` (the tile-grid landing screen, added after victim's original i18n pass and never wired up) and the shared `SeverityMapScreen.tsx`/`AreaSeverityMap.tsx` component (used by victim/donor/volunteer alike). Plus the three role Home-tile-grid screens (`DonorHomeScreen.tsx`, `VolunteerHomeScreen.tsx`, and the same `VictimHomeScreen.tsx`) via a new shared `home.*` namespace (title/subtitle + one key per tile label). Full list: `RegisterDonationScreen.tsx`, `MyDonationsScreen.tsx`, `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` (donor/volunteer core screens), `NotificationsScreen.tsx` (victim-reachable, mobile-only — no web equivalent to reuse), plus the three Home screens and the shared map.
- **Reused web's already-translated namespaces wherever the wording matched**, rather than re-translating from scratch — `donorDonationForm`, `donorMyDonations`, and `volunteerDeliveries`/`volunteerCommunityReport` were copied key-for-key (English/Sinhala/Tamil) from `web/src/locales/*.json` into mobile's locale files, since mobile's screens were themselves originally near-verbatim ports of the web pages and the strings line up almost exactly. Only genuinely mobile-specific differences got new keys (e.g. `volunteerDeliveries.deliveryLine`/`rejectModalTitle`/`rejectModalSubtitle`/`reasonPlaceholder` for mobile's card-plus-modal reject flow, which has no equivalent in web's single `window.prompt`-based rejection).
- **`AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (mobile's shared 6-tab map component) needed the most restructuring** — its module-level `LEVEL_LABEL`/`GAUGE_STATUS_LABEL`/`RESERVOIR_RISK_LABEL`/`FLOOD_RISK_LABEL` `Record<string,string>` constants and its `TABS` array all had hardcoded English baked in at module scope, where `t()` isn't callable. Converted each to a small `xLabel(t: TFunction)` function returning the same shape, called with the live `t` from inside the component (`levelLabel(t)`, `tabsFor(t)`, etc.) — the same `TFunction`-as-parameter pattern already established for `VolunteerNavigation.tsx` on web. Also added ~25 new `severityMap.*` keys (`reservoirsTab`, `gdacsTab`, `earthquakesTab`, `floodRiskTab`, `reservoirElevated`/`reservoirNearCapacity`/`reservoirSpilling`, `gdacsAlertLabel` (interpolated `{{level}} alert`), `floodRiskLow/Moderate/Elevated/High`, `showSriLankaOnly`/`showGlobalAlerts`/`showRegionalEarthquakes`, `capacityPercent`/`capacityUnknown`/`districtArrow`/`floodRiskThisMonth` (interpolated), etc.) alongside the ~34 that already existed from the original victim-facing translation pass — reused existing keys (`highNeed`, `majorFlood`, `unofficialData`, `gdacsNoEvents`, `earthquakesNoEvents`, the `activeRequests` plural pair, etc.) wherever the English wording already matched exactly, rather than minting near-duplicates.
- **`NotificationsScreen.tsx`'s `timeAgo()` helper is the one place with real relative-time pluralization** — moved from a plain module-level function to one that takes `t` as a parameter (called as `timeAgo(n.createdAt, t)`) and uses i18next's `_one`/`_other` plural-key convention for `notifications.unreadCount`/`minutesAgo`/`hoursAgo`/`daysAgo`, the same convention `severityMap.activeRequests` already established. Sinhala/Tamil don't grammatically need the one/other split the way English does, but both keys were still populated for each (identical text for `_one`/`_other`) rather than left to silently fall back to English.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across every edited file; grepped all touched screens for any remaining hardcoded English JSX text (`>[A-Z][a-zA-Z ...]{3,}<` pattern, excluding lines already wrapped in `t(...)`) and found zero matches — confirming this wasn't just adding translations alongside forgotten leftovers. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8090`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward — the user's own real dev-client session was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB), with every new namespace and several representative new keys (`donorDonationForm`, `volunteerDeliveries`, `rejectModalTitle`, `volunteerCommunityReport`, `notifications.justNow`, `reservoirsTab`, `gdacsAlertLabel`, `capacityPercent`) confirmed present in the compiled output via grep — the same "prove it bundles, not just type-checks" discipline used throughout this file for mobile. **On-device visual confirmation not done from this session** — same standing mobile-testing limitation documented throughout this file; no native module was touched, so this should appear via Fast Refresh on the user's existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed.

## Visual design pass

Landing page redesigned to feel less generic: sticky header with a scroll-triggered shadow, subtle blurred-blob hero background (pure CSS, no images) with a wavy SVG divider, a live stats bar (`StatCard.tsx`, real numbers from `GET /api/stats`), a "How It Works" 3-step section with `lucide-react` icons, scroll-reveal fade-ins (`ScrollReveal.tsx`, IntersectionObserver, no animation library), icons on the role cards, and a proper `Footer.tsx` (platform links + DMC/Irrigation Dept data-source credit). Kept deliberately restrained — one accent color, no parallax, no heavy animation — since this is a disaster-relief site where "calm and credible" matters more than flashy.

`GET /api/stats` gained two fields to support the stats bar: `totalVolunteers` (count of users where role="volunteer") and `districtsReached` (distinct `nearestDistrict()` values among all requests with a location) — both also shown on TransparencyDashboard now.

### Color/palette reskin (deep slate blue + emergency orange)

A follow-on request, superseding the "one accent color" restraint described just above: the user supplied an AI-image-generation-style prompt ("modern... deep slate blue and emergency orange accent color palette... soft rounded cards...") and asked for the whole app to be reskinned to match, with an explicit "if I don't like it, change it back" condition. **No git commits exist in this repo** (confirmed via `git log` — `fatal: ... does not have any commits yet`), so there was no commit-based rollback available; the change was instead planned as a small number of mechanical, reversible find-and-replace patterns so it could be hand-reverted file-by-file if ever needed.

- **The core move: blue was freed up to mean "info/status" only, once the brand/action color moved to orange.** Grepped every `blue-*` usage across `web/src` first and found it was already cleanly split into two non-overlapping uses — (a) brand/structural color (solid CTA buttons, active-tab/nav-selection states, text links) and (b) semantic status color (`StatusBadge.tsx`'s delivery-status pills, SOS/broadcast/water-alert banner tints, GDACS "normal" badges, audit-log badges) — so recoloring (a) to orange left (b) untouched and unambiguous, with zero manual disambiguation needed file-by-file.
- **Mechanical, scoped sed passes across `web/src/**/*.tsx`, not a manual per-file rewrite:** `bg-blue-600`/`hover:bg-blue-700` → `bg-orange-600`/`hover:bg-orange-700` (26 solid-button/active-tab instances); `text-blue-600 hover:underline` and its two class-order variants → `text-slate-700 hover:underline` (text links only — confirmed via grep that every non-link `text-blue-600` usage was exactly 4 instances, all in `Landing.tsx`, handled by hand instead); `rounded-lg` → `rounded-xl` app-wide for "soft rounded cards" (safe globally because buttons use plain `rounded` and pills use `rounded-full` — confirmed no class collision before running it, so this one didn't need per-file scoping either).
- **Two "selected choice card" spots initially missed by the button/link greps, caught by a second full-inventory grep afterward:** `DonorDonationForm.tsx`'s delivery-method picker and `VictimRequestForm.tsx`'s category picker both used `border-blue-600 bg-blue-50 text-blue-700` for their selected state — visually a selection indicator, not a status badge, despite using the same color combination as one — updated to the orange equivalent by hand.
- **`Landing.tsx` hand-edited beyond the mechanical passes:** hero gradient background `from-blue-50` → `from-slate-50`; the two decorative blurred hero blobs recolored from blue/indigo to slate/orange; the "STEP 1/2/3" uppercase accent label → orange; the three role-card icons (`LifeBuoy`/`Gift`/`Truck`) → slate (structural/informational, not action, so kept off the orange reserved for CTAs).
- **`DashboardLayout.tsx` sidebar got a structural change, not just recoloring** — this is the one place the "deep slate blue... dashboard chrome" part of the brief needed more than a find-and-replace: background flipped from white to `bg-slate-900`, nav text to `text-slate-300`/`hover:text-white`, the active-nav-item highlight changed from `bg-blue-50 text-blue-700` to solid `bg-orange-600 text-white`, and the bottom profile/logout block recolored to match (`bg-slate-800` logout button). Every other page's layout structure (cards, forms, tables, modals) was left alone — only colors and border-radius changed there.
- Verified via `npx vite build` (clean, no new errors — the handful of pre-existing `tsc --noEmit` errors around `import.meta.env` typing and one `Earthquake` lat/lng field predate this change and are unrelated) and live in the browser: Landing page (hero, "How It Works" icons, map view-toggle tabs, role cards, footer), the login/register auth cards, and the admin dashboard (sidebar, KPI cards, Aid Requests table with priority badges) — all rendering with the new palette, semantic status badges (priority score pill, delivery-status pills) confirmed untouched and still their original colors. Registered a throwaway admin account through the real `/register` UI to check the logged-in sidebar/dashboard view, then deleted it (Firestore doc + Firebase Auth user) via the Admin SDK afterward, same cleanup convention used throughout this file.
- **If the user says they don't like it**: every change above is a small number of scoped, greppable patterns (`orange-600`→`blue-600`, `rounded-xl`→`rounded-lg`, the `DashboardLayout.tsx`/`Landing.tsx` hand-edits reversed individually) — there is no git commit to fall back on, so reverting means re-applying the inverse of each bullet above, not a `git checkout`.

### Sidebar overlap bug (fixed)

User-reported, from a screenshot: on `/dashboard` (admin role, which has the longest nav list — 13 items), the bottom-left profile name, role label, and "Log out" button visually overlapped the last couple of nav links ("Audit Log", "Volunteer Workload").

- **Root cause**: `DashboardLayout.tsx`'s `<aside>` had no height/flex structure — `<nav>` rendered in normal flow with no cap, and the profile/logout block was `absolute bottom-0`, anchored to the `<aside>`'s own box height rather than pushed below the nav content. Once the admin role's nav list (12 items + the always-present "Overview" link) grew taller than the aside's rendered height, the absolutely-positioned footer sat on top of the overflowing tail end of the nav instead of after it.
- **Fix**: `<aside>` is now `flex w-64 shrink-0 flex-col`; `<nav>` is `flex-1 flex-col gap-1 overflow-y-auto` (grows to fill remaining space, scrolls internally if a role's list is ever too tall for the viewport); the profile/logout block dropped `absolute`/`bottom-0`/the redundant `w-64` and is just `shrink-0` in normal flow. This guarantees the footer always renders immediately after the nav with zero overlap, for any nav length, on any role.
- **An earlier version of this fix also added `sticky top-0 h-screen self-start`** (to pin the sidebar to the viewport and let only the nav scroll) — reverted after live testing showed it caused a *different* overflow: `h-screen` is 100vh measured from wherever the aside sits in normal flow, and the real "Active emergency declared in: Colombo" district banner (a persistent bar, not a one-time dismissible one — see "Active emergency districts") pushes the whole layout down ~49px, so a full-100vh sidebar then poked ~49px past the bottom of the viewport. Simplest robust fix: don't pin the sidebar to the viewport at all — let it stretch naturally to match the row's height (its original, pre-bug behavior), with `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` on the nav as the safety net if content ever exceeds that height. No viewport-offset edge case, and the overlap is still structurally impossible.
- **Also removed while fixing this**: the admin `NAV_BY_ROLE` array had a redundant last entry, `{ labelKey: "nav.dashboard", path: "/dashboard" }` ("Dashboard") — pointing to the exact same route as the always-rendered "Overview" link above it (confirmed via grep as the only reference to `nav.dashboard` anywhere in the codebase, and via `en.json` that `nav.overview`/`nav.dashboard` are two different label strings for one destination). Removing it also shortens the admin nav list by one item, directly reducing how easily it overflows a short viewport in the first place.
- Verified live: registered a throwaway admin account through the real `/register` UI, measured the sidebar's actual DOM geometry via `getBoundingClientRect()` on `/dashboard` (13 nav links, all visible, no duplicate "Dashboard" entry) — nav's bottom edge and the footer block's top edge sit at the exact same pixel with zero overlap, both before and after re-checking with the real "Colombo" active-district banner pushing the layout down. Deleted the test account (Firestore doc + Firebase Auth user) afterward.
- Checked for the same class of bug elsewhere per the user's "fix all such issues" instruction: grepped `web/src` for other `absolute` + `bottom-0` combinations — none found, this was the only instance. Mobile has no equivalent risk — it uses per-role bottom-tab navigators with the logout button in the header (`LogoutButton.tsx`), not a persistent sidebar, so there's no analogous overflow-prone layout there.

### SOS modal rendering behind the Leaflet map (fixed)

Also user-reported, from a screenshot: opening the floating SOS button's modal on `/admin/sos` showed the Leaflet map's zoom controls and tiles rendered *on top of* the bottom portion of the modal, cutting off its text.

- **Root cause**: `SosButton.tsx`'s modal overlay used Tailwind's `z-50` (`z-index: 50`), but Leaflet's own CSS assigns much higher **raw** z-index values to its internal panes and controls — `.leaflet-pane` at 400, `.leaflet-top`/`.leaflet-bottom` (zoom controls etc.) at 1000 — which aren't on Tailwind's scale and easily outrank an app z-index chosen without accounting for them. On any page with a Leaflet map (`/admin/map`, `/admin/sos`), the map's own elements sat in the same stacking context as the modal and simply won on raw z-index.
- **Fix**: bumped the modal overlay to `z-[1200]` (comfortably above Leaflet's 1000 ceiling). Applied the identical fix to every other component sharing this exact `fixed inset-0 z-50` full-screen-overlay pattern — `QrScanModal.tsx`, `CaseNotesModal.tsx` — plus `NotificationSetup.tsx`'s corner toast (`fixed bottom-4 right-4 z-50`), even though none of those three are currently opened on a page with a map, since they're the same pattern and would hit the identical bug the moment one ever is.
- Verified live: registered a throwaway admin, opened `/admin/sos` (has a Leaflet map) and triggered the SOS modal, then used `document.elementFromPoint()` at the modal's own center to confirm the element actually painted there is the modal's content, not the map — confirmed `overlayZIndex: "1200"` vs `leafletPaneZIndex: "400"` and `isModalOnTop: true`. Deleted the test account afterward.

### Floating SOS button hidden on the SOS dispatch board itself

User asked to "remove [the] SOS dispatch small tab" — clarified via a direct question (ambiguous between the floating 🆘 button, the sidebar nav link, or something else) to mean the floating `SosButton` specifically, and specifically only while already on `/admin/sos`. Reasoning: an admin looking at the dispatch board is already at the "handle SOS reports" screen — a floating shortcut to *submit* a new one there is redundant self-referential clutter, unlike on every other page where any role might want to flag an emergency.

- **Fix**: `DashboardLayout.tsx` (the one place `<SosButton />` is mounted, for every logged-in role) now gates it on `location.pathname !== "/admin/sos"` — a one-line conditional, no change to `SosButton.tsx` itself, no change to any other page.
- Verified live: registered a throwaway admin, confirmed the floating button is absent on `/admin/sos` and still present on `/admin/requests` (and, by the same unconditional render for every other route, everywhere else). Test account deleted afterward.

### Auth screen close buttons + an enhanced "View Full Map" button

Two small, unrelated UI requests bundled into the same pass.

- **`Login.tsx` and `Register.tsx` had no way back to the landing page** other than the browser back button. Both gained a small "✕" close link pointing to `/`, `aria-label`d via the existing `common.close` i18n key. **Positioned top-right, with `LanguageSwitcher` moved to top-left** — the user's own explicit HCI convention call (close controls read right, not left) — not the initial placement, which had them the other way round.
- **Landing.tsx's "View Full Map" link was easy to miss** — small underlined text sitting next to the section header. Turned into a proper pill button (rounded-full, orange border/background, a `lucide-react` `Map` icon) matching the visual weight of this app's other CTAs, rather than blending into body text.
- Verified live: the close link renders correctly on both auth pages and returns to `/`; the enhanced map button renders with the correct pill styling and icon, confirmed in both English and Tamil (translation unaffected by the style change, since it still wraps the same `landing.viewFullMap` key).

### Follow-on: web Landing.tsx condensed (info-density pass, mirroring the mobile fix)

Direct follow-on to the mobile Home screen's "too much information" condensing (see the mobile section below) — the user asked to "optimize" `Landing.tsx` too. Asked a clarifying question first since "optimize" was ambiguous (info-density vs. trimming the map tabs vs. load-speed) — the user picked info-density, explicitly keeping every feature (all 6 map tabs, the weather strip, the stats bar) and asking only for tighter, less cluttered presentation. Web has far more room than mobile, so this was a lighter tightening pass, not a mobile-style gutting.

- **Hero**: `py-24` → `py-16 sm:py-20`; stats grid's top margin `mt-14` → `mt-10`. Text and CTAs unchanged.
- **"How it works"**: rebuilt from vertical stacked cards (a connecting horizontal line, an orange-filled circle icon, a separate uppercase "STEP N" label, then title, then description — 4 visually distinct elements per step) into compact horizontal rows (icon in a subtle `bg-orange-50` circle + title + description inline, `items-start gap-3`). Dropped the connecting line and the "STEP N" label entirely — redundant once the layout itself reads left-to-right — and reduced the icon from `size={22}` on a solid orange circle to `size={18}` on a light orange circle (quieter, less visually dominant on a page whose real CTA is orange buttons). Section padding `py-16` → `py-12`.
- **Role cards** ("For Victims/Donors/Volunteers"): same icon-above-text-in-a-tall-card treatment replaced with the identical compact icon+text-inline row pattern just used for How It Works, for visual consistency between the two back-to-back 3-column sections. Padding `p-6` → `p-4`, gap `gap-6` → `gap-4`, dropped the `hover:-translate-y-1` lift (kept a subtle `hover:shadow-sm` only). Section bottom padding `pb-24` → `pb-16`.
- **Affected Areas section** (weather strip + `AreaSeverityMap` with all 6 tabs) was deliberately left untouched — the user explicitly kept this content, only asking for the *surrounding* sections to be less dense. Bottom padding trimmed `pb-16` → `pb-12` for consistency with the tighter spacing elsewhere, no content change.
- No i18n changes — every string reused its existing key, just rearranged into a denser layout.
- Verified live: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; screenshotted the page top-to-bottom in the browser and confirmed the hero/stats, condensed How-It-Works rows, the untouched weather+map section, the condensed role-card rows, and the footer all render correctly with no overlap or clipped text; confirmed zero console errors.

### Follow-on: role cards trimmed to icon+title only, a real spacing bug fixed, short descriptions added back to How It Works, weather moved above Affected Areas

Direct user feedback on the pass above, pasted with a screenshot: still "too much words," a genuinely large empty gap between the stats bar and How It Works, and a request to reorder the weather strip ahead of the Affected Areas heading.

- **Role cards** ("For Victims/Donors/Volunteers") dropped their description paragraphs entirely — now a single centered row of icon+title only (`flex flex-wrap items-center justify-center gap-x-10 gap-y-4`), matching the fully-trimmed treatment mobile's Home screen already uses. The `forVictimsDesc`/etc. i18n keys are unused by this page now but left in the locale files since web's other surfaces don't reference them either — no harm keeping them.
- **A real layout bug, not just "add more padding":** the visible empty gap between the stats grid and How It Works was the hero section's own bottom padding (`py-20`, applied symmetrically top *and* bottom) stacking with the wavy SVG divider's height *and* the next section's top padding — three independent paddings compounding into one wall of white space. Fixed by splitting the hero's padding asymmetrically (`pt-16 sm:pt-20` / `pb-8`, since the stats grid is the last element and doesn't need matching bottom breathing room) and dropping the next section's top padding to near-zero (`pt-2`) — the wave divider alone is now the visual separator, not stacked with two more padding blocks.
- **How It Works got a very short one-line description back** under each title (`step1ShortDesc`/`step2ShortDesc`/`step3ShortDesc`, new i18n keys, all three locales — "Tell us what you need." / "We match you to help." / "You confirm when delivered.") — a direct, explicit user request ("add the shortest description"), distinct from the earlier pass's full removal. Rendered as icon + stacked title/description (`text-sm` title, `text-xs text-gray-500` description) in a `max-w-[180px]` column so the short second line doesn't force the row to wrap awkwardly at typical viewport widths.
- **Weather ("Current Conditions") now renders before the Affected Areas heading/description/"View full map" row**, not after — a straightforward JSX reorder inside the same section, no logic or fetch changed, just which block comes first.
- Verified live: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, all three locale JSON files re-validated with `JSON.parse`; confirmed in the browser that the stats-to-How-It-Works gap is now tight (screenshotted before/after), that each How-It-Works item shows its new short description, that Current Conditions renders above the Affected Areas title/map, and that the role-card row is icon+title only with no leftover description text. Zero console errors.

## Mobile app (Expo / React Native)

The user asked to start the mobile app, chose **full parity with web** (every role including admin, eventually) as the scope, and **Expo Go on their own phone** (not an emulator) as the test device. Given the size of that scope, being built the same way this whole project has been — one vertical slice at a time, each verified before moving to the next — rather than everything at once. `mobile/` is a fresh Expo TypeScript app (blank-typescript template), separate from `web/` and `server/`.

**Done: navigation shell, auth, all four roles, SOS, i18n, push notification client code, the full admin surface (main + secondary screens), community reports, and now maps.** Full mobile parity with web is functionally complete pending on-device verification of the two newest pieces (push notifications, maps) — both need a fresh custom dev-client build the user has to trigger themselves.

- **QR code generation on mobile uses `react-native-qrcode-svg` + `react-native-svg`**, not `qrcode.react` (that's a browser-only library) — `mobile/src/components/DeliveryQrCode.tsx` is the RN equivalent of web's component of the same name, same `{deliveryId, token}` JSON payload contract, shown to whoever has physical custody of the goods (volunteer or self-delivering donor) exactly like on web.
- **Donor flow** (`mobile/src/screens/donor/`): `RegisterDonationScreen.tsx` (category picker from live `GET /api/categories`, delivery-method choice, `expo-location` pickup capture) and `MyDonationsScreen.tsx` (status list, self-delivery "Mark as delivered" + QR display once delivered) — near-verbatim ports of `DonorDonationForm.tsx`/`DonorMyDonations.tsx`.
- **Volunteer flow** (`mobile/src/screens/volunteer/MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`) — accept/reject, picked_up/delivered progression (captures `currentLocation` via `expo-location` on each status advance, matching web), QR display once delivered, plus the availability toggle and the "Set my location" pill from "Automatic volunteer assignment" above (both needed here too, not just on web, since a volunteer testing only from the phone would otherwise never be eligible for auto-assignment).
- **A real cross-platform gotcha hit while building the reject-reason flow: `Alert.prompt` is iOS-only in React Native — it doesn't exist on Android.** The web version captures an optional rejection reason via a plain `window.prompt`; the first mobile instinct (`Alert.prompt`) would have silently failed to prompt for text on every Android device, which is what most real users of this app will be on. Fixed with a small custom `Modal` + `TextInput` (state-driven, `rejectTarget`/`rejectReason`) instead — works identically on both platforms. Worth remembering for any future feature that wants a text-input prompt on mobile: there is no cross-platform built-in for this, always reach for a custom `Modal` first.
- Community-report submission (the form at the bottom of `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` on web) was deliberately **not** ported yet — out of scope for this pass, picked up together with the rest of "Remaining admin pages + community reports on mobile" later, since it's really a community-reports feature, not core delivery flow.
- **Admin flow, deliberately simplified, not a full port** (`mobile/src/screens/admin/`): `AidRequestsScreen.tsx` (live `onSnapshot` on `aidRequests`, priority-sorted cards, approve/reject on pending, the same duplicate/medicine flag badges as web) and `DonationsScreen.tsx` (live `onSnapshot` on `donations`, "Find match" surfacing who got auto-assigned, the same assign/reassign picker from "Automatic volunteer assignment" gated on `pending_acceptance`) plus `OverviewScreen.tsx` (the same four-`StatCard` live KPI row as `Dashboard.tsx`'s `AdminKpiRow` on web — pending requests, unmatched donations, active deliveries, avg. time to verification). **Deliberately left off mobile, staying web-only**: the filter bar, bulk select, CSV/PDF export, and case notes modal on `AidRequestsScreen`, and the export/period-filter on `DonationsScreen` — desktop-table conveniences that don't translate to a phone screen and aren't worth rebuilding as mobile-native UI for this project's scope. `mobile/src/components/StatCard.tsx` is a new RN port of web's component of the same name (`flex-row flex-wrap` grid instead of CSS grid, everything else identical).
- **SOS flow** — `mobile/src/components/SosButton.tsx` (floating action button + modal, same type-picker/location-capture/optional-details flow as web's `SosButton.tsx`) and `SosStatusBanner.tsx` (live `onSnapshot` on the reporter's own unresolved report) both rendered once in `RootNavigator.tsx` — the mobile equivalent of web mounting them in `DashboardLayout.tsx`, since there's no single shared layout component across the four tab navigators; wrapped the whole logged-in tree in one `View` with the banner above `NavigationContainer` and the floating button as a sibling below it, gated on `user && profile` so neither renders on the auth screens. **The floating SOS button needed a mobile-specific position adjustment web didn't**: web's `fixed bottom-5 right-5` has nothing else docked there, but mobile has a bottom tab bar — placed the button at `bottom: 90` instead of the naive `bottom: 20` equivalent, or it would sit on top of the tab bar icons. `SosDispatchScreen.tsx` (admin, first tab — "the most time-sensitive queue in the app," same reasoning as web's nav ordering) is **list-only, no map** — mobile maps have since been built (see "Mobile maps" below), but adding one here was out of scope for that pass (the user's ask was specifically "severity map + admin situation map"); a reasonable follow-on if ever wanted, not a gap that was missed.
- **Shares the same `sosRequests` Firestore rules as web's SOS feature** — both `SosStatusBanner.tsx` and `SosDispatchScreen.tsx` read it directly via the client SDK's `onSnapshot`. The rules are now deployed and verified (see "SOS / rescue flow" above for the direct Firestore REST proof) — this was shared infrastructure with web, not a separate blocker, so it's resolved for mobile too without any mobile-specific action.
- **i18n (EN/SI/TA), matching web's exact coverage boundary at the time this was originally built — victim-facing + auth translated, admin/donor/volunteer stay English-only on mobile too, not a new decision, just carried over from web's own scope choice** (see "Internationalization" above). **Since extended** — donor/volunteer are now translated on mobile too, matching web's later donor/volunteer pass; only admin remains English-only, on both platforms (see "Follow-on: mobile's donor + volunteer screens translated too" under "Internationalization"). `mobile/src/i18n.ts` copies `web/src/locales/*.json` verbatim (same keys, same AI-translated-not-native-reviewed caveat) rather than sharing a single source of truth — separate projects, no build-time link between them, so a future translation fix has to be applied in both places. **The real port here wasn't the strings, it was the detection/persistence mechanism**: web's `i18next-browser-languagedetector` reads/writes `localStorage` synchronously, which has no RN equivalent (`AsyncStorage` is async-only) — worked around by initializing i18next synchronously with the **device locale** (via `expo-localization`'s `getLocales()`, mapped to en/si/ta or falling back to `en`) so there's never a flash of untranslated content, then asynchronously overriding with any previously-saved explicit choice once the `AsyncStorage` read resolves. `mobile/src/components/LanguageSwitcher.tsx` calls a new `persistLanguage()` export from `i18n.ts` on every explicit switch, the mobile equivalent of the detector's auto-persist. Wired into `LoginScreen.tsx`, `RegisterScreen.tsx`, `SubmitRequestScreen.tsx`, `MyRequestsScreen.tsx` — the same four screens' web equivalents are translated.
- **The rest of admin (`CategoriesScreen`, `BroadcastScreen`, `ActiveDistrictsScreen`, `WaterAlertsScreen`, `CommunityReportsScreen`, `AuditLogScreen`, `VolunteerWorkloadScreen`) plus volunteer community-report submission** — near-verbatim ports of their web equivalents (same endpoints, same logic), reusing `mobile/src/lib/districts.ts`'s `DISTRICTS` for `ActiveDistrictsScreen` exactly like web's `AdminActiveDistricts.tsx` does. **A real navigation decision, not just more screens**: 7 secondary admin screens would overcrowd a bottom tab bar (mobile UX convention tops out around 5 visible tabs), so `AdminTabs.tsx` gained a 5th "More" tab (`AdminMoreStack.tsx`, a native-stack navigator) holding a simple menu screen (`MoreScreen.tsx`) that pushes to each secondary screen — the 4 primary screens (SOS, Overview, Aid Requests, Donations) stay as always-visible tabs, everything else lives one tap deeper. The "More" tab sets `headerShown: false` on itself so its own stack's per-screen headers don't double up under the tab navigator's shared header. Community report submission was added as a new section at the bottom of `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` (volunteer), matching exactly where it lives on `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` on web — not a new screen, since a volunteer submitting a report is naturally already looking at their delivery queue.

### Push notifications on mobile — code complete, needs a custom dev build to actually test

**A real, project-changing discovery while scoping this: Expo Go dropped support for remote push notifications entirely, around SDK 53**, due to Apple/Google policy changes — this has nothing to do with this app's code, it's a platform-wide Expo change. Testing push notifications now requires a **custom development build** (`eas build` or local `expo prebuild`), not the plain Expo Go app this whole mobile build has been tested with up to this point. Flagged directly to the user rather than silently building unverifiable code; **the user chose to set up a custom dev build now** rather than defer push notifications or ship the code untested.

- **`mobile/src/lib/notifications.ts`** — mirrors `web/src/lib/notifications.ts`'s contract exactly (`requestAndRegisterPushToken()`, `onForegroundMessage()`), registering against the *same* `POST /api/users/fcm-token` endpoint with **zero server-side changes**. The key design choice: uses `expo-notifications`'s `getDevicePushTokenAsync()` — the **raw native FCM registration token** on Android — rather than an Expo push token. This is the same *kind* of token web already sends via the Firebase JS SDK's `getToken()`, so the backend's existing `admin.messaging().sendEachForMulticast()` call keeps working unmodified; switching to Expo's own push token would have required a second, divergent send path server-side.
- **This means the mobile Android app needs its own `google-services.json`** from the *same* Firebase project (`disaster-aid-system`), not Expo's — tied to registering a new Android app entry in Firebase Console with package name `com.disasteraidsystem.mobile` (now set in `app.json`, wasn't needed before since Expo Go doesn't care about package names). **Still needed from the user, same category of blocker as the FCM VAPID key and OpenWeatherMap key**: download it from Firebase Console (Project Settings > General > Your apps > add an Android app if none exists, package `com.disasteraidsystem.mobile` > download `google-services.json`) and place it at `mobile/google-services.json` (gitignored, same treatment as other Firebase credentials in this project).
- **`mobile/eas.json`** (new) — a `development` build profile (`developmentClient: true`, `distribution: internal`, APK not AAB for easy sideloading) plus stub `preview`/`production` profiles for later. **`app.json` gained `android.package`/`ios.bundleIdentifier`** (`com.disasteraidsystem.mobile`) and an `expo-notifications` config plugin entry — neither existed before since Expo Go doesn't need them.
- **A real regression risk caught and defended against, not just theoretical**: `NotificationSetup.tsx` mounts unconditionally for every logged-in user (`RootNavigator.tsx`), so the moment this code exists, it runs — including for anyone still testing the *other* already-working features (victim/donor/volunteer/admin flows) in plain Expo Go while the dev build is being set up. Since Expo Go's notifications native module may not fully support what's called here, **every notification API call is wrapped in try/catch, including `Notifications.setNotificationHandler(...)` at module load time** (a throw there would crash app startup entirely, for everyone, not just fail one feature) — fails soft to `"unsupported"` everywhere rather than risk breaking the whole app for the sake of a feature that can't be tested yet anyway.
- **What the user still needs to do themselves, in order** (none of these are things this session can do — account login and triggering a cloud build both need the user's own credentials, per this project's standing rule against creating accounts or entering passwords on someone's behalf):
  1. Get `google-services.json` from Firebase Console as described above, save it to `mobile/google-services.json`.
  2. `npx eas login` (their own Expo account — create one at expo.dev if they don't have one).
  3. `npx eas build:configure` (links this project to an EAS project, writes a project ID into `app.json` — needs step 2 done first).
  4. `npx eas build --profile development --platform android` — cloud build, takes several minutes, produces a downloadable APK (a QR code/link appears when done).
  5. Download and install that APK on their Android phone (replaces Expo Go for this project from here on — a dev-client build, not a production app).
  6. Run `npx expo start --dev-client` (not plain `expo start`) and open the installed dev-client app instead of Expo Go to connect.
- **Not yet verified — genuinely can't be, from here or without the dev build existing**: real device push notifications, the actual `google-services.json`-backed token retrieval, and the whole dev-client build/install flow itself. `npx tsc --noEmit` is clean and the Metro bundle compiles (`200`, real module count) — that's the limit of what's provable without the steps above.
- **The user's first `eas build` failed**: `[Reanimated] Your installed version of React Native (0.81.5) is not compatible with installed version of Reanimated (4.5.3)`. Root cause: `react-native-reanimated` was never a direct dependency this project chose — it's pulled in transitively by NativeWind's own dependency `react-native-css-interop`, whose `peerDependencies` range (`>=3.6.2`, deliberately loose) lets npm resolve it to whatever's newest at install time rather than a version pinned to this app's actual React Native version. Locally it had resolved to `4.1.7` (correct for RN 0.81.5) by the time this was investigated, and `package.json` had picked up an explicit `"react-native-reanimated": "~4.1.1"` pin along the way (likely written by `eas build:configure`) — but the **failed cloud build had almost certainly run against an earlier, unpinned dependency snapshot** (from right after `expo install expo-notifications expo-dev-client`, before that pin existed), so it independently resolved the same loose peer range to a different, incompatible version (`4.5.3`). Same root failure mode as the SDK-57-to-54 downgrade's nested-`react-native` bug — an unpinned transitive dependency drifting between install passes — just surfacing in EAS's cloud environment instead of locally this time.
- **Fixed with the same discipline already established for this exact class of bug**: full clean reinstall (`rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json && npm install --legacy-peer-deps`), then verified three ways — `npm ls react-native-reanimated` shows exactly one deduped `4.1.7` (no nested second copy anywhere in `node_modules`), `npx expo install --check` reports "Dependencies are up to date," and both `tsc --noEmit` and a fresh Metro bundle pull succeeded. **The currently-running/failed EAS build should be treated as stale, not retried** — it was kicked off against the broken dependency snapshot; the user needs to run `eas build --profile development --platform android` again now that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` are consistent, not wait on or re-trigger the existing one.
**Downgraded from Expo SDK 57 to SDK 54** after the user's installed Expo Go app (client v54.0.8) rejected the project as incompatible — Expo Go only ever supports the one SDK version matching its own current release, and the project had been scaffolded on whatever was newest at the time (57), ahead of what the user's Expo Go build actually supports. Fixed with `npm install expo@^54.0.0` then `npx expo install --fix` (the official Expo-recommended flow for changing SDK versions — updates `expo` itself, then re-aligns every `expo-*`/`react`/`react-native`/`@types/react`/`typescript` version to what that SDK expects). **Two real problems hit while doing this, both dependency-resolution issues, not code bugs:**
- **A stale nested `react-native@0.86.2` (the old SDK 57 version) survived an in-place `npm install`**, sitting under `node_modules/nativewind/node_modules/react-native` alongside the correct top-level `0.81.5` — two different `react-native` type identities existing at once silently broke NativeWind's `className` prop augmentation (TypeScript errors like `Property 'className' does not exist on type '...ViewProps'` across every screen, because the augmentation was being applied to the *wrong* copy of the `react-native` types). Fixed by deleting `node_modules` and `package-lock.json` and reinstalling once, fresh, in a single pass — never trust an in-place dependency-version change in this ecosystem, always nuke and reinstall clean when bumping the Expo SDK.
- **`npm install` on a truly clean `node_modules` then failed differently** — a real `ERESOLVE` conflict between `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage`'s peer range and the newly-pinned `react-native@0.81.5` that plain `npm install` couldn't auto-resolve (a known-common friction point in the RN/Expo ecosystem, not specific to this project). Fixed with `npm install --legacy-peer-deps`, Expo's own standard recommendation for exactly this failure mode.
- **A third, separate gap only surfaced once the app actually tried to bundle**: SDK 54's `babel-preset-expo` now expects `react-native-worklets` (for Reanimated-style babel transforms) even though nothing in this app uses worklets directly — bundling failed with `Cannot find module 'react-native-worklets/plugin'` until `npx expo install react-native-worklets` was run.
- Verified the fix properly, not just "it installed": confirmed `node_modules/react-native` (and `react-native-css-interop`, NativeWind's own dependency) each resolve to exactly one copy at the top level, not nested/duplicated; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; and — the real proof — pulled the actual Metro bundle directly via `curl http://localhost:8081/index.bundle?platform=android&dev=true` and got a `200` with all ~1420 modules bundled, not just a type-check pass. `package.json` now correctly reads `expo: ^54.0.37`, `react: 19.1.0`, `react-native: 0.81.5`.

- **Firebase JS SDK, not React Native Firebase.** Same Firebase project as web, same config values (`mobile/.env`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` — Expo auto-inlines any `EXPO_PUBLIC_` prefixed var from `.env` at build time, no extra config needed, unlike web's Vite-specific `import.meta.env`). Chosen because it works inside plain **Expo Go** with zero native build step — React Native Firebase needs a custom dev client (`expo prebuild` + EAS build), which would have blocked the "test in Expo Go" requirement from the start.
- **`mobile/src/lib/firebase.ts`** uses `initializeAuth(app, { persistence: getReactNativePersistence(AsyncStorage) })` instead of web's plain `getAuth()` — without this, a logged-in user gets signed out every time the app restarts, since RN has no browser storage for Firebase Auth to fall back to automatically.
- **A real, documented TypeScript quirk, not a runtime bug:** `getReactNativePersistence` lives in `firebase/auth`'s React-Native-specific build, which **Metro** resolves correctly (via the package's legacy `"react-native"` main-field, which Metro understands and bare `tsc` doesn't), but plain `tsc --noEmit` only sees the browser build's types and reports it as missing. Fixed with a module-augmentation declaration in `mobile/env.d.ts` (needs `export {}` at the top of that file to force module — not global-script — mode, or the augmentation silently *replaces* firebase/auth's real types instead of merging with them — hit this exact failure once while building it, worth remembering if this pattern is ever copied elsewhere). The same file also declares `*.css` ambiently, working around a second, unrelated TS 6.0 quirk: `tsc`'s `moduleResolution: "bundler"` demands an actually-resolvable file for relative-path side-effect imports (`import "./global.css"` in `App.tsx`) even with a wildcard ambient declaration present, a stricter behavior than pre-6.0 TypeScript had — worked around with a `// @ts-ignore` on that one import line instead of fighting the compiler further, since Metro (via NativeWind's plugin) handles the actual file at build time regardless of what `tsc` thinks.
- **NativeWind v4** (Tailwind classes via `className` on RN components) — keeps the "Tailwind utility classes only" convention consistent across web and mobile. Requires `babel.config.js` (`nativewind/babel` preset), `metro.config.js` (`withNativeWind`), `tailwind.config.js`, and `global.css`, all now in `mobile/`.
- **Navigation:** React Navigation v7. `mobile/src/navigation/RootNavigator.tsx` mirrors `DashboardLayout.tsx`'s `NAV_BY_ROLE` switch on web — shows `AuthNavigator` (Login/Register, native-stack) when logged out, then swaps to one role-specific bottom-tab navigator (`VictimTabs`/`DonorTabs`/`VolunteerTabs`/`AdminTabs`) once `AuthContext`'s `profile.role` is known. Every tab navigator gets a shared `LogoutButton.tsx` in its header (`headerRight`) since there's no persistent sidebar the way `DashboardLayout.tsx` has on web.
- **`mobile/src/lib/api.ts`, `auth.ts`, `districts.ts`, `context/AuthContext.tsx`** are near-verbatim ports of their `web/src` equivalents — all pure logic with no DOM dependency, so the only real changes were import paths and `import.meta.env` → `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_*`.
- **Victim flow** (`mobile/src/screens/victim/`): `SubmitRequestScreen.tsx` (multi-category picker with live caps from `GET /api/categories`, `expo-location` for the geolocation capture instead of `navigator.geolocation`, the same "no active emergency in your area" soft note as web), `MyRequestsScreen.tsx` (per-item delivery status, pull-to-refresh via `RefreshControl`). **QR confirmation uses `expo-camera`'s native `CameraView` + `onBarcodeScanned`, not `html5-qrcode`** (that's a browser-only library) — a real architecture difference from web's `QrScanModal.tsx`, not just a styling port, but the actual scanned payload contract (`{deliveryId, token}` JSON → `POST /:id/confirm`) is identical.
- **`expo-camera` and `expo-location` both need config in `app.json`'s `"plugins"` array** (permission strings shown in the OS prompt) — without this, permission requests fail silently or the native module doesn't build correctly. Already added for both.
- **Local dev networking — the part most likely to trip up a future session:** a phone running Expo Go can't reach `localhost:5000` (that would mean the phone itself, not this computer). `mobile/.env`'s `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` must be the computer's **LAN IP** (`ipconfig` → IPv4 Address under the Wi-Fi adapter), and the phone + computer must be on the same Wi-Fi network. **CORS is a non-issue for the mobile app** — `fetch()` from React Native doesn't send an `Origin` header the way a browser does, so Express's `cors()` middleware (which only checks `CLIENT_ORIGIN`) never blocks it; no server-side change was needed. **The one thing that *can* silently block this and can't be fixed from code: Windows Firewall.** The first time Node.js accepts an inbound connection from another device, Windows normally shows an Allow/Block prompt (or it may already be silently blocked from a past "Block" click) — if the phone can't reach the API, this is the first thing to check, not the code.
- **Verified real (not assumed):** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; the actual Metro bundle requested directly via `curl http://localhost:8081/index.bundle?platform=android&dev=true` returns `200` end-to-end (confirms every screen, nav file, and native module actually resolves and transforms — not just that individual files type-check in isolation); the Express backend confirmed reachable at the LAN IP via `curl`. **Not yet verified: an actual phone in Expo Go.** That needs the user to run `npx expo start` themselves (from their own terminal, so they get the real interactive QR code — a background-run instance doesn't produce anything scannable) and walk the victim flow for real. Do that before trusting this is fully proven, and before starting the next role's screens.

Continuing in the same "one at a time" order as every other feature in this file: donor flow next, then volunteer, then admin, then maps/SOS/i18n/push notifications, each verified before moving to the next.

## Not started

### Photo evidence — declined, not building this

The user asked for victims to attach a photo when submitting a request, and volunteers/self-delivering donors to attach a photo at delivery confirmation (would have directly answered the "no post-contribution visibility" gap in the dissertation's literature review, Section 2.4.4). It needs Firebase Storage, which — unlike Firestore/Auth — requires the Firebase project to be on the **Blaze (pay-as-you-go) plan** even to stay within the free quota. Initially left open pending a Google Cloud billing decision the user was making anyway for Maps navigation; **once billing was actually enabled for Maps, the user explicitly declined to also turn on Storage for this** — no more spending on this project. Treat this as closed, not "still waiting to hear back" — don't re-raise it unless the user brings it up first.

## Turn-by-turn volunteer navigation ("like PickMe") — built (web)

**The Google Maps blocker is resolved: the user supplied a real API key** (Google Cloud Console, project has Maps JavaScript API, Maps SDK for Android, Places API, Geocoding API, and Routes API enabled). Asked the user which to build first, web navigation or mobile maps — **they chose web navigation first**; mobile maps stays deferred (see its own entry below).

- **`server/src/routes/deliveries.js`** gained `GET /:id/navigation-info` (volunteer-only, ownership-checked against `delivery.volunteerId`) — combines `donation.location` (pickup) and the linked `aidRequest.location` (dropoff) into one response, `{id, status, category, pickupLocation, dropoffLocation}`. A volunteer has no other endpoint that would let them read either the donation or the request document directly (both are admin/donor/victim-scoped elsewhere), so this is a new minimal-surface endpoint rather than widening an existing one.
- **`web/src/pages/VolunteerNavigation.tsx`** (`/deliveries/:id/navigate`) — uses `@react-google-maps/api` (`useJsApiLoader`, `GoogleMap`, `Polyline`, `MarkerF`). Destination is derived from the delivery's own status, not a separate field: `status === "accepted"` → head to the **pickup** (donation location, not yet collected); `status === "picked_up"` → head to the **dropoff** (victim's request location). Volunteer's own position is a one-off `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition()` snapshot (same pattern as everywhere else in this app) with a "Refresh my location" button to recompute, **not** continuous tracking — that's the separate live-location-tracking rework already flagged elsewhere in this file, deliberately not bundled into this pass.
- **Deliberately built against Routes API's REST endpoint directly (`POST https://routes.googleapis.com/directions/v2:computeRoutes`), not the legacy `google.maps.DirectionsService` class, and not `@react-google-maps/api`'s `DirectionsRenderer`.** First version used `DirectionsService` (the obvious/default choice with this library) and hit `REQUEST_DENIED` on the very first live test — turned out `DirectionsService` calls the **legacy Directions API**, a separate product from Routes API despite the similar name, and the user's initial API list had Routes API enabled but not Directions API. **The user's explicit direction on fixing this**: don't enable Directions API at all — Routes API is Google's current/recommended product for anything started in 2026, so rewrite to call it directly instead. `computeRoute()` in `VolunteerNavigation.tsx` does the `fetch()` call itself (confirmed Routes API sends proper CORS headers for direct browser calls, unlike the older Directions/Places REST endpoints which need a server-side proxy), decodes the returned encoded polyline via `google.maps.geometry.encoding.decodePath()` (needs `libraries: ["geometry"]` passed to `useJsApiLoader`, as a module-level stable array reference — a new array literal every render makes `@react-google-maps/api` reload the script), and renders it with a plain `<Polyline>`.
- **Cost-conscious field mask, confirmed with the user rather than assumed.** Routes API bills per-step turn-by-turn text instructions (`routes.legs.steps` + `navigationInstruction`) at a pricier tier than a basic route (polyline + total distance/duration only). Asked the user directly given they'd just said "don't overuse the free usage, I don't want to pay" — **they chose the basic/cheapest tier**, so the `X-Goog-FieldMask` header requests only `routes.duration,routes.distanceMeters,routes.polyline.encodedPolyline`. This is also why there's no numbered turn-by-turn instructions list in the UI anymore (an earlier version, built against the wrong API, had one) — just the drawn route line plus a "17.0 km · about 44 min" summary line. Revisit this field mask (and the cost tradeoff) if per-step instructions are ever wanted later.
- **Billing still required regardless of which Maps product is used — this doesn't go away by avoiding Directions API.** Hit `REQUEST_DENIED` a second time after the API-mismatch fix, this time with an explicit `error_message`: `"You must enable Billing on the Google Cloud Project..."` — confirmed via a direct `curl` against the Directions REST endpoint (isolating the failure from any app code) that the *project itself* had no billing account linked, a separate requirement from which specific API is enabled. Flagged directly to the user rather than assuming Routes API would sidestep this (it wouldn't have — billing is a project-level Google Cloud Platform requirement for essentially every Maps Platform product, Routes API included) — they enabled billing, then this section's fix was verified working end-to-end.
- **`web/src/pages/VolunteerDeliveries.tsx`** gained a "Navigate" link next to the status badge, shown only while a delivery is `accepted` or `picked_up` (the two statuses that actually have a "go somewhere" destination — `pending_acceptance` has nothing to navigate to yet, `delivered`/`confirmed` are already done).
