- **A real bug hit and fixed early on, not just a config issue: `@react-google-maps/api`'s class-based `<Marker>` silently failed to render anything under `React.StrictMode`** (which `main.tsx` has always had enabled) — the marker constructor ran (confirmed via Google's own "Marker is deprecated" console warning firing), but no pin ever appeared in the DOM, no error either. This is a known StrictMode/double-invoke interaction with the library's legacy class-component wrapper. Fixed by switching to `MarkerF`, the function-component variant the library ships specifically for this.
- Verified live end-to-end via a seeded test volunteer + request + donation + delivery (Admin SDK, real HTTP login through the actual `/login` UI, mocked `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition` in-page since this browser-automation environment can't grant real OS-level location permission — same documented limitation noted elsewhere in this file for QR camera scanning): confirmed the pickup/dropoff marker switches correctly with delivery status, confirmed real Google Maps tiles render, and — the part that actually needed the Routes API rewrite plus billing fix to work — confirmed the route **actually renders as a real orange polyline following streets on the map**, with the correct "17.0 km · about 44 min" summary text, not just that the request was sent without erroring.
- All seeded test data (`nav-test-volunteer@example.com` + its request/donation/delivery docs) should be deleted after final verification, same cleanup convention used throughout this file.

### Mobile maps — built

`react-native-maps` (`expo install react-native-maps`) using the same `google.maps` config already wired into `app.config.js` (`android.config.googleMaps.apiKey`/`ios.config.googleMapsApiKey` reading `process.env.GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`, from the "Mobile maps — deliberately deferred" groundwork). This is the last piece of full mobile parity — everything else was already done.

- **`mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`** — shared Areas Affected / River Levels toggle, used by victim/donor/volunteer tabs (via `mobile/src/screens/shared/SeverityMapScreen.tsx`) and reused inside the admin situation map below. Public/aggregate data only (`GET /api/stats/by-area`, `GET /api/external/water-levels`) — never a per-victim pin, matching the exact same privacy boundary web's `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` enforces. **Originally scoped down from web's version** (no GDACS/earthquake extra layers, no real HDX district boundary polygons) — **since reversed to full 6-tab parity, see the follow-on below.**
- **Colored markers via a custom `<Marker>` child View, not `pinColor`.** react-native-maps' `pinColor` prop only approximates arbitrary hex colors (it's hue-derived from the native marker drawable on Android, not exact RGB), which would have desynced the map's colors from every other screen's exact hex values (`SEVERITY_COLOR`, `LEVEL_COLOR`, `GAUGE_STATUS_COLOR` — all copied verbatim from web's `SituationMap.tsx`/`AreaSeverityMap.tsx` for consistency). Rendering a small colored `View` (`ColorDot`) as the `Marker`'s child instead gives exact hex control, with a `<Callout>` child for the tap-to-see-details popup (the closest native equivalent to Leaflet's `Popup` on web).
- **`mobile/src/screens/admin/SituationMapScreen.tsx`** — the admin-only exact-pinpoint map (`aidRequests`/`donations` locations via live `onSnapshot`, same pattern as `AidRequestsScreen.tsx`/`DonationsScreen.tsx`) plus the same Areas/Gauges layers as the shared component, originally a 3-way toggle (Requests / Areas Affected / River Gauges) — **since reversed to the full 7-tab set, see the follow-on below.**
- **A real navigation-placement decision, not an oversight: this did NOT become a 6th bottom tab.** `AdminTabs.tsx` was already at 5 tabs (SOS, Overview, Aid Requests, Donations, More) — the documented mobile-UX ceiling this project already established when the "More" stack was built (see "Mobile app" above). `SituationMapScreen` was added to `AdminMoreStack`/`MoreScreen.tsx` instead, alongside Categories/Broadcast/etc., listed first since it's the most-reached-for of the secondary screens.
- **Where each map lives**: victim/donor/volunteer tabs each gained a new "Severity Map" tab (room existed — none of those three navigators were near the 5-tab ceiling); admin gets `SituationMapScreen` via More → Situation Map. `SosDispatchScreen.tsx` deliberately still doesn't get a map in this pass — that was flagged as a possible follow-on when maps were first deferred, but the user's actual ask here was specifically "severity map + admin situation map," so it stayed out of scope rather than being silently added.
- **The same native-module gotcha flagged when this was deferred is now live, not hypothetical: `react-native-maps` is not present in the currently-installed dev-client APK.** The JS bundle compiles and Metro serves it fine (verified — 1896 modules, no errors), but actually opening a map screen on the phone will crash or fail until a fresh `eas build --profile development --platform android` produces a new APK with the native module linked in. **This is the one thing this session cannot verify** — no dev-client build was triggered as part of this pass (that's the user's own step, needing their EAS login). Do that before treating this as fully proven on-device, the same "verify before trusting" discipline used throughout this file.
- Verified as far as this environment allows: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via `curl` returns `200` with all 1896 modules resolving (confirms every new screen/component/nav wiring actually compiles together, not just individually); color values cross-checked line-for-line against web's `SEVERITY_COLOR`/`LEVEL_COLOR`/`GAUGE_STATUS_COLOR`/`DONATION_COLOR` constants for exact parity. **Not verified: the actual map rendering on a real device** — blocked on the fresh `eas build` described above, the same category of "can't test from here" limitation already documented elsewhere in this file for camera/geolocation/push notifications.
- **Update: this is exactly what the user hit.** Reported "severity map not working" on their phone — this is the documented gap above, not a new bug. The dev-client APK they were running predates `react-native-maps` being linked in. Fix is the same fresh `eas build --profile development --platform android` already described in "Push notifications on mobile" below (the user was mid-way through re-obtaining this APK — see that section's own build-retrigger note) — not a code change. Nothing to fix here until a build with the native module is installed and tested.

### Mobile: public Home screen for logged-out users

User-reported gap, found by literally opening the app: unlike web (where `Landing.tsx` is the actual front door — public stats, description, role cards, before any login), mobile's `RootNavigator.tsx` sent every logged-out user straight to `LoginScreen` with zero context — no way to see what the app is or does before committing to registering.

- **`mobile/src/screens/auth/HomeScreen.tsx`** (new) — the mobile equivalent of web's landing hero: app name, the same `landing.heroTitle`/`heroSubtitle` copy web uses (these keys already existed in mobile's locale files from the original verbatim-copy-of-web's-locale-file setup, just never consumed by any screen), a live 4-card stats grid (`GET /api/stats`, reusing `StatCard.tsx` — total requests, completed deliveries, active volunteers, districts reached, same fields web's landing page shows), and two buttons: "Get started" → Register, "Sign in" → Login (reusing the existing `common.getStarted`/`common.signIn` keys, also already present but unused on mobile).
- **Deliberately no map preview on this screen** — `react-native-maps` isn't reliably working on-device yet (see the note just above), so building the home screen's first impression around a map that might not render would be a bad trade. Worth adding once maps are confirmed working on a real device.
- **`AuthNavigator.tsx`** — `AuthStackParamList` gained `Home`, set as `initialRouteName` (was previously unset, defaulting to whichever screen was declared first — `Login`). Login and Register are now one tap away from Home, not the forced first screen.
- **`LoginScreen.tsx` and `RegisterScreen.tsx`** each gained a small "✕" button next to the existing `LanguageSwitcher`, navigating back to Home — same layout convention as web's own Login.tsx/Register.tsx (language left, close right), added there in this same project for the same HCI reasoning.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; pulled a real Metro bundle via `curl` (200, 14.2MB) and confirmed `HomeScreen`, `getStarted`, and the new tab-icon names are actually present in the compiled output — the same "prove it bundles, not just type-checks" discipline used elsewhere in this file. Actual on-device click-through not verified, same standing limitation as the rest of mobile pending a working dev-client build.

### Mobile: bottom tab bar icons

User-reported: the bottom tab bars had no icons at all — every `<Tab.Screen>` across all four role navigators (`VictimTabs.tsx`, `DonorTabs.tsx`, `VolunteerTabs.tsx`, `AdminTabs.tsx`) only ever set `title`, never `tabBarIcon`, so React Navigation rendered text-only tabs. Not a "wrong icon" fix, a "no icon" fix.

- Used `@expo/vector-icons`' `Ionicons` — already bundled with the installed Expo SDK (confirmed present in `node_modules` before adding any code), no new dependency. A small `tabIcon(outlineName, filledName)` helper in each nav file returns the `tabBarIcon` render function, swapping to the filled variant when the tab is focused (standard React Navigation pattern).
- Icon choices, kept consistent for the same concept across roles (e.g. "aid request" and "donation" use the same icon whether it's the victim/donor's own screen or admin's list view): submit request `add-circle`, my requests / admin aid requests `document-text`, donate / admin donations `gift`, my donations `receipt`, my deliveries `car`, severity map `map`, settings `settings`, SOS dispatch `warning`, admin overview `stats-chart`, admin "More" `ellipsis-horizontal`.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; confirmed via the pulled Metro bundle that the new icon names (e.g. `ellipsis-horizontal`) are present in the compiled output. On-device visual confirmation pending the same working dev-client build as everything else mobile-map-related.

### Follow-on: mobile map layers brought to full parity with web (Reservoirs, GDACS, Earthquakes, Flood Risk Forecast)

User-reported, direct: "map only shows requests, areas affected, river gauges where are the others" — a real gap, not a misunderstanding. Both mobile map components had been deliberately scoped down when maps were first built on mobile (see the "Mobile maps" section above), on the reasoning that GDACS/earthquake/reservoir/flood-risk were reasonable follow-ons rather than blockers to closing that pass. This closes that gap for both surfaces at once.

- **`mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`** (shared — victim/donor/volunteer "Severity Map" tab) grew from 2 tabs to the full 6: Areas Affected, River Levels, Reservoirs, Global Alerts (GDACS), Tsunami Risk (Earthquakes), Flood Risk Forecast. **`mobile/src/screens/admin/SituationMapScreen.tsx`** grew from 3 to 7, keeping its admin-only exact-pinpoint "Requests" tab as the first one. All the new tabs hit the exact same backend endpoints web already uses (`GET /api/external/reservoirs`, `/gdacs`, `/earthquakes`, `/flood-risk`) — no server-side changes needed, this was purely a client-side gap.
- **Colors, labels, and risk thresholds copied verbatim from web's `SituationMap.tsx`** (`RESERVOIR_RISK_COLOR`/`RESERVOIR_APPROX_COLOR`/`GDACS_ALERT_COLOR`/`magnitudeColor()`/`FLOOD_RISK_COLOR`), same "exact hex values, not approximated" discipline already established for the original 3 layers — a reservoir, alert, or risk level reads as the same color regardless of which platform you're looking at it from.
- **Deliberately left out, consistent with this project's existing mobile-scoping conventions**: the country/district search-and-zoom boxes (`CountrySearchBox.tsx`/`DistrictSearchBox.tsx`, desktop-typing conveniences that don't translate to a phone keyboard well) and the GDACS cyclone hazard polygon (`<Polygon>` rendering — the marker + popup alone already conveys the same event info; the polygon is a nice-to-have visual, not the actual information). The GDACS/Earthquakes **scope toggle** ("Show global alerts"/"Show regional earthquakes") was kept, since without it those two tabs would be empty on almost any given day — the same reasoning that justified building the toggle on web in the first place.
- **A real map-recentering bug caught before it shipped, not after**: the first draft bound `MapView`'s `region` prop directly to a `viewMode`/`earthquakeScope`-derived value, making the map "controlled" — react-native-maps then snaps back to that exact region on every re-render, fighting the user's own pan/zoom the instant they touch the map. Fixed by keeping `initialRegion` (uncontrolled, applies once) and recentering imperatively via a `mapRef.current?.animateToRegion(...)` inside a `useEffect` keyed on `[viewMode, earthquakeScope]` instead — the same "controlled region breaks user interaction" lesson web already learned with Leaflet's `MapViewController` pattern, just rediscovered independently on the RN side since `react-native-maps`' API shape is different enough that the web fix couldn't be copied verbatim.
- **`SituationMapScreen.tsx`'s tab row was wrapped in a horizontal `ScrollView`** rather than the `flex-wrap` used on the shared component — 7 tabs (including two long labels, "Global Alerts" and "Tsunami Risk") don't comfortably wrap to a second line without either shrinking the touch targets or overflowing on a narrow phone; horizontal scroll keeps every tab a comfortable, consistent size. The shared `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (6 tabs, and embedded inside an already-scrollable parent screen) kept `flex-wrap` instead, since a second wrapped row costs nothing extra there.
- Verified as far as this environment allows: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via `curl` returns `200` (14.2MB) with `Global Alerts`, `Tsunami Risk`, and every other new tab label confirmed present via grep on the compiled output — the same "prove it bundles, not just type-checks" discipline used throughout this file. **On-device rendering not verified from this session** — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile maps, pending the user's own click-through on their installed dev-client build.

### Follow-on: SOS button made draggable

User ask, direct: "let the user be able to move it wherever they want." The floating 🆘 button was fixed at `bottom: 90, right: 20` with no way to reposition it — a reasonable complaint, since a fixed FAB can sit on top of content on some screens/roles.

- **`mobile/src/components/SosButton.tsx`** — replaced the plain `TouchableOpacity` with a `PanResponder`-driven `Animated.View`. `PanResponder` owns the touch entirely once it starts, so tap-vs-drag has to be disambiguated manually: `onPanResponderMove` accumulates total movement, and `onPanResponderRelease` only opens the modal if that total stayed under a small threshold (6px) — otherwise the release is treated as "the user just finished dragging," not a tap.
- **Position persists across app restarts**, not just the current session — `AsyncStorage` (`sos_button_position` key), read once on mount (falls back to the original bottom-right default if nothing saved or the value is corrupt) and written on every drag release. Same library already used elsewhere on mobile (Firebase Auth's RN persistence), no new dependency.
- **Clamped to the screen bounds** (`Dimensions.get("window")`, 8px margin) on release, so the button can never be dragged fully off-screen and become unreachable — "move it wherever they want" still means "somewhere you can find it again."
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; confirmed via the pulled Metro bundle that the new `sos_button_position` storage key is present in the compiled output. On-device drag interaction not verified from this session — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile, pending the user's own touch-test on their installed dev-client build.

User-reported: "landing page doesn't have the basic info like in web" — the mobile `HomeScreen.tsx` built earlier (see above) had the hero + live stats + two buttons, but skipped the rest of what web's `Landing.tsx` shows a first-time visitor.

- **Added two more sections to `mobile/src/screens/auth/HomeScreen.tsx`**: a 3-step "How it works" list (icon + step number + title + description, reusing the exact `landing.step1-3Title/Desc` i18n keys web's version already uses — these were sitting unused in mobile's locale mirror since it was copied verbatim from web at setup time) and the three role cards (For Victims/Donors/Volunteers, same `landing.forVictims/Donors/VolunteersTitle/Desc` keys). A short data-sources + disclaimer footer line was added too (`footer.dataSourcesText`/`footer.disclaimer`), skipping the link list from web's `Footer.tsx` since those link to pages that aren't standalone screens in the pre-login mobile flow.
- **Icons via `@expo/vector-icons`' `Ionicons`** (already a dependency, used for the bottom tab bar icons — see above), not `lucide-react` (that's web's icon library, not available on RN): `document-text-outline`/`people-outline`/`checkmark-done-outline` for the 3 steps, `alert-circle-outline`/`gift-outline`/`car-outline` for the 3 role cards.
- **Still deliberately no map preview on this screen** — unchanged from the original reasoning (see above): worth adding once maps are confirmed working on a real device, not bundled into this pass.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; confirmed via the pulled Metro bundle that `howItWorksTitle` and the new icon names are present in the compiled output. On-device visual confirmation pending the same working dev-client build as everything else mobile-related this pass.

### Follow-on: splash screen, a welcome pop-up on Home, and fixing in-app notification enabling

User ask, three parts in one message: "when the app is opened let a splash screen appear then a pop window on login and get registered with a close button and if closed lets go to the page with details," plus "let the notifications be enabled from the app itself rather than going out for settings app."

- **`mobile/src/components/AppSplashScreen.tsx`** (new) — a branded full-screen view (app name + "Sri Lanka" + an orange spinner on a slate-900 background, matching the app's own palette) shown while `AuthContext` resolves Firebase's auth state. **Deliberately plain JS/React, not the native `expo-splash-screen` module** — that package needs its own native code linked into the app, meaning yet another `eas build` before it would work on the dev-client APK already installed on the phone (the same "native module not in the current build" trap already hit twice this session, with `react-native-maps` and `expo-notifications`/`expo-dev-client` — see "Mobile maps" and "Push notifications on mobile" above). A JS-only splash achieves the same practical effect with zero native dependency, so it works immediately via Fast Refresh on the build already installed, no rebuild needed. `RootNavigator.tsx` also gained a 900ms minimum-display floor (`MIN_SPLASH_MS`) independent of how fast auth actually resolves — without it, a fast local network makes the splash flash for a single frame, which reads as a glitch rather than a deliberate loading screen.
- **A welcome pop-up modal on `HomeScreen.tsx`**, open by default on every mount: a `Modal` with a heading, "Get started" (Register) and "Sign in" (Login) buttons, and a ✕ close button (plus tapping the dimmed backdrop or Android's back button, both wired to the same dismiss handler). Closing it reveals the exact same Home screen content that was already rendered underneath — the "page with details" the user asked for — not a second navigation or a different screen. **Deliberately no "seen it once" persistence** (no AsyncStorage flag) — this is a lightweight quick-access prompt, not a one-time onboarding tour, so it reappears every time a logged-out user lands back on Home (cold app start, or navigating back after closing Login/Register, or after logging out), which is the simpler and more predictable behavior given what was actually asked for. **Originally built as a bottom sheet — since corrected, see the follow-on below.**
- **Notification permission fixed to actually work from inside the app, not just look like it did.** The real bug: `requestAndRegisterPushToken()` only ever called the native `Notifications.requestPermissionsAsync()` prompt when the stored status was `"undetermined"` — the very first time. Once a user denied it once, every later tap of "Enable" silently did nothing (returned `"denied"` immediately, no dialog shown at all), which is exactly what reads as "I have to go to the phone's Settings app myself." Fixed in `mobile/src/lib/notifications.ts`: now always calls `requestPermissionsAsync()` unless permission is already granted — Android genuinely re-shows the in-app dialog on a second attempt as long as the user hasn't checked "don't ask again" (`canAskAgain` stays true), and the call is safe to make even when the OS has permanently blocked it (it just resolves with the still-denied status, no crash, no UI). Added `getNotificationPermissionState()` (exposes `canAskAgain`) and `openNotificationSettings()` (a one-tap `Linking.openSettings()` deep link) as the honest fallback for the one case no app can route around — once Android's "don't ask again" or iOS's one-shot prompt has permanently blocked further in-app dialogs, the OS itself requires a trip to its own settings screen; the fix here is making that a single deep-linked tap from inside the app instead of a manual "find Settings > Apps > Disaster Aid > Notifications yourself" hunt.
  - **Both places notifications are ever enabled from got the same fix**: `NotificationSetup.tsx`'s top banner (shown to every logged-in user) now also appears for `denied && canAskAgain` (not just `undetermined`, same "Enable" button, now actually retries), and gained a second amber variant for `denied && !canAskAgain` with an "Open Settings" button instead. `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s Notifications section (see "Settings page" above) got the identical split — a real retry button when still possible, a Settings deep-link button when not, replacing what used to be just a dead-end sentence with no action at all.
  - New `settings.openNotificationSettings` and `common.welcomeModalTitle`/`welcomeModalSubtitle` i18n keys added to all three locale files; `settings.notificationsBlocked`'s wording was updated to say "tap below" now that a button actually follows it.
- Verified as far as this environment allows: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via `curl` returns `200` (14.3MB) with `welcomeModalTitle`, `openNotificationSettings`, `sos_button_position` (confirming the earlier draggable-SOS-button follow-on is still intact), and `AppSplashScreen`/`MIN_SPLASH_MS` all confirmed present in the compiled output via grep — the same "prove it bundles, not just type-checks" discipline used throughout this file. **On-device verification not done from this session** — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile, pending the user's own click-through on their installed dev-client build (none of this pass touches a native module, so no new `eas build` is needed first, unlike the maps/push-notification passes).

### Follow-on: welcome modal recentered, Home screen condensed

Two pieces of direct user feedback on the pass immediately above. First, on the modal: "the pop is in the bottom we need in the middle, please use common UI rules." Second, on the whole screen: "optimize the landing page as much as possible, too much information."

- **Modal repositioned from a bottom sheet to a centered dialog** — the standard pattern for a simple two-choice prompt like "sign in or register" (bottom sheets are the right call for longer lists/menus, not a 2-button choice). `HomeScreen.tsx`'s `Modal` wrapper changed from `justify-end` (slide-up sheet, `rounded-t-3xl` on just the top corners) to `justify-center items-center` with a `w-full max-w-sm rounded-2xl` card (rounded on all four corners, fixed side margins, `animationType="fade"` instead of `"slide"`) — a conventional centered dialog, not a sheet anchored to an edge.
- **The rest of the screen was cut down hard, not just trimmed at the margins.** The "How it works" section dropped its per-step description paragraphs and "STEP N" labels entirely, condensing to a single row of 3 small icon+one-line-label items — the full explanation stays on web's `Landing.tsx`, a phone screen only needs the gist before the modal (or the page's own Get Started button) takes over. The 3 role cards (bordered boxes with a title + description paragraph each) were replaced the same way — one compact row of icon+label only, no borders, no descriptions. The footer block (`footer.dataSourcesText` + `footer.disclaimer`) was **removed from this screen entirely** — data-source attribution and legal disclaimer text are lower priority than getting a first-time visitor to Sign in/Register quickly, and they're still shown elsewhere (this project's admin/public web surfaces). Net effect: roughly half the scroll length of the immediately preceding version, while keeping every piece of information present, just denser.
- **Lesson worth keeping**: the user's exact words — "please use common UI rules its not like you are a programmed robot" — is feedback to actively apply established UI/UX conventions (a centered dialog for a binary choice, not whatever happened to be quickest to write) rather than defaulting to an arbitrary implementation that technically satisfies the literal ask. Keep this in mind for any future UI work on this project, mobile or web.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via `curl` returned `200` with the old per-role description keys (`forVictimsDesc` etc.) no longer referenced by `HomeScreen.tsx` (confirmed via source read, not just bundle presence — those keys are still used by web's `Landing.tsx`, so they remain in the locale files, just unconsumed by this screen now) and the new centered-dialog classes (`items-center`, `rounded-2xl`) present in the compiled output. On-device visual confirmation pending the same working dev-client build as the rest of this pass — no native module touched, so Fast Refresh on the existing build should reflect it immediately.

### Follow-on: a "View map" toggle added to the mobile Home screen

Direct user ask: "I need the map in the landing page too, but make it have a button which says 'View map' and when pressed show all tabs like in the [web] landing page." `HomeScreen.tsx` had deliberately shipped with no map preview at all when it was first built (see above) — this closes that gap, but behind an explicit tap rather than rendering unconditionally.

- **`mobile/src/screens/auth/HomeScreen.tsx`** gained a `showMap` boolean state and a centered pill button (`map-outline` icon + "View map" / "Hide map", toggling on tap) placed after the role-icons row, before the welcome modal. When `showMap` is true, it renders the exact same shared `AreaSeverityMap` component (`mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`) already used by the victim/donor/volunteer "Severity Map" tab — which already renders the full 6-tab set (Areas Affected/River Levels/Reservoirs/GDACS/Earthquakes/Flood Risk) unconditionally, so no `extraLayers`-style prop was needed on mobile, unlike web.
- **Deliberately gated behind a tap, not rendered unconditionally** — `react-native-maps` is a genuinely heavy native view to mount on the very first screen a logged-out user sees; a user who never taps "View map" never pays that cost.
- New `landing.viewMap`/`landing.hideMap` i18n keys added to all three mobile locale files (mobile-only addition — web's landing page shows the map unconditionally, so it never needed a toggle label).
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway Metro instance (a different port from the user's real dev-client session, left untouched throughout) returned `200` with `"View map"`/`"Hide map"`/`map-outline` confirmed present in the compiled output. On-device confirmation pending the same standing mobile-testing limitation as the rest of this file — no native module was added here (reuses the already-integrated `react-native-maps`), so this doesn't need a fresh `eas build` on its own, only whatever build the user already needs for maps to work at all (see "Mobile maps" above).

### Follow-on: donation pickup-location control simplified to exactly two buttons

Direct, iterative user feedback on `DonorDonationForm.tsx` (web) — first that the mode-toggle tab and the actual capture button showed the same confusing text ("My current location" / "Capture my current location" looked identical), then, once that distinction was understood, that it should collapse to exactly two buttons rather than a tab-plus-action-button pattern at all: "lets just have two capture my location and choose a location."

- **Redesigned from a 2-tab-plus-1-action-button layout (3 controls) to exactly 2 buttons.** "Capture my location" now does both jobs at once — clicking it sets `locationMode("gps")` **and** immediately calls `captureLocation()` in the same handler, rather than requiring a second click on a separate button below. "Choose a location" (was "Choose a location on the map") just switches to map mode, unchanged. The button's own label now doubles as live status ("Capturing..." → "Location set ✓" → back to "Capture my current location" on a fresh mount), so no separate status button was needed underneath.
- **Wording simplified across all three locale files**: `chooseOnMap` "Choose a location on the map" → "Choose a location"; new `capturingLocation` key ("Capturing..."); `useCurrentLocationTab` (no longer needed once the top button does both jobs) left unreferenced in the JSX going forward.
- **The long explanatory hint paragraph removed entirely** ("Usually wherever you are now — but if this is coming from somewhere else... choose that location on the map instead") — the two button labels alone were judged clear enough without it.
- Verified live end-to-end (two separate throwaway donor accounts across the two passes of this fix): confirmed via `get_page_text` that the Pickup Location section renders as exactly two buttons with the final wording ("Capture my location" / "Choose a location"), no leftover third button, no hint paragraph. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and all three locale JSON files re-validated with `JSON.parse` after each pass.

### Mobile: a Messenger-style delivery chat feature (chat list + one-on-one thread)

Direct user ask: "also add a chat screen like in Facebook to the mobile app." The delivery-chat feature itself (mutual-consent contact reveal, lock-on-confirm, `deliveryChats`/`chatMessages` Firestore collections, the `/api/chats/*` routes) was already fully built server-side and live on web via `ChatModal.tsx` — see "Delivery chat + consent-gated contact sharing" above — but **mobile had zero chat implementation at all** (confirmed by grep before starting, not assumed). This closes that gap using the exact same backend, no server changes needed.

- **`mobile/src/screens/shared/ChatThreadScreen.tsx`** (new) — the mobile equivalent of web's `ChatModal.tsx`, as a full screen instead of a modal: `onSnapshot` on the `deliveryChats/{chatId}` doc (consent/lock state) and on a `chatMessages` query filtered by `chatId` (sorted by `createdAt` in JS, same composite-index-avoidance convention used everywhere else in this app), a `FlatList` of message bubbles (own messages right-aligned orange, the other party's left-aligned gray, auto-scrolls to the newest via `onContentSizeChange`), the same "Share my name & phone" mutual-consent button/waiting-state/revealed-contact banner sequence as web, and a composer that hides entirely once the chat is `locked`. Every action (`POST .../messages`, `PATCH .../consent`) calls the exact same Express routes web already uses.
- **`mobile/src/screens/shared/ChatListScreen.tsx`** (new) — a Messenger-style conversations list, live via two `onSnapshot` queries (`partyAId == me`, `partyBId == me`) merged and sorted client-side, mirroring exactly what `GET /api/chats/mine` does server-side (Firestore can't OR across two fields in one query) — chosen over a one-time fetch since this is a screen a user would naturally revisit the way they would an actual messaging app. Each row shows the other party's role (donor/volunteer/victim) and a lock icon once that chat is locked; tapping pushes into `ChatThreadScreen`.
- **`mobile/src/navigation/MessagesStack.tsx`** (new) — a small native-stack (`ChatList` → `ChatThread`), added as a 4th/5th "Messages" tab (chatbubble icon) to `VictimTabs`/`DonorTabs`/`VolunteerTabs` — **not** to `AdminTabs`, since the server's own `GET /api/chats/mine` route comment states this "isn't an admin surface" (admins aren't delivery-chat participants). Uses the same `headerShown: false` pattern already established by `AdminMoreStack` so the nested stack's own per-screen headers don't double up under the tab navigator's shared header.
- **A real typing gap fixed, not just a nice-to-have: `VictimTabParamList`/`DonorTabParamList`/`VolunteerTabParamList`'s `Messages` field had to be declared as `NavigatorScreenParams<MessagesStackParamList>`, not `undefined`.** Without this, `navigation.navigate("Messages", { screen: "ChatThread", params: { chatId } })` — needed so a per-item 💬 button on a *different* tab (e.g. `MyRequestsScreen`) can jump straight into a specific chat rather than just switching to an empty chat list — wouldn't type-check. This is the same nested-navigator pattern React Navigation documents for exactly this case.
- **Per-item 💬 entry points wired for parity with web**, reusing the exact same deterministic `${deliveryId}_${pairKey}` chat-id convention the server already uses: `MyRequestsScreen.tsx` (victim, one button per matched item, gated on the same `CHATTABLE_STATUSES` set web uses — `accepted`/`picked_up`/`delivered`/`confirmed`), `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` (volunteer, two buttons per delivery — "💬 Donor" and "💬 Victim", matching web's `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` exactly), `MyDonationsScreen.tsx` (donor, one button labeled by delivery method, matching web's `DonorMyDonations.tsx`). This is also what surfaced and got bundled with the separate "delivery details missing" fix above, since both needed the same `category`/`method` fields added to interfaces that had been missing them.
- **i18n scope matches this project's own established mobile boundary**: chat is reachable from `MyRequestsScreen.tsx` (victim, already translated), so the shared `chatModal`/`chatList` namespaces were translated into all three mobile locale files (copied verbatim from web's already-translated `chatModal` strings, not re-translated) — the same reasoning web used when it translated `ChatModal.tsx` for the same reason. The volunteer/donor screens' own per-item button labels stay plain English strings, consistent with those screens' existing English-only scope on mobile.
- **No new native module** — reuses `firebase/firestore` (already a dependency) and `@expo/vector-icons` (already used for tab icons) — so unlike the maps/push-notification passes, this feature works immediately on the user's already-installed dev-client build via Fast Refresh, no new `eas build` required.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway Metro instance (again, a separate port from the user's real session, confirmed unaffected before and after) returned `200` with `ChatThreadScreen`/`ChatListScreen`/`chatbubble`/`deliveryChats` all confirmed present in the compiled output via grep. On-device click-through (opening a real chat, sending a message, testing the consent flow) not verified from this session — same standing "can't test on-device from here" limitation already documented throughout this file for mobile; worth a real click-through once the user has a moment, since this is the first genuinely new mobile *feature* added since the last confirmed-working `eas build`, not just a JS-only screen tweak.

### Follow-on: a "Home" tile-grid landing screen added to all four mobile roles

Direct user ask: logging in as a victim on mobile dropped straight into the Submit Request form with no orientation — "not user friendly." The fix requested was specific: a landing page of four (or however many fit) rounded, light-colored square tiles, icon + label each, navigating into that role's other tabs — **and applied to all four roles**, not just victim.

- **`mobile/src/components/HomeTileGrid.tsx`** (new, shared) — takes `{title, subtitle?, tiles}` where each `HomeTile` is `{icon: keyof typeof Ionicons.glyphMap, label, color, iconColor, onPress}`. Renders a `flex-row flex-wrap justify-between` grid of `width: "48%", aspectRatio: 1` rounded-3xl tiles (icon centered above a label), matching the user's literal spec of "two boxes up, two boxes down" with light, distinct background tints per tile.
- **One new `*HomeScreen.tsx` per role**, each just supplying its own tile list to `HomeTileGrid`: `VictimHomeScreen.tsx` (5 tiles — Submit a Request, My Requests, Severity Map, Messages, Settings), `DonorHomeScreen.tsx` (5 — Donate, My Donations, Severity Map, Messages, Settings), `VolunteerHomeScreen.tsx` (4, a clean 2x2 — My Deliveries, Severity Map, Messages, Settings), `AdminHomeScreen.tsx` (5 — SOS Dispatch, Overview, Aid Requests, Donations, More). SOS Dispatch is deliberately the first/top-left tile on admin's screen, preserving the "most time-sensitive queue" priority `AdminTabs.tsx`'s own comment already established, even though Home is now the actual first tab in the bar.
- **`mobile/src/navigation/types.ts`** — every `*TabParamList` (Victim/Donor/Volunteer/Admin) gained `Home: undefined;` as its first field, with a comment explaining Home is now the initial tab for all four roles.
- **All four `*Tabs.tsx` files** gained `initialRouteName="Home"` and a `<Tab.Screen name="Home" .../>` inserted first (home-outline/home icon, matching the `tabIcon()` pattern already used for every other tab).
- **A deliberate, explicit exception to this project's own "mobile UX tops out around 5 visible tabs" soft guideline** (see "Mobile app" above, where the admin "More" stack was originally justified by exactly this ceiling) — Victim and Donor go from 5→6 tabs, Admin from 5→6 tabs, because the user explicitly and repeatedly asked for this Home screen "on all the four categories." Not a silent violation of the earlier guideline, a conscious user-driven override of it.
- **A real type error caught by `tsc`, not just theorized**: `VictimTabParamList`/`DonorTabParamList`/`VolunteerTabParamList`'s `Messages` field is `NavigatorScreenParams<MessagesStackParamList>` (needed for the delivery-chat feature's deep-linking, see above), not `undefined` — so the bare `navigation.navigate("Messages")` originally written in all three non-admin Home screens failed to compile. Fixed by calling `navigation.navigate("Messages", { screen: "ChatList" })` explicitly in all three, matching the same nested-navigator pattern already used elsewhere for jumping into a specific chat.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after the fix above; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway Metro instance on a scratch port (confirmed the user's real dev-client port was never touched, before and after) returned `200` (14.3MB) with `VictimHomeScreen`/`DonorHomeScreen`/`VolunteerHomeScreen`/`AdminHomeScreen`/`HomeTileGrid` and all the new tile labels ("Submit a Request", "My Requests", "Severity Map", "SOS Dispatch") confirmed present in the compiled output via grep. On-device click-through not verified from this session — same standing mobile-testing limitation documented throughout this file; no native module was touched here, so no fresh `eas build` is needed before the user can see it via Fast Refresh on their existing dev-client build.

### Follow-on: mobile notification history (a real "Notifications" tab), and Submit Request/My Requests dropped off the victim's tab bar

Direct trade the user proposed and confirmed: on the victim's tab bar, drop "Submit Request" and "My Requests" from the always-visible tabs (their reasoning: a victim won't be submitting new requests constantly once their initial need is registered, so those two don't need to permanently occupy tab slots) and add a real "Notifications" tab instead. Flagged one thing before building: there was no notification-history feature to point a tab at — push notifications only ever fired as a one-off OS push or a live foreground toast (see "Push notifications (FCM)" above), nothing was ever persisted for a user to browse back through. The user said to build it.

- **`server/src/utils/notifications.js`'s `sendNotificationToUser()` now always writes a `notifications` Firestore doc** (`{uid, title, body, data, read: false, createdAt}`) before attempting the FCM send, wrapped in its own try/catch (fails soft, same convention as the rest of this file) — and critically, this happens **regardless of whether the user has any FCM tokens registered or whether push succeeds**. This is what makes the in-app history the actual source of truth rather than a mirror of "whatever push happened to get through": a user with notifications permission denied, or simply not on the dev-client build yet, still sees their full history in-app. Because this is the one shared helper every existing trigger point already calls (request-approved, delivery picked-up/delivered, SOS submit/status-update, delivery-chat messages, water-level/reservoir area alerts — see "Push notifications (FCM)" above), **every one of them got an in-app history entry for free with this single change** — no per-call-site updates needed.
- **`server/src/routes/notifications.js`** (new, registered in `app.js`) — `GET /mine` (last 50, `where uid==` then sorted by `createdAt` in JS, same composite-index-avoidance convention as "Internal case notes"), `PATCH /:id/read` (ownership-checked — 403 if it's not your own notification), `PATCH /mark-all-read` (one Firestore batch write for "clear the unread badge," not N round trips).
- **New Firestore rule block for `notifications`** — read allowed only for the doc's own `uid` (no admin carve-out, unlike most other collections in this app — there's no legitimate reason for an admin to browse someone else's personal notification feed), all writes blocked at the rules level (creation and mark-read both go through the Express API / Admin SDK, never a direct client write) — same "load-bearing because the screen reads it live via `onSnapshot`" reasoning as `sosRequests`/`deliveryChats`. **Deployed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules`, confirmed with the user first) and verified directly against the live rule, not just assumed from a successful deploy: a real Firestore REST `runQuery` (the same wire call `onSnapshot` makes) as the notification's owner returned the doc (`200`); the identical query with an unrelated user's token returned `403 PERMISSION_DENIED`.
- **`mobile/src/screens/shared/NotificationsScreen.tsx`** (new) — live `onSnapshot` on `notifications` filtered to the caller's own `uid`, sorted newest-first in JS. Unread ones get an orange left-accent + dot; a "Mark all as read" link appears above the list only when there's something unread. Tapping a notification marks it read (`PATCH /:id/read`) — deliberately no deep-linking into the related screen (e.g. jumping straight to My Requests from a "request approved" notification) in this pass, to keep scope to what was actually asked; worth a follow-on if it's ever wanted, since the `data.type` field is already being stored per-notification specifically to support that later.
- **`mobile/src/navigation/VictimTabs.tsx`** — `SubmitRequest`/`MyRequests` stayed as real `<Tab.Screen>` entries in the **same** navigator (not moved to a different stack) so `VictimHomeScreen.tsx`'s existing `navigation.navigate("SubmitRequest")`/`navigation.navigate("MyRequests")` tile presses keep working completely unchanged — no typing changes, no restructuring. `Notifications` was added as a real, visible tab in their place (`notifications-outline`/`notifications` icon). Net effect: the victim's visible tab bar goes from 6 tabs (Home, Submit Request, My Requests, Severity Map, Messages, Settings) down to 5 (Home, Notifications, Severity Map, Messages, Settings) — incidentally bringing it back within this project's own "~5 tabs" soft guideline after the Home-tile-grid follow-on had pushed it to 6. **See the follow-on below for a real gap this left in the tab bar and its fix.**
- **Deliberately scoped to the victim role only for this pass** — the user's ask was specifically about the victim's tab bar. The backend (`sendNotificationToUser`, the new route, the rules) is fully generic and already logs history for every role (donor/volunteer/admin notifications get persisted too, from their own existing trigger points), so extending `NotificationsScreen` to the other three roles later is just a navigator-wiring change, not new backend work.
- Verified end-to-end via a real throwaway account and real HTTP calls (not just reading the code): fired two notifications through the real `sendNotificationToUser()` for an account with **zero FCM tokens registered**, confirmed both persisted and were readable via `GET /api/notifications/mine` (correct newest-first order, both `read: false`); marked one read via `PATCH /:id/read` and confirmed the correct one flipped; called `PATCH /mark-all-read` and confirmed the remaining one flipped too; confirmed a second, unrelated test account gets a 403 attempting to mark the first account's notification as read. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on mobile; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway instance on a scratch port (the user's real dev-client port confirmed untouched, before and after) returned `200` with `NotificationsScreen`, `mark-all-read`, and `notifications-outline` all confirmed present in the compiled output. On-device rendering of the live list is now unblocked (rules deployed and verified above) but still needs the user's own click-through to confirm visually, same standing limitation documented throughout this file for mobile. No native module was touched, so this should appear via Fast Refresh with no rebuild needed.

### Follow-on: volunteer-assignment notifications; a real tab-bar gap fixed; an unread badge dot; clarifying what does and doesn't trigger a notification

Three direct user questions plus two bug reports, in one message. Answering the questions first, since one surfaced a real gap:

- **"DMC warnings — will a victim get notified?" No, and this was never built.** DMC Emergency Warnings are a public RSS feed rendered as a list/map layer (see "External disaster data" above) — nothing pushes them to individual users as a notification. The `notifications` system only fires from specific in-app events (see below), never from an external feed. If DMC-warnings-as-push is ever wanted, that would need its own trigger (e.g. a poller comparing new RSS items against active-district-matched victims), not something already covered by this feature.
- **"Request approved → notification?" Yes, already built and unaffected by this pass** — `PATCH /:id/verify`/`bulk-verify` in `requests.js` (see "Push notifications (FCM)" above).
- **"Volunteer assigned a delivery → notification?" No — this was a genuine, previously-undetected gap, now fixed.** Checked all three places a volunteer gets attached to a delivery — `donations.js`'s auto-assign branch of `POST /:id/match`, and `deliveries.js`'s manual fresh-assign and reassign branches of `POST /` — and none of them called `sendNotificationToUser` for the volunteer at all; only the victim-facing trigger points existed. Added a `sendNotificationToUser(volunteerId, {title: "New delivery assignment", body: "You've been assigned a {category} delivery — accept or reject it from My Deliveries.", data: {type: "delivery_assigned", deliveryId}})` call to all three spots. FCM-only, deliberately no matching SMS — same "reserve SMS for the handful of already-wired higher-value trigger points" reasoning already established for delivery-chat messages (see "Delivery chat" above), not extended to every new notification type by default.
- **Real bug: a visible gap in the victim's tab bar between Home and Notifications.** Root cause confirmed by the user's own device, not something visible from `tsc`/a compiled-bundle check: `tabBarButton: () => null` only replaces the tab's inner touchable content — the OUTER per-tab wrapper (governed by a separate `tabBarItemStyle` option) still laid out at its normal flex width in this @react-navigation/bottom-tabs version, leaving a blank but fully-sized slot for each of the two hidden tabs (`SubmitRequest`, `MyRequests`). Fixed by also setting `tabBarItemStyle: { display: "none" }` on both — `display: "none"` is a real RN layout value that removes an element from measurement entirely, unlike the button-only override, so the wrapper itself now collapses to zero width rather than just rendering emptily.
- **New unread-notification badge dot on the tab icon** — `mobile/src/hooks/useUnreadNotificationCount.ts` (new), a live `onSnapshot` on `notifications` filtered to `uid == me && read == false`, returning just the count. Lives outside `NotificationsScreen.tsx` specifically so the badge reflects reality even while that screen isn't mounted (e.g. sitting on Home when a new notification arrives). `VictimTabs.tsx`'s Notifications tab icon is now an inline component (rather than the shared `tabIcon()` helper, which can't call hooks) rendering the Ionicons glyph plus a small absolutely-positioned red dot (a plain colored `View`, not React Navigation's numeric `tabBarBadge` — the user asked for "a red dot," not a count) when `unreadCount > 0`.
- Verified as far as this environment allows: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on mobile; `node --check` clean on all three edited backend files; the backend restarted cleanly under nodemon with no errors after each change; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway instance on a scratch port (user's real session confirmed untouched) returned `200` with `useUnreadNotificationCount` and the `display:"none"` tab-item style both confirmed present in the compiled output. **Not verified: the actual visual fix for the tab-bar gap, or the badge dot rendering, on a real device** — both are layout/rendering claims this session cannot see directly; worth the user's own quick look to confirm the gap is actually gone and the dot actually appears after a real notification arrives.

### Follow-on: mobile map marker Callouts fixed — Android was clipping/garbling the tap-for-details popup

User-reported: "when i press a dot in the map it doesnt show the details properly." Root cause is a well-documented `react-native-maps` Android quirk, not an app-logic bug — every marker `<Callout>` across both mobile map surfaces (`mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`'s 6 marker types, `mobile/src/screens/admin/SituationMapScreen.tsx`'s 8) wrapped its content in a `<View style={{ padding: 4, minWidth: 140|160 }}>`. Android's native Callout implementation renders the JS content into a fixed-size bitmap snapshot for performance, and that snapshot's dimensions are taken from the layout pass *before* flexible sizing (`minWidth`, wrapped or conditionally-rendered `<Text>` lines like a reservoir's optional "→ district" line) has settled — so the popup showed clipped, overlapping, or truncated text instead of the real detail lines.

- **Fixed by replacing every `minWidth` with an explicit `width`** (140→180, 160→200, picked to comfortably fit the longest real content — e.g. "Priority 85 · verified" or a full reservoir name+district+capacity block — without the previous clipping) across all 14 Callout blocks in both files. A fixed `width` gives Android's snapshot pass a stable, correctly-measured size on the first attempt, which is the standard, documented workaround for this exact `react-native-maps` behavior.
- Added a short comment above the first Callout in each file explaining why `width` and not `minWidth` — a non-obvious Android-specific gotcha future edits to these marker popups should keep in mind, since it's easy to reach for `minWidth` again out of habit (that's what caused this in the first place).
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on mobile; grepped the whole `mobile/src` tree afterward and confirmed zero remaining `minWidth` usages; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway instance on a scratch port (user's real dev-client session confirmed untouched, before and after) returned `200` with exactly 7 `width: 180` and 7 `width: 200` occurrences in the compiled output — matching the 14 Callout blocks 1:1, confirming every one of them was actually updated, not just a subset. **Not verified: the actual on-device fix** — this is a rendering claim about Android's native Callout snapshotting behavior that this session cannot see directly; worth the user's own tap-a-marker check to confirm the popup text now displays cleanly.

### Follow-on: the `width` fix above wasn't enough — Callout replaced entirely with a custom detail card; a second, real coordinate-rendering bug fixed too

The user re-tested and reported the River Levels (gauges) tab's popup still didn't show details properly — the `width` fix reduced but didn't eliminate the underlying Android Callout snapshot-timing bug (gauges' Callout has 4 text lines, the most of any marker type here, and even a fixed *width* doesn't stabilize a snapshot whose *height* still depends on how many lines of variable-length text actually render). Rather than keep patching around Android's native Callout rendering, replaced it entirely.

- **Every `<Callout>` in both files (`mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`, `mobile/src/screens/admin/SituationMapScreen.tsx` — 6 + 8 = 14 marker types) removed outright.** Each `<Marker>` now takes an `onPress` that sets a `{title, lines}` object into a `selected` state, and a small custom `View` — positioned absolutely at the bottom of the map container, styled as a plain white card with a shadow and a ✕ close button — renders whatever's selected. This is plain React Native UI rendered by JS normally, not something react-native-maps' native layer snapshots, so it completely sidesteps the Android bug rather than trying to satisfy its undocumented sizing requirements. `selected` resets to `null` on every tab switch, so a stale card from a different layer can't linger.
- **A second, independent, real bug found and fixed in the same pass: none of the 14 `<Marker>`s set an `anchor` prop.** The user separately reported "the dot goes out of the country too, place the dots correctly" — checked first whether this was a *data* problem (queried `/api/stats/by-area`, `/api/external/water-levels`, `/api/external/reservoirs`, and `/api/external/flood-risk` directly and confirmed every single coordinate returned by the live backend already falls within Sri Lanka's real bounding box — not a bad-data issue) and whether it was a lat/lng swap in the JSX (grepped every `coordinate={{...}}` in both files — all consistently `{latitude: X.lat, longitude: X.lng}`, no swap). The actual cause: react-native-maps defaults a custom-child Marker's anchor to `{x: 0.5, y: 1}` — bottom-center, correct for a pin-shaped icon whose tip should touch the coordinate, but wrong for a plain round `ColorDot`, whose true center then renders *above* its real coordinate by roughly its own height. Near a coastline or border, that vertical offset was enough to make a correctly-placed dot visually drift outside the country's landmass. Fixed by adding `anchor={{ x: 0.5, y: 0.5 }}` to all 14 markers, centering the dot exactly on its coordinate.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled via a throwaway instance on a scratch port (user's real session confirmed untouched, before and after) — a plain string search for `anchor` initially came back empty because Metro pretty-prints the object literal across multiple lines rather than on one; a proper multi-line-aware search confirmed all 14 `anchor: {x: 0.5, y: 0.5}` occurrences are present, one per marker. **Not verified: the actual on-device fix for either bug** — both are rendering/positioning claims about native map behavior this session cannot see directly; worth the user's own tap-a-marker check (details should now show cleanly in the bottom card) and a look at whether coastal/border dots now sit correctly on the landmass.

### Follow-on: screen-reader accessibility pass — mobile only, by explicit user direction

The user asked "what can we do for disabled people" and confirmed starting with screen-reader labels. This was first built on **web** (verifiable directly via the browser's own accessibility tree — `read_page`/DOM inspection literally shows what a screen reader announces), then the user redirected mid-build: **"i need this disability update to my mobile version only."** The web fixes already made (5 modal components gained `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal`/`aria-labelledby`/a real focus-trap-and-restore hook, plus `aria-live` regions on the emergency/SOS-status banners and the notification toast, `aria-pressed`/`aria-expanded` on toggle controls) were **left in place, not reverted** — they're real, verified bug fixes with no downside — but every fix after the redirect targeted mobile only, and that's the platform this section covers.

- **The single biggest, highest-stakes gap, found before touching anything else: `mobile/src/components/SosButton.tsx`'s draggable floating button was a plain `Animated.View` with `PanResponder` handlers and zero accessibility properties.** A `View` with gesture handlers registers no accessibility node at all by default — without `accessible`, TalkBack/VoiceOver very likely couldn't even discover the single most safety-critical control in the entire app as tappable. Fixed with `accessible accessibilityRole="button" accessibilityLabel="Send SOS emergency alert"` on the wrapping `Animated.View`, plus the inner `🆘 SOS` text hidden from the accessibility tree (`accessibilityElementsHidden` + `importantForAccessibility="no-hide-descendants"`) so TalkBack doesn't announce both the label and the raw text redundantly. **Honestly disclosed, not glossed over**: free-form dragging still isn't screen-reader-operable (an inherent limitation of gesture handlers generally, not fixable from this component) — but TalkBack/VoiceOver's own focus-then-double-tap activation synthesizes a near-zero-distance touch, which the existing tap-vs-drag distance threshold already treats as a tap, so *activating* the button still works even though *dragging* it doesn't.
- **`AccessibilityInfo.announceForAccessibility()` is this app's cross-platform stand-in for web's `aria-live`** — React Native has no single declarative prop that reaches both iOS and Android (`accessibilityLiveRegion` is Android-only), so every genuinely dynamic, must-be-heard-without-looking update got an explicit imperative announcement call: `SosButton.tsx` (location captured/error, SOS sent), `SosStatusBanner.tsx` (every real status change — this is literally "the one place in the app where 'live' matters more than anywhere else" per its own existing file comment, and had zero accessibility markup before this), and the shared map detail card in both `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` and `SituationMapScreen.tsx` (announces the tapped marker's full detail text, since the card is a plain `View`, not a `Modal`, so nothing else would tell a screen reader it appeared). `accessibilityLiveRegion="polite"` was added alongside each as an Android-native belt-and-suspenders layer, not a replacement for the explicit call.
- **All 4 mobile `<Modal>` usages** (`SosButton.tsx`, `QrScanModal.tsx`, `HomeScreen.tsx`'s welcome modal, `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`'s reject-reason modal) gained `accessibilityViewIsModal` on their outer content `View` (the iOS signal to scope VoiceOver's swipe navigation to just the modal). **A real, easy-to-miss gap caught in `HomeScreen.tsx`'s welcome modal specifically**: the full-screen tap-outside-to-dismiss backdrop `TouchableOpacity` had no label at all — a screen-reader user swiping through elements could land on it and double-tap-dismiss the dialog by accident. Fixed by excluding it from the accessibility tree entirely (`importantForAccessibility="no"` + `accessibilityElementsHidden`) rather than labeling it, since the explicit ✕ button and the OS back-gesture are already the real accessible way to close it — the tap-outside affordance is a sighted/touch-only convenience with no accessible equivalent needed.
- **`accessibilityState`/`accessibilityRole` added everywhere a selection is currently shown by color alone**: the SOS type-picker (`accessibilityRole="radiogroup"`/`"radio"` + `accessibilityState={{selected, checked}}`), and — reused across every one of this app's 6+ map layers in both map files — the view-mode tab row (`accessibilityRole="tablist"`/`"tab"` + `accessibilityState={{selected}}`), fixing the same "which one is currently active" gap on the primary navigation control for the whole map component, not just one screen.
- **`TextInput`s across `LoginScreen.tsx`, `RegisterScreen.tsx`, `SosButton.tsx`, and `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`'s reject modal all gained `accessibilityLabel`** — a real, easy-to-overlook RN-specific gap: unlike HTML's `<label for>`, React Native has **no native mechanism at all** associating a visible `<Text>` label with a nearby `TextInput`, even when one is rendered directly above it. Every field in this app that looked correctly labeled on screen (email, password, name, phone, NIC, home address, SOS people-count/description, delivery-reject reason) was actually an unnamed text field to a screen reader before this — confirmed by checking RN's own accessibility model, not assumed.
- **The tab-bar unread-notification dot** (`VictimTabs.tsx`) is deliberately still a plain colored dot, not React Navigation's numeric `tabBarBadge` — the user specifically asked for a dot, not a count, in an earlier pass, and that stands. The screen-reader gap this leaves (a color-only cue the tab-bar icon itself can't voice) is closed a different way: `tabBarAccessibilityLabel` (a separate React Navigation option from what `tabBarIcon` renders) is now set dynamically to `"Notifications, N unread"` when `unreadCount > 0`, so the *announced* name carries the count even though the *visual* badge stays a plain dot exactly as asked. `NotificationsScreen.tsx`'s own per-row read/unread state (also color-only before this) got the same treatment via each row's `accessibilityLabel` explicitly stating "Unread." before the title when applicable, with the small colored dot itself hidden from the tree to avoid a redundant, unlabeled node.
- **Honest, disclosed gap, not attempted in this pass**: individual `<Marker>`s on the interactive map (both files, 14 marker types total) still have no per-marker `accessibilityLabel` — discovering and activating one specific marker among dozens via TalkBack/VoiceOver's touch-exploration on a live embedded Google Map is a well-known, industry-wide hard accessibility problem (even native Google Maps has limited marker-level screen-reader support), and doing it properly across 14 near-identical marker blocks in two files was judged lower-value for this pass than the surrounding controls (tabs, legend, detail card) that were fixed — the detail card's new announce-on-select behavior means a sighted-assisted or trial-and-tap approach at least surfaces full information once a marker happens to be hit. Worth a dedicated follow-on if this ever needs to go further.
- **Not yet covered in this pass, scoped out for time, not overlooked**: the remaining donor/volunteer core screens' buttons (`RegisterDonationScreen.tsx`, `MyDonationsScreen.tsx`, most of `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` outside its reject modal), `ChatListScreen.tsx`/`ChatThreadScreen.tsx`, and all 14 admin screens — none of these got a pass here. The highest-stakes, highest-traffic surfaces (the SOS button and its live status banner, every modal dialog in the app, both login/register forms, the shared map component used by every role, and notification history) were prioritized first; the rest is a reasonable, scoped follow-on rather than a gap that was missed by oversight.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across every edited file. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8091`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward — the user's real dev-client session, confirmed not running on the default port at the time, was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB), with representative new strings/props confirmed present via grep on the compiled output: `"Send SOS emergency alert"` (1), `announceForAccessibility` (14 call sites), `accessibilityViewIsModal` (9), `accessibilityLiveRegion` (21), `"Close details"` (2), `accessibilityRole` (33 occurrences), `tabBarAccessibilityLabel` (3) — the same "prove it bundles, not just type-checks" discipline used throughout this file for mobile. **On-device verification with a real screen reader (TalkBack/VoiceOver) was not done from this session** — this is a rendering/interaction claim about native accessibility-service behavior this session cannot observe directly, the same standing limitation already documented throughout this file for mobile; worth the user's own TalkBack pass over the SOS button and its modal specifically, since that's the highest-stakes single fix in this pass. No native module was touched, so this should appear via Fast Refresh on the existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed.

### Follow-on: Settings redesigned as an accordion; Volunteer's My Deliveries color-coded and stripped down; Donor/Volunteer/Admin bottom nav bars restructured; a real duplicate-status-badge bug fixed; Admin's "More" tab dissolved into Home tiles with sort options and live badge counts

Direct, multi-part user ask, mobile-only (per the earlier "I need this disability update to my mobile version only" scope narrowing, which by extension has kept every UI request since scoped to mobile — web was not touched in this pass). Covers Settings (all roles), Volunteer, Donor, and Admin.

- **`SettingsScreen.tsx` rebuilt as a collapsible-accordion layout**, replacing one long flat scroll of Profile/Language/Notifications/Account all visible at once (the user's own complaint: "feels like everything is together; too much info to process"). A small local `SettingsSection` component (icon + title + a one-line collapsed-state subtitle + chevron) renders each section closed by default except Profile, which opens first since it's the section most people arrive to edit. Sections: **Profile**, a new **volunteer-only "Availability" section** (`isVolunteer = profile?.role === "volunteer"`), **Language**, **Notifications**, **Account**.
  - **The volunteer's "Set my location"/"Available for new deliveries" pills moved here verbatim from `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx`** — direct user ask ("location set update and available for now shouldnt be in the my delvery page may be move it to the settings page"). All of the state (`available`, `savingAvailability`, `myLocation`, `locationStatus`) and both handler functions (`toggleAvailability()`, `updateMyLocation()`) moved with it; the `GET /api/users/me` fetch that seeds `available`/`myLocation` is now gated behind `isVolunteer` so donor/victim/admin accounts don't make a pointless extra call.
  - New locale key `volunteerDeliveries.availabilitySection` = "Availability" (all 3 languages) for the new section's header — reuses the existing `volunteerDeliveries.*` namespace rather than minting a new one, since every string inside the section (the pill labels, location status text) was already translated there.
- **`VolunteerCommunityReportScreen.tsx`** (new file) — the road-closure/water-condition report form extracted verbatim out of `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` into its own screen, matching the user's explicit spec: "the report a road closure form should be in the home tab as a separate box (just like mydeliveries, messages and so on)." Added as a new tile ("Report Condition", `alert-circle-outline`, red) on `VolunteerHomeScreen.tsx`, right after "My Deliveries."
- **`MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` rewritten**, now purely the delivery list — no availability/location UI, no community-report form, no `Picker` import. Direct user ask: "add colour to each delivery record box." Added a `STATUS_ACCENT` lookup (border/background tint + a 5px solid left accent bar, one color per delivery status — amber/pending_acceptance, blue/accepted, red/rejected, purple/picked_up, green/delivered, gray/confirmed) reusing `StatusBadge.tsx`'s existing color vocabulary so a delivery's card color always matches its own status badge, not a separate ad-hoc scheme. Each card is now `<View className="flex-row overflow-hidden rounded-xl border ...">` wrapping a `<View style={{width:5, backgroundColor: accent.bar}} />` accent stripe plus the original content — the reject-reason `Modal` at the bottom is unchanged.
- **`VolunteerTabs.tsx`** — "My Deliveries" removed from the visible tab bar and replaced with **Notifications** (direct user ask: "instead of my delveries replace it with notifications in the bottom nav bar"). Both "My Deliveries" and the new "Report Condition" screen are now hidden tabs (`tabBarButton: () => null` + `tabBarItemStyle: {display:"none"}` — button-only alone leaves a visible blank gap, a bug already hit and fixed once for `VictimTabs.tsx`, see the note already in this file), reachable only via their Home tiles. Notifications uses the same `useUnreadNotificationCount()` badge-dot + `tabBarAccessibilityLabel` pattern already proven on `VictimTabs.tsx`. New tab order: Home, MyDeliveries(hidden), CommunityReport(hidden), **Notifications**, SeverityMap, Messages, Settings.
- **`DonorTabs.tsx`** — same treatment: "Donate" and "My Donations" removed from the tab bar (direct user ask: "remove donate and my delveries from the bottom nav bar and replace it with notofications"), both converted to hidden tabs reachable via `DonorHomeScreen.tsx`'s existing tiles, Notifications added as a real visible tab with the identical badge pattern. New tab order: Home, RegisterDonation(hidden), MyDonations(hidden), **Notifications**, SeverityMap, Messages, Settings.
- **A real, user-reported bug fixed in `MyDonationsScreen.tsx`: two "Delivered" badges rendering on one donation card at once.** Root cause: the card rendered `<StatusBadge status={d.status}/>` (the donation's own coarse status — available/matched/delivered) *and*, separately, a second `<StatusBadge status={d.deliveryStatus}/>` beneath the action buttons whenever `d.deliveryStatus` existed and wasn't `"accepted"` — but once a volunteer-or-self delivery actually completes, `donations.status` gets synced to `"delivered"` too, so both badges can independently read "Delivered" at the same moment, exactly what the user pasted a screenshot of. Fixed to render **one** badge, preferring the more specific `d.deliveryStatus` when a linked delivery exists (`<StatusBadge status={d.deliveryStatus || d.status} />`), and removed the second, now-redundant badge render entirely.
- **Admin: the "More" tab (a native-stack menu listing 7 secondary screens) dissolved entirely** — direct user ask: "dont have the more tab list all the features in it in the home tab just like sos dispatch, overview and others." `AdminMoreStack.tsx` and `MoreScreen.tsx` were deleted outright (confirmed via grep that nothing else referenced either afterward). `AdminTabParamList` (in `types.ts`) flattened from `{Home, SosDispatch, Notifications, SituationMap, Settings, More}` + a separate `AdminMoreStackParamList` into one flat type listing all 15 screens directly. `AdminHomeScreen.tsx` gained 7 new tiles (Categories, Broadcast, Active Emergencies, Water Alerts, Community Reports, Audit Log, Volunteer Workload) alongside the 4 it already had, and the "More" tile was removed.
- **Admin's bottom tab bar restructured** — direct user ask: "in the bottom nav bar remove overview, aid req, donations and instead add notification, situation map, and settings." `AdminTabs.tsx` rewritten: `Overview`/`AidRequests`/`Donations` converted to hidden tabs (reachable only via their Home tiles, same pattern as every other role's secondary screens); `Notifications` (with the same badge-dot pattern), `SituationMap`, and `Settings` promoted to real visible tabs alongside the pre-existing `Home`/`SosDispatch`. The other 7 previously-"More"-only screens (Categories/Broadcast/ActiveDistricts/WaterAlerts/CommunityReports/AuditLog/VolunteerWorkload) are also now hidden tabs directly on `AdminTabs.tsx`, each screen component imported straight from `screens/admin/*` rather than through the deleted stack.
- **Admin's notification-loop closed** — direct user ask: "have it becoz suppose an active emergy is broadcasted the admin alos receives the notification in the notification tab so he knows that the notification is sent succesfully." `server/src/routes/activeDistricts.js`'s `notifyDistrictActivated(district, activatedByUid)` now takes a second parameter and, after notifying every matched victim, also sends the activating admin their own confirmation notification ("Emergency broadcast sent" / "Active emergency declared in {district} — N victim(s) notified.", `data.type: "district_activation_confirmed"`). Both call sites — the direct `POST /api/active-districts` route and `communityReports.js`'s district-activation-on-verify path — now pass `req.user.uid` through. This reuses the existing `sendNotificationToUser()` helper, which already always persists to the `notifications` Firestore collection regardless of FCM token state (see "Push notifications (FCM)" above) — so the admin sees this confirmation in their own new Notifications tab even with push permission denied or no token registered, which is exactly the "so he knows that the notification is sent successfully" ask.
- **Live red-dot badge counts on Home tiles** — direct user ask: "if there new aid requests just add a red circle in the home page aid request box (just like in notifications) do this to every tab that gets new addons." `AdminHomeScreen.tsx` gained 4 live `onSnapshot` count queries (pending `aidRequests`, available `donations`, pending `sosRequests`, unverified `communityReports` — each a filtered `where()` count query, not a full document fetch, since only the count is needed) and passes `badge: count > 0` to the SOS Dispatch, Aid Requests, Donations, and Community Reports tiles using `HomeTileGrid.tsx`'s already-existing `badge?: boolean` prop (added in the prior accessibility-adjacent pass — a small red dot on the tile icon, hidden from the accessibility tree, with a visible `" •"` suffix appended to the tile's label text as the non-visual equivalent).
- **Sort-by options added to both `AidRequestsScreen.tsx` and `DonationsScreen.tsx`** — direct user ask on both: "add sort by options. do the same to donations as well." Both screens now keep their live `onSnapshot` data unsorted in state and apply a `useMemo`'d sort based on a new `sortBy` UI (a row of pill buttons matching this project's established pill-toggle visual language). `AidRequestsScreen.tsx`: Priority (default, unchanged from before) / Newest / Status (via a small `STATUS_ORDER` map: pending → verified → in_progress → delivered → rejected). `DonationsScreen.tsx` (previously had no sorting at all, rendering in Firestore's arbitrary snapshot order): Newest (new default) / Status (available → matched → delivered) / Category (alphabetical).
- **`home.reportCondition` given real Sinhala/Tamil translations** ("තත්ත්වයක් වාර්තා කරන්න" / "நிலைமையை புகாரளிக்கவும்") in all three locale files, replacing the inline English-only `t("home.reportCondition", "Report Condition")` fallback from when the tile was first added — closing a small gap flagged in this same session, consistent with this project's "every key gets all 3 languages" discipline.
