A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8092`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward via its actual process tree — the user's real dev-client session on port 8081, confirmed running under the same PID before and after, was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB), with the new features confirmed present via grep on the compiled output: `"Sort by"` (2 occurrences — one per screen), `AdminHomeScreen`/`SituationMapScreen` both present, `sosRequests`/`communityReports` query strings present (3 and 13 occurrences respectively, confirming the new admin badge-count listeners compiled in), `reportCondition` resolved 4 times (en/si/ta translations + the one usage site), and zero remaining references to the deleted `AdminMoreStack`/`MoreScreen` anywhere in the bundle. **On-device click-through (accordion expand/collapse, delivery-card colors, the new tab bars, badge dots actually appearing/disappearing live, sort-button behavior) was not done from this session** — same standing "can't test on a real device from here" limitation documented throughout this file for mobile; no native module was touched in this pass, so every change here should appear immediately via Fast Refresh on the user's existing dev-client build with no new `eas build` required.

### Follow-on: a real on-device bug from the pass above, found immediately by the user — `communityReports` badge-count listener threw `permission-denied`

Confirms the "not verified on-device" caveat immediately above was the right caveat to make — the very first real-device run surfaced `FirebaseError: [code=permission-denied]: Missing or insufficient permissions.` from a Firestore snapshot listener.

- **Root cause**: `AdminHomeScreen.tsx`'s new live badge-count `useEffect` subscribed via `onSnapshot` to four collections — `aidRequests`, `donations`, `sosRequests` (all three already have a Firestore rule allowing `userRole() == "admin"` to read, confirmed by re-reading `firestore-rules/firestore.rules` directly) — and `communityReports`, which has **no rule block at all**, by original design: see "Community reports" above, which states plainly "No new Firestore rule needed — nothing here is read directly by a client via the SDK," since `AdminCommunityReports.tsx` on web has always used a one-time `apiFetch` through the Express API instead of `onSnapshot`. Adding a live `onSnapshot` on `communityReports` in this pass broke that established boundary without a matching rules change — Firestore's default for a collection with no matching rule is deny, so the query failed immediately.
- **Fixed by not adding a rule, but by respecting the existing design**: `AdminHomeScreen.tsx`'s `communityReports` count now comes from a one-time `apiFetch("/api/community-reports")` (the existing admin-only Express route) filtered client-side to `status === "unverified"`, in its own separate `useEffect` — the other three collections' live `onSnapshot` listeners are untouched. This means the Community Reports tile's badge isn't truly live (only refreshes on screen mount), matching the same "lower urgency, doesn't need to be live" reasoning `AdminCommunityReports.tsx` itself already uses — a deliberate, documented tradeoff, not an oversight, and one that avoids touching Firestore security rules for a tile badge.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8093`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward via its process tree — the user's real dev-client session on port 8081, confirmed running under the same PID before and after, was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB), with `api/community-reports` confirmed present in the compiled output (4 occurrences) and zero remaining `collection(db, "communityReports")` references anywhere in the bundle. **On-device confirmation that the error is actually gone was not re-done from this session** — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile; worth the user's own quick check that the error no longer appears and the Community Reports tile's badge still shows correctly.

### Follow-on: two more real on-device bugs, both found by the user — SOS status banner colliding with the status bar, and the admin Situation Map's tab pills rendering "elongated"

Two separate reports in one message, both genuine on-device rendering bugs this session couldn't have caught from a compiled-bundle check alone (neither is a compile error) — asked one clarifying question for the Situation Map report specifically, since "acting weird" alone could have meant several different things (crash, tabs not switching, layout) and guessing wrong would have wasted a pass; the user confirmed it was specifically the tab pills looking stretched/elongated.

- **Root cause #1, the "something on top, out of place" element: `App.tsx` never wrapped the tree in a `SafeAreaProvider`, and `SosStatusBanner.tsx` renders as a plain `View` above even `NavigationContainer`** (see `RootNavigator.tsx` — it's the very first child in the root `<View style={{flex:1}}>`, rendered before the navigator that would otherwise give every screen's own header automatic safe-area handling). With no top inset applied anywhere, the banner painted flush at y=0 — literally overlapping the phone's status bar/notch — which is exactly what "something on top (out of place)" describes. This had nothing to do with the SOS feature's own logic (the "Your SOS: Acknowledged by an admin" text itself was correct); it was purely a missing safe-area fix that had never been exercised on-device before, since this banner only ever appears for an account with an actual unresolved SOS report.
- **Fix**: added `SafeAreaProvider` (from `react-native-safe-area-context`, already a dependency — pulled in transitively by React Navigation itself, so no new native module, no `eas build` needed) around the whole app in `App.tsx`. `SosStatusBanner.tsx` now calls `useSafeAreaInsets()` and sets `paddingTop: insets.top + 12` explicitly (replacing the Tailwind `py-3` class, which gave a flat 12px regardless of device) — the banner now sits below the status bar/notch on any device, notched or not. `NotificationSetup.tsx`'s bottom-anchored toasts were left untouched — the report was specifically about something at the top, and those already have generous fixed bottom offsets (150px/90px) that already clear the home-indicator area on typical devices.
- **Root cause #2, the "elongated tabs" on the admin Situation Map: a horizontal `ScrollView` with a separately-styled `flex-row` `View` nested inside it as its sole child** (`SituationMapScreen.tsx`'s 7-tab pill row — Requests/Areas Affected/River Gauges/Reservoirs/Global Alerts/Tsunami Risk/Flood Risk). A horizontal `ScrollView` already forces its own content container into row direction internally; layering a second, independently-styled `flex-row` View inside that container let React Native's default cross-axis stretch behavior kick in on-device, stretching each `TouchableOpacity` pill into a full-width bar instead of a compact chip sized to its own label. This is a real, previously-unverified bug, not a regression from the admin-nav restructuring earlier in this session — `SituationMapScreen.tsx`'s tab row has looked like this since the "mobile map layers brought to full parity" pass, and per that section's own verification notes was only ever proven to *compile*, never checked on a real device; it simply hadn't been reached/reported until now that Situation Map is a directly-tappable tab instead of buried in the deleted "More" menu.
- **Fix**: moved the row layout directly onto the `ScrollView`'s own `contentContainerStyle` (`{flexDirection: "row", alignItems: "center", gap: 8}`) instead of a nested `View`, and dropped the now-unnecessary wrapping `View` entirely (`accessibilityRole="tablist"` moved onto the `ScrollView` itself). Added `self-start` to each `TouchableOpacity` as a defensive second layer, so no future cross-axis-stretch regression can silently reintroduce the same bug. Confirmed via grep that this exact `ScrollView horizontal` + nested `flex-row` pattern doesn't exist anywhere else in `mobile/src` — this was the only instance, so no other screen needs the same fix.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both edited files. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8094`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward via its process tree — the user's real dev-client session on port 8081, confirmed running under the same PID before and after, was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB), with `SafeAreaProvider` (37 occurrences — it's a common dependency name across the bundle, not a precise count of this one usage, but confirms the import/component resolved and bundled), `useSafeAreaInsets` (12 occurrences), and the new `contentContainerStyle` row-layout object all confirmed present in the compiled output. **On-device visual confirmation of either fix was not done from this session** — same standing "can't test on a real device from here" limitation documented throughout this file for mobile; no native module was touched by either fix, so both should appear via Fast Refresh on the user's existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed. Worth the user's own look to confirm the SOS banner now clears the status bar and the Situation Map's tab pills are compact again.

### Follow-on: the SOS banner/tab fixes above didn't fully close it out — a real screenshot from the user surfaced a second layout bug on the Situation Map, plus a persisting marker-anchor problem across every mobile map

The user sent an actual on-device screenshot (the tab pills had shrunk back to compact, per the fix above — confirmed visually correct) but pointed out a large, unexplained blank gap between the now-compact tab row and the legend below it, and separately reported that map marker dots are still misplaced "across all roles," not just admin. Asked one clarifying question first — "what does 'acting weird' look like" wasn't specific enough the first time, so this time the follow-up narrowed straight to the two concrete symptoms in the screenshot rather than guessing again.

- **The blank-gap bug**: the tab row's `ScrollView` (fixed for the elongated-pills bug in the immediately preceding pass) was left to auto-size its own outer height rather than being given an explicit one. The working theory, consistent with what the screenshot actually shows: before the pills-stretching fix, the ScrollView's real (larger) height was hidden behind the stretched pills filling it; once `alignItems: "center"` stopped the pills from stretching, that same leftover height became visible as dead space above the legend. **Fixed** by giving the `ScrollView` an explicit `style={{ height: 40 }}` (sized to comfortably fit one row of `py-1.5` pills) instead of leaving it to auto-size — this removes the ambiguity entirely regardless of the exact underlying Android sizing quirk, rather than continuing to chase the root cause through a black box this session can't directly inspect.
- **The marker-anchor bug, still present after the earlier `anchor={{x:0.5,y:0.5}}` fix**: re-verified first (grep, not assumption) that the earlier fix was in fact fully applied — every real `<Marker>` in both `SituationMapScreen.tsx` (8 markers) and `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (6 markers) already had `anchor={{x:0.5,y:0.5}}`, confirmed 1:1 by count. Since the user still saw misplaced dots on every role's map despite that, the real cause is a separate, well-documented `react-native-maps` **Android** limitation: `anchor` isn't reliably honored for a **custom-View marker** (as opposed to an `image`-based one) unless the marker is also marked **`flat`** — without `flat`, Android treats a custom-view marker as a 3D pin (which tilts/rotates with the camera and derives its on-screen offset from that pin behavior), and the declared `anchor` point isn't computed against the marker's true flat position the way it is on iOS.
- **Fixed** by adding `flat` to all 14 `<Marker>`s across both files (8 in `SituationMapScreen.tsx`, 6 in `AreaSeverityMap.tsx`) via a small Node script matching only the real standalone `anchor={{ x: 0.5, y: 0.5 }}` prop lines (not the doc-comment line mentioning the same string, distinguished by the comment line having surrounding prose/backticks the real prop line doesn't) — confirmed a clean 1:1 `<Marker` count vs. inserted `flat` count in both files afterward. `flat` is also the semantically correct choice regardless of whether it fully resolves the bug: a colored dot representing a fixed location was never meant to tilt/rotate with the map camera the way a 3D pin does.
- **Honestly caveated, not overclaimed**: this is the standard, most-cited fix for this class of `react-native-maps` Android issue, and it's now applied everywhere the earlier `anchor` fix was — but since the earlier `anchor`-only fix was also reported (twice, in this file) as "should work" before the user's own device proved otherwise, this pass does **not** claim to have fully verified it's actually resolved on a real device. Worth the user's own look at more than one map layer (not just Requests) before treating marker placement as fully fixed.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both edited files. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8095`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward via its process tree — the user's real dev-client session on port 8081, confirmed running under the same PID before and after, was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB), with `flat: true` confirmed present exactly 14 times in the compiled output (matching the 8+6 marker count exactly) and the new `height: 40` ScrollView style confirmed present. **On-device confirmation of either fix was not done from this session** — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile; no native module was touched, so both should appear via Fast Refresh with no rebuild needed.

### Follow-on: the explicit-height ScrollView fix above didn't actually close the gap — abandoned the ScrollView approach entirely, switched to the already-proven flex-wrap pattern

The user re-tested and reported the exact same blank gap between the tab row and the map, unchanged by the `height: 40` fix. Two failed guesses in a row on the same `ScrollView` (first the nested `flex-row` View, then an explicit height) is a real signal that a horizontal `ScrollView`'s own on-device sizing behavior here is genuinely unpredictable in a way this session can't fully explain without live device inspection — not something worth a third speculative patch on the same component.

- **Fix: dropped the `ScrollView` entirely.** `SituationMapScreen.tsx`'s 7-tab row now uses the exact `flex-row flex-wrap` `View` pattern `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (the shared severity map used by victim/donor/volunteer roles) already uses successfully for its own tab row — no `ScrollView` at all, no guessing about outer/inner container sizing, just tabs that wrap to a second line on narrow phones instead of scrolling horizontally. That original "horizontal scroll instead of wrap" design choice (documented earlier in this file, under "Mobile map layers brought to full parity") was reasonable at the time but is now a worse tradeoff than a working, already-proven layout — a wrapped second row of tabs is a normal, common mobile pattern, and nowhere else in this app that uses it has ever had a gap reported.
- Removed the now-unused `ScrollView` import from `SituationMapScreen.tsx` (would otherwise be a dead import once nothing in the file references it).
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8096`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward via its process tree — the user's real dev-client session on port 8081, confirmed running under the same PID before and after, was never touched) returned `200` (14.4MB); confirmed `height:40` (the just-abandoned fix) is now completely absent from the compiled output — proof the old code path is genuinely gone, not just shadowed. **On-device confirmation was not done from this session** — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile; this is the third attempt at this specific layout, so it's worth the user's own careful look this time before assuming it's resolved.

### Follow-on: marker dots switched from custom-View to image-based, after real diagnosis (not another guess) confirmed the root cause — plus country/district search added to both mobile map files

The gap fix above landed correctly (user-confirmed), but the marker-position bug the user separately reported ("dots still off") was still present after two speculative prop fixes (`anchor`, then `flat` — see the two follow-ons above). Rather than guess a third prop, this pass **diagnosed it properly**: the user sent a screenshot of a tapped river-gauge marker ("Nagalagam Street, Kelani Ganga") sitting visibly out in the ocean; the real coordinate the backend returns for that station (`6.955246666666667, 79.87669666666667`) was reverse-geocoded via OpenStreetMap's Nominatim API and resolved to the **exact correct street name** — proving conclusively this was a rendering bug, not bad source data, before touching any code.

- **Researched the actual documented cause** (`WebSearch`/`WebFetch` against `react-native-maps/react-native-maps` GitHub issues #230, #328, #5435, and the library's own `marker.md` docs) rather than reasoning from memory a third time: custom-View markers (a JSX child passed to `<Marker>`, which is what `<ColorDot>` was) have a **long-standing, library-maintainer-acknowledged, unfixed Android bug** — Android snapshots the JS-rendered child into a bitmap and can compute the `anchor` offset against the wrong measured size. Issue #5435 was closed by the maintainers as "not planned" — there is no upstream fix coming, at any version (the installed version here, 1.20.1, predates that issue's specific 1.21+ regression report, yet still exhibits the same class of bug, confirming this is a chronic weakness of the whole custom-View-marker approach, not one version-specific regression).
- **Fix: switched every marker in both mobile map files from a `<ColorDot>` JSX child to an `image`-based marker** — the community's consistently-recommended workaround, since an image-based marker gives Android a real bitmap with known pixel dimensions immediately, with no JS-view-snapshot timing ambiguity to get wrong.
  - **`mobile/scripts/generate-marker-dots.js`** (new, one-off, re-run manually if a marker color ever changes) — uses `pngjs` (already present as a transitive dependency, confirmed before reaching for it, no new dependency added) to hand-generate a filled-circle-with-white-border PNG (matching `ColorDot`'s exact visual style) for every one of the 8 distinct marker hex colors used across both files (confirmed via grep, excluding non-marker colors like detail-card text `#374151`), at two sizes each (`small`/`large`) — 16 PNGs total, written to `mobile/assets/dots/`.
  - **`mobile/src/lib/markerDots.ts`** (new) — `dotImage(hexColor, size?)`, a small lookup mapping a hex color + a numeric size (mirroring the old `<ColorDot size={n}/>` API) to the right pre-generated `require()`'d PNG, picking the `large` tier only above `size >= 24` (covers the "areas" tab's count-scaled dot, the only marker that ever exceeded the original 10-18 range). **A real, deliberate scope cut, not an oversight**: this trades the old *continuous* per-count dot scaling (`16 + count*2`, capped at 36) for two discrete tiers — correctness of *position* was the actual bug being fixed; losing a smooth size gradient on one tab is a small, acceptable cost.
  - All 8 markers in `SituationMapScreen.tsx` and all 6 in `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` converted from `<Marker anchor={...} flat><ColorDot .../></Marker>` to a self-closing `<Marker anchor={...} image={dotImage(...)} />` — `flat` dropped (not meaningful for image markers the same way), `anchor={{x:0.5,y:0.5}}` kept (an image marker's own default anchor is also `{0.5,1}`, bottom-center — still wrong for a plain dot, still needs the override). `ColorDot` itself wasn't deleted — it's still used for the small, non-Marker legend swatches in both files, which were never part of this bug.
  - `Marker.image`'s TypeScript type (`number | ImageURISource`, react-native-maps' own narrower type) required `dotImage()`'s return type to be `number`, not React Native's general `ImageSourcePropType` (which can also be an array) — a real, minor type mismatch caught immediately by `tsc`, not a runtime issue.
- **Country/district search added to both mobile map files**, closing a gap the user pointed out directly ("search by region country is not available in the map in mobile") — this existed on web (`CountrySearchBox.tsx`/`DistrictSearchBox.tsx`, see "Country search + zoom-to-outline" and "District search + zoom on the admin Situation Map" above) but was explicitly scoped out of mobile's original map-parity pass as a "desktop-typing convenience." Reversed here at the user's direct request, ported faithfully:
  - **`mobile/src/components/DistrictSearchBox.tsx`** (new) — RN port of web's component (`TextInput` + `FlatList` dropdown over the existing static `DISTRICTS` list), same "no API fetch needed, it's already a small static list" reasoning.
  - **`mobile/src/components/CountrySearchBox.tsx`** (new) — RN port, lazily fetching the same `GET /api/external/world-countries` GeoJSON endpoint web already uses (verified live: `{type: "FeatureCollection", features: [...]}`, 176 countries).
  - **`mobile/src/lib/geoBounds.ts`** (new) — `regionForGeometry(geometry)`, the RN equivalent of Leaflet's built-in `L.geoJSON(feature).getBounds()` (which react-native-maps has no equivalent for at all): walks a Polygon/MultiPolygon coordinate tree recursively to find min/max lat/lng, then returns a react-native-maps `Region` (center + deltas, sized with the same ~15% padding factor Leaflet's own default `fitBounds` padding uses) for `animateToRegion`.
  - Wired into **both** `SituationMapScreen.tsx` (district search on the 5 Sri-Lanka-focused tabs — Requests/Areas/Gauges/Reservoirs/Flood Risk; country search on GDACS/Earthquakes) and `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (district search on its 4 local tabs — Areas/Gauges/Reservoirs/Flood Risk; country search on GDACS/Earthquakes) — full parity across every mobile role, not just admin, matching the "the map in mobile" phrasing of the actual request.
  - **A searched district/country persists across tab switches within its own compatible group**, same low-effort, expected-behavior consequence already documented for web's version — the recentering `useEffect` checks `selectedDistrict`/`selectedCountry` first and only falls back to the tab's own default region when neither applies to the current tab, so nothing needs to be force-cleared on every tab change.
  - **A selected country's outline is drawn on the map too** (not just zoomed to), via react-native-maps' `Geojson` component (confirmed exported, checked its exact prop shape via its `.d.ts` before using it) — violet dashed outline (`#7c3aed`, matching web's identical color choice for the identical reason: not already used by any risk/alert-level palette on this map, so it's never mistaken for a hazard indicator). `Geojson`'s `geojson` prop type (from the `geojson` npm package) doesn't line up 1:1 with this app's looser `CountryFeature` interface, requiring one explicit type cast — the runtime shape is already verified correct (it's the same real API response web already consumes), so the cast reflects a real type-system gap, not an actual runtime risk.
- **A serious, unrelated environment problem hit mid-pass, worth recording**: the development machine's C: drive hit **0 bytes free**, which caused an `npm`/Metro `ENOSPC` failure and then — more seriously — a plain text `Edit` tool call also failed with `ENOSPC` partway through. Work paused immediately rather than risk a corrupted file from a failed write; the user freed space (confirmed back to ~3.5GB free) before this pass resumed. Worth remembering for future sessions: if a `Bash` or `Edit` call ever fails with `ENOSPC` on this machine again, stop and confirm free disk space before any further file writes, don't assume it's a transient fluke.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across every new/edited file (fixed one real type error, the `Marker.image` narrowing, along the way — not silently ignored). A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8098`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward via its process tree — the user's real dev-client session was confirmed **not running** at the time, for an unrelated reason: it had stopped, most likely a casualty of the same disk-full incident, not something this session did) returned `200` (14.4MB), with `"Search for a district"`/`"Search for a country"` (4 occurrences) and `regionForGeometry`/`dotImage` (21 occurrences) confirmed present in the compiled output. **On-device confirmation of the marker-position fix specifically was not done from this session** — same standing limitation as the rest of mobile, but stated more pointedly here given this is the third attempt at fixing this exact bug; this pass is materially different from the previous two (a structural fix backed by external research and a live coordinate-verification proof, not another prop guess), but "materially more likely to be correct" is not the same claim as "confirmed working on a device," and shouldn't be presented as one.

### Follow-on: the image-based marker fix above introduced a real, found-immediately-on-device sizing bug — markers rendered several times too big

User-reported directly: "the red dot is hugeeeeee in the areas affected map... make it a proper size all the dots." A real regression introduced by the custom-View-to-image-marker switch immediately above, not a new bug in unrelated code.

- **Root cause: `mobile/assets/dots/*.png` had no `@2x`/`@3x` density-suffixed siblings.** `mobile/scripts/generate-marker-dots.js` originally wrote one flat PNG per color/tier at `48px` ("small") and `96px` ("large") with no matching `@2x`/`@3x` filenames alongside them. Metro's asset pipeline treats an unsuffixed image as the **1x** density variant — meaning `<Marker image={require(...)}>` rendered it at its raw pixel dimensions as the *logical* (dp) size, on every device, regardless of that device's actual screen density. So "small" markers (most fixed dots across both map files) rendered at 48dp instead of the intended ~16dp, and "large" (the Areas Affected tab's count-scaled dot, used for the reddest/most-visible high-need districts) rendered at 96dp instead of ~32-36dp — roughly 3x oversized, exactly the "hugeeeeee" symptom reported.
- **Fixed at the generator, not with a runtime style override** — react-native-maps' `Marker.image` has no `width`/`height` prop to constrain an image-marker's display size (that's only available on the old, now-abandoned custom-View child pattern, which has its own unfixed Android anchor bug — see the immediately preceding follow-on). `generate-marker-dots.js` now emits a real `@1x`/`@2x`/`@3x` density triplet per color/tier at the **actual target dp size** (`small: 16dp`, `large: 32dp` — `TARGET_DP`, replacing the old raw-pixel `SIZES` constant), e.g. `red-small.png` (16px, the 1x/base file), `red-small@2x.png` (32px), `red-small@3x.png` (48px). Metro auto-discovers the `@2x`/`@3x` siblings from the base filename and serves whichever matches the device's real pixel ratio, reporting the correct 16dp/32dp logical size regardless of which physical file loads — the same convention every other image asset in a React Native app relies on, just never applied here since these were hand-generated. **No code change needed in `markerDots.ts` or either map file** — both already `require()` the base (unsuffixed) filename, which Metro resolves to the right density variant transparently.
- Verified: regenerated all 48 PNGs (8 colors × 2 tiers × 3 densities, up from 16) and confirmed via direct pixel inspection (`pngjs`) that `red-small.png`/`@2x`/`@3x` are exactly `16×16`/`32×32`/`48×48`, and `red-large.png`/`@2x`/`@3x` are exactly `32×32`/`64×64`/`96×96` — matching the new `TARGET_DP` values precisely. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (freed afterward, the user's real dev-client session on port 8081 confirmed untouched throughout) returned `200`, confirming the new asset set bundles without error. **On-device visual confirmation was not done from this session** — same standing mobile-testing limitation documented throughout this file; no native module was touched (this is a plain asset regeneration), so the corrected marker sizes should appear immediately via Fast Refresh on the user's existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed.

## Verbose-text trimming pass (web + mobile, all roles)

Direct user ask, prompted by pointing at one specific example (`volunteerCommunityReport.subtitle`, 138-177 chars depending on platform) and asking for the same treatment "as much as possible... among all roles." Scoped and executed as two separate sweeps: translated (i18n) strings first, then admin's English-only hardcoded strings.

- **i18n sweep**: wrote a small Node script (`node -e`, not checked in) that walked both `web/src/locales/en.json` and `mobile/src/locales/en.json` recursively and printed every string over 90 characters — 20 candidates on web, 14 on mobile. **Before touching any of them, checked which ones are gated behind an already-collapsed "How is this calculated?" `InfoDisclosure` toggle** (`severityMap.gdacsCaption`/`earthquakesCaption`/`reservoirsCaption` on web) — those were deliberately left alone. They're the intentionally-thorough detail text for someone who opts in to read more, a different design goal from a glanceable always-visible subtitle, and shortening them would undo the exact "plain language first, detail on demand" work from the earlier "Public map accessibility pass" (see above).
- **11 always-visible strings actually shortened** (shared between web and mobile where the key exists on both): `sos.modalSubtitle`, `landing.heroSubtitle`, `landing.affectedAreasDesc`, `donorDonationForm.whatAreYouDonatingHint`, `volunteerCommunityReport.subtitle` (the user's own example — web's 177-char and mobile's separately-diverged 138-char version unified into one 71-char line), `severityMap.description`, `severityMap.weatherCaption`, `severityMap.communityReportsCaption`, plus web-only `severityMap.gdacsSimpleCaption`/`earthquakesSimpleCaption`/`floodRiskSimpleCaption` (the already-short `InfoDisclosure` summary lines, tightened further) and the 3 `mapWalkthrough.step*Body` onboarding-modal strings (confirmed via grep that mobile never built the walkthrough feature at all, so only web needed these).
- **A real, separate drift bug surfaced and fixed along the way, not just a length issue**: mobile's `footer.tagline` (133 chars) and `footer.dataSourcesText` (263 chars) were still the *original* verbose copy — web had already trimmed both of these to 69/84 chars in an earlier pass (visible in `Footer.tsx`), but mobile's locale file, being a manually-maintained mirror rather than a shared source of truth (see "Internationalization" above), never got that sync. Fixed by copying web's already-short Sinhala/Tamil translations directly into mobile's files for these two keys, rather than composing new ones — same English source, same translation, finally actually in sync across platforms.
- Two mobile-only captions with no web equivalent (`severityMap.reservoirsMapCaption`, `severityMap.floodRiskCaption`) were also shortened independently, since mobile never built the `InfoDisclosure` split for these sections at all (confirmed via grep — mobile shows one plain caption per section, not a summary/details pair), so both were always-visible on mobile despite one of the two (`reservoirsMapCaption`) being **completely unused dead code on web** (confirmed via grep — its 330-char English value only exists in web's locale files, never referenced by any `.tsx` file, a leftover from the same earlier caption-removal pass that deleted its actual usage but not the now-orphaned translation key; left alone rather than deleted, since removing dead i18n keys wasn't the ask and carries its own small risk of being wrong about "truly unused").
- **Admin sweep (English-only, hardcoded JSX strings — not i18n keys, since admin stays English everywhere per this project's standing convention)**: a second script scanned every `.tsx` file under `web/src/pages` and `mobile/src/screens` for `<p>`/`<Text>` elements with a plain-text child over ~100 characters. Found and shortened 9 page-header subtitles: `AdminActiveDistricts.tsx`/`ActiveDistrictsScreen.tsx` (both platforms, same trim), `AdminCommunityReports.tsx`/`CommunityReportsScreen.tsx` (both), `AdminSos.tsx`, `AdminWaterAlerts.tsx`/`WaterAlertsScreen.tsx` (both, `AdminWaterAlerts.tsx` needed two rounds — the first trim was still over the re-scan's tighter 115-char threshold), `AdminBroadcast.tsx`, and one hardcoded (non-i18n) string in mobile's `SosButton.tsx` success screen. Two short functional status messages in `AdminDonations.tsx` ("No auto-assign match...", "No volunteers registered yet...") were deliberately left alone — those are informational states, not explanatory prose, a different category from what was actually asked to be trimmed.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `web` and `mobile` after every round of edits; all 6 locale JSON files re-validated with `JSON.parse` (the i18n sweep used a small Node script to set values by key path directly on the parsed object rather than string-replacing raw JSON text, avoiding any risk of a quote/escape mismatch corrupting the file). A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (`8099`, confirmed stopped and the port freed afterward — the user's real dev-client session, confirmed not running at the time for the same disk-space-related reason noted above, was unaffected either way) returned `200` (14.4MB) with `"For life-threatening emergencies only"`, `"Seen a blocked road or rising water"` (the trimmed, unified version), and `"Rising gauge status can notify"` all confirmed present in the compiled output. **On-device visual confirmation was not done from this session** — same standing mobile limitation; no native module was touched, so this should appear via Fast Refresh with no rebuild needed once the user's own Metro session is running again.
- **A genuine, unrelated blocker hit mid-pass**: the one-off Node scripts used for this sweep were written to `scripts/` at the repo root and deleted immediately after use (not checked into the project) — kept separate from `server/scripts/` (the project's real, permanent utility scripts) since these were purely mechanical text-editing aids with no ongoing purpose.

### Road closures — investigated, declined, not building this

Followed up on the DMC "Closed Road" bulletin link left uninvestigated in an earlier pass. Traced it directly on the real site (`dmc.gov.lk` > Present Situation > Closed Road): the page itself has **no structured HTML data at all** — just a heading and a link, exactly the same PDF-only shape as the already-declined DMC Situation Reports. The link points to a single static PDF (`ගමනගමනය_කල_නහක_මරග_edit_2.pdf`, Sinhala for "roads that cannot be travelled").

- **A real, honest limitation, not a skipped step: the PDF itself could not be reliably fetched at all during this investigation** — repeated attempts (browser navigation, `curl`, `WebFetch`) hit either `ERR_CONNECTION_TIMED_OUT` or the whole `dmc.gov.lk` domain going briefly unreachable, the identical server-side resource-exhaustion pattern this project already documented for GDACS's REST API. The DMC homepage and RSS feed loaded fine in between attempts, so this isn't a dead domain — it's specifically this asset/page being slow or intermittently broken on their end.
- Even setting connectivity aside, the PDF would have needed Sinhala-language text/OCR extraction with no coordinates to geocode the named roads against — the same effort-vs-value tradeoff that already justified not scraping DMC's Situation Reports or water-level bulletins.
- **Declined for a second, better reason: this app already has a real, live equivalent.** Volunteers can already submit exactly this kind of report (road closures, water conditions) via the Community Reports feature (`/community-report` on web, the volunteer's delivery screen on mobile — see "Community reports" above), which a verified report can even promote straight into an active-district declaration. Building a second, parallel, DMC-PDF-sourced road-closure feed would duplicate that path for no real gain, on top of the reliability problems above.
- **Net conclusion, stated plainly rather than left open: DMC has no usable structured feed for road closures, and this project doesn't need one anyway** — the volunteer-reported Community Reports channel already covers this need. Not building a scraper, not linking out to the PDF either (a link to an asset this unreliable would just be a broken-link risk on a live page).

### Extending i18n coverage
AdminRequests, AdminDonations, VolunteerDeliveries, DonorDonationForm, DonorMyDonations are still English-only — see "Internationalization" above for the pattern to follow.

## API reference

Base URL: `http://localhost:5000`. All protected routes expect `Authorization: Bearer <firebase-id-token>`.

| Method | Path | Role | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/` | none | health check |
| GET | `/api/stats` | none | aggregate counts for transparency dashboard |
| GET | `/api/stats/by-area` | none | district-aggregated severity for the public map (count + avg severity + color level, no coordinates below district-centroid granularity) |
| POST | `/api/users/profile` | any authed user | create/update profile; requires+validates nic/homeAddress for role victim |
| PATCH | `/api/users/profile` | any authed user | edit own name/phone from Settings; body `{name?, phone?}` — only touches fields provided |
| GET | `/api/users/me` | any authed user | get own profile |
| GET | `/api/users/volunteers` | admin | list all volunteers |
| PATCH | `/api/users/availability` | volunteer | set own availability; body `{available}` |
| PATCH | `/api/users/location` | volunteer | set own location, used for auto-assignment distance ranking; body `{location: {lat, lng}}` |
| POST | `/api/users/fcm-token` | any authed user | register this device's push-notification token; body `{token}` |
| GET | `/api/water-alerts/settings` | admin | current auto-send toggle for water-level area alerts |
| PATCH | `/api/water-alerts/settings` | admin | flip the auto-send toggle; body `{autoSend}` |
| GET | `/api/water-alerts/pending` | admin | unresolved water-level escalations awaiting admin review |
| POST | `/api/water-alerts/pending/:id/approve` | admin | send a pending area alert now to every matching victim |
| POST | `/api/water-alerts/pending/:id/reject` | admin | dismiss a pending area alert without notifying anyone |
| GET | `/api/water-alerts/sent` | admin | history of area alerts actually sent (auto or admin-approved), last 50 |
| POST | `/api/sos` | any authed user | submit a life-safety SOS report; body `{type, location, peopleCount?, description?}` |
| GET | `/api/sos` | admin | all SOS reports, unresolved first |
| GET | `/api/sos/mine` | any authed user | own submitted SOS reports |
| PATCH | `/api/sos/:id/status` | admin | advance status; body `{status: "acknowledged"\|"in_progress"\|"resolved"}` |
| POST | `/api/community-reports` | volunteer | submit a road-closure/water-condition report; body `{type, description, location}` |
| GET | `/api/community-reports` | admin | all reports, unverified first |
| GET | `/api/community-reports/verified` | none | verified reports only — what the public/map surfaces actually show |
| PATCH | `/api/community-reports/:id/verify` | admin | approve or dismiss; body `{approve, activateDistrict?}` — approving with `activateDistrict: true` also declares the report's district an active emergency |
| GET | `/api/chats/mine` | any authed user | every delivery chat the caller is a participant in |
| POST | `/api/chats/:chatId/messages` | any authed user | send a message; body `{text}`; 403 if not a participant, 400 if the chat is locked |
| PATCH | `/api/chats/:chatId/consent` | any authed user | set the caller's own consent to share name/phone; reveals contact once both sides have consented |
| GET | `/api/chats/:chatId/contact` | any authed user | the other party's name+phone, only once both sides have consented (`{revealed: false}` otherwise) |
| GET | `/api/notifications/mine` | any authed user | own notification history, last 50, most recent first |
| PATCH | `/api/notifications/:id/read` | any authed user | mark one notification as read; 403 if it's not your own |
| PATCH | `/api/notifications/mark-all-read` | any authed user | mark every one of the caller's unread notifications as read |
| POST | `/api/requests` | victim | submit multi-item request; validates category caps, sets possibleDuplicate flag |
| GET | `/api/requests` | admin | list all, sorted by priorityScore desc |
| GET | `/api/requests/mine` | victim | own requests |
| PATCH | `/api/requests/:id/verify` | admin | approve/reject |
| PATCH | `/api/requests/bulk-verify` | admin | approve/reject several at once; body `{ids, approve}` |
| PATCH | `/api/requests/:id/status` | admin | manual status override, rarely needed |
| GET | `/api/requests/:id/notes` | admin | internal case-note thread for a request, chronological |
| POST | `/api/requests/:id/notes` | admin | add a case note; body `{text}` |
| POST | `/api/donations` | donor | register donation with deliveryMethod |
| GET | `/api/donations` | admin | list all |
| GET | `/api/donations/mine` | donor | own donations |
| POST | `/api/donations/:id/match` | admin | matches to best request still needing this category; self-delivery auto-creates+auto-accepts its delivery here |
| POST | `/api/deliveries` | admin | assign a volunteer (volunteer-delivery donations only) |
| GET | `/api/deliveries/mine` | volunteer | own deliveries, including ones awaiting accept/reject |
| PATCH | `/api/deliveries/:id/accept` | volunteer | pending_acceptance → accepted |
| PATCH | `/api/deliveries/:id/reject` | volunteer | pending_acceptance → rejected; body `{reason?}`; reopens donation for reassignment |
| PATCH | `/api/deliveries/:id/status` | volunteer | picked_up / delivered |
| PATCH | `/api/deliveries/:id/self-deliver` | donor | accepted → delivered, self-delivery only |
| GET | `/api/deliveries/by-request/:requestId` | victim | all deliveries for own request (confirmToken stripped) |
| GET | `/api/deliveries/by-donation/:donationId` | donor | delivery for own donation (includes confirmToken, for the QR) |
| POST | `/api/deliveries/:id/confirm` | victim | ownership-checked AND body `{token}` must match delivery.confirmToken (scanned from QR); updates item + recomputes request status |
| GET | `/api/external/alerts` | none | DMC Emergency Warnings, parsed from their real RSS feed (often empty — no active warning) |
| GET | `/api/external/water-levels` | none | live river gauge readings, sourced directly from the Dept. of Irrigation's own ArcGIS feature service, with computed status vs flood thresholds |
| GET | `/api/external/gdacs` | none | GDACS global disaster events (RSS-sourced), filtered to Sri Lanka relevance by default; `?scope=global` returns every current event worldwide (for demo purposes when nothing is Sri Lanka-relevant) |
| GET | `/api/external/district-boundaries` | none | real Sri Lanka district (admin-2) boundary polygons, sourced from HDX's official COD-AB dataset, simplified server-side; cached 24h and warmed at server startup |
| GET | `/api/external/earthquakes` | none | recent magnitude 4.0+ earthquakes from USGS across the Indian Ocean/Bay of Bengal/Sumatra subduction zone — a regional tsunami-risk indicator, not a Sri Lanka earthquake feed |
| GET | `/api/external/weather` | none | current conditions for 8 cities spanning Sri Lanka's main regions, from OpenWeatherMap — returns `[]` without `OPENWEATHER_API_KEY` set |
| GET | `/api/external/reservoirs` | none | daily storage %/spilling status for 109 Irrigation Dept major+medium reservoirs plus 3 CEB/Mahaweli hydropower reservoirs (Kotmale/Victoria/Randenigala), risk-sorted worst-first, `source` field distinguishes the two — no coordinates (see "Reservoir risk monitoring") |
| GET | `/api/external/flood-risk` | none | per-district flood risk for the current month from a trained logistic regression (real 1981-2020 DesInventar + NASA POWER historical data), cached 6h — see "Flood risk forecast (ML)" |
| POST | `/api/external/flood-risk/retrain` | admin | re-runs the training pipeline live (~30-60s) and reloads the model — the "Retrain model now" button on `/admin/map`'s Flood Risk Forecast tab |
| GET | `/api/categories` | any authed user | current category limits, `{[key]: {label, max, unit}}` — self-seeds Firestore on first call |
| POST | `/api/categories` | admin | add a new category; body `{label, unit, max: number\|null}` |
| PATCH | `/api/categories/:key` | admin | adjust an existing category's cap/label/unit; body `{max?, label?, unit?}` |
| GET | `/api/broadcasts/active` | none | current emergency banner, or `null` if none is active |
| GET | `/api/broadcasts` | admin | broadcast history, most recent 20 |
| POST | `/api/broadcasts` | admin | post a new banner (deactivates any current one); body `{message, severity?}` |
| PATCH | `/api/broadcasts/:id/deactivate` | admin | clear the active banner without posting a replacement |
| GET | `/api/audit-log` | admin | last 100 logged actions (approve/reject/bulk/match/assign/district), most recent first |
| GET | `/api/active-districts` | none | list currently active-emergency districts |
| POST | `/api/active-districts` | admin | activate a district; body `{district, sourceAlertTitle?}` |
| DELETE | `/api/active-districts/:district` | admin | deactivate a district |

## Running it

```bash
cd server && npm install && npm run dev     # http://localhost:5000
cd web && npm install && npm run dev        # http://localhost:5173
```
Requires `server/.env` with `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_PATH` (forward slashes even on Windows) and `web/.env` with `VITE_FIREBASE_*` values. Once Google Maps navigation work starts, `web/.env` will also need a Maps API key — not yet added, ask the user for it rather than assuming a placeholder works.

## Manual end-to-end test procedure

No automated tests exist yet. Confirmed working live in the browser as of this handoff:
1. Register a victim — NIC + home address required; a 2nd victim at the same address submitting a request while the 1st has an active one gets `possibleDuplicate: true` (flagged, not blocked)
2. Victim submits a multi-category request (e.g. food + water) — per-category caps enforced server-side even if the client is bypassed; a medicine item shows no cap but flags "⚕ Contains medicine — review" for admin
3. Admin approves at `/admin/requests`
4. Donor registers a donation picking "Find a volunteer" or "I'll deliver it myself"
5a. **Volunteer path:** admin matches then assigns a volunteer; volunteer must Accept or Reject (optional reason) at `/deliveries/mine` — a reject reopens the donation for reassignment and shows the admin the reason; then picked_up → delivered
5b. **Self-delivery path:** admin matches (delivery auto-created+accepted); donor marks "Mark as delivered" at `/donations/mine`
6. Victim scans the QR code (shown by the volunteer or self-delivering donor once status is "delivered") via "Scan to confirm" at `/request/mine` — a wrong/missing token gets rejected (403), verified directly against the API; request only flips to "Delivered" once every item is confirmed
7. `/transparency` and `/severity-map` both reflect the updated data; `/admin/map` shows exact pinpoints
8. Language switcher (EN/SI/TA) works on all translated pages and persists across reloads

## Concurrent-load testing

Directly answers a gap the dissertation itself acknowledges rather than glosses over: formal evaluation at high concurrent user volume, alongside structured cross-role usability evaluation, had been outstanding. **The two are genuinely different things and were not conflated**: usability evaluation (SUS scores, facilitator-guided scenarios — see `evaluation/`) needs real human participants and can't be simulated or faked; concurrent-load testing is pure engineering and can be run directly against the real system, which is what this section covers.

- **`server/load-test.js`** (kept in the repo, not deleted, as evaluation-chapter evidence/reproducibility material) — creates real throwaway Firebase accounts (30 victims, 30 donors, 15 volunteers, 1 admin) via the Admin SDK, gets real ID tokens via the Identity Toolkit REST API (same technique used throughout this project's manual testing history), then fires genuinely concurrent (`Promise.all`, not sequential) requests at the real Express + Firestore backend across five phases: concurrent request submission, concurrent admin approval, concurrent donation registration, concurrent donation-matching (the real race-condition probe, see below), concurrent volunteer accept, and a 50-request concurrent read burst. Reports min/avg/p95/max latency and success/failure counts per phase, then deletes every trace of itself (Firestore docs + Auth accounts).
- **The race-condition probe (Phase 3) is the highest-value check, not just a performance number.** 30 donations all needing to match against the same pool of 30 pending "water" request items, fired at `POST /api/donations/:id/match` simultaneously — `donations.js`'s match logic reads candidates then writes without a Firestore transaction, so this is exactly the scenario where two concurrent match calls could both read the same "best" request before either write lands, and both try to claim the same item. **Verified clean**: a post-run integrity check (counting how many times each `donationId` appears across all request items) found zero double-matched items across the run. Worth re-running this specific check if the matching logic in `donations.js` is ever touched.
- **A real bug found and fixed, not just numbers collected**: the first run showed `/api/stats` and `/api/stats/by-area` doing a full, uncached collection scan on every call — Phase 5's 50-concurrent-read burst had a p95 of ~11s against a min of 18ms (the fast ones were cache hits on `/api/external/water-levels`, the slow ones were these two). Added caching via the existing `utils/cache.js` (30s TTL, `STATS_CACHE_TTL_MS`) — but the very next run got **worse** (p95 ~25s), which surfaced a second, more interesting bug: **`getCached()` itself had no protection against concurrent cache misses.** It cached the *resolved value*, not the in-flight promise, so 50 simultaneous requests hitting a cold cache all independently triggered their own full collection scan — a classic cache-stampede, worse than no caching at all under this exact load pattern. Fixed by storing the in-flight *promise* in the cache map synchronously (before the first `await`), so concurrent callers past the first share one fetch instead of each starting their own — re-verified: p95 dropped to ~2.4s, better than the original uncached baseline. **This fix is in shared infrastructure** (`utils/cache.js`), so it also protects every other endpoint that uses it (`/api/external/alerts`, `/water-levels`, `/gdacs`, `/district-boundaries`, `/earthquakes`, `/weather`) from the same stampede risk under concurrent load, not just the two stats endpoints that surfaced it.
- **Honest inconsistency, not smoothed over**: a third consecutive run (same script, run back-to-back within a few minutes) showed Phases 3 and 4 running much slower than the first two runs (Phase 4 avg jumped to ~59s). Neither of those phases touches the code that changed (`donations.js`/`deliveries.js` match/accept logic, untouched by the cache fix), so this wasn't a regression — almost certainly Firebase Auth/Firestore rate-limiting or quota backoff after three rapid runs each creating and deleting 76 accounts in quick succession. Documented rather than hidden, since a dissertation evaluation chapter reporting only the best run would be less credible than one that shows the full picture and reasons about the outlier correctly.
- Verified: all three runs, 100% request success rate across every phase (zero HTTP-level failures against the real backend under concurrency); the integrity check passed on every run; the cache-stampede fix specifically confirmed via before/after re-run, not assumed.
