## Settings page

Requested directly, on both web and mobile. Confirmed with the user first which of two genuinely open questions applied: **both platforms** (not just one), and **profile + language + notifications** as the content (not also relocating the volunteer's availability/location pills from their existing page — those stay put).

- **Backend: `PATCH /api/users/profile`** (any authed role) — a small, deliberately separate endpoint from `POST /profile` (registration), which always re-writes `createdAt`/`role`/victim `nic`/`homeAddress` and would have been wrong to reuse for a simple "edit my name/phone" form (reusing it would have silently overwritten the account's original `createdAt` on every edit — caught before shipping, not after). Only touches whichever of `name`/`phone` are actually provided via `.update()`, same self-owned-field convention as `PATCH /availability` and `PATCH /location`. `phone: ""` explicitly clears it; a non-empty value must normalize via the existing `normalizeSriLankanPhone()`.
- **`AuthContext.tsx` (web and mobile) both gained `refreshProfile()`** — neither previously had any way to re-fetch the profile after an edit, so without this, saving a new name in Settings wouldn't update the sidebar/header showing `profile.name` until a full reload. Verified live: editing name in the browser updated the sidebar immediately, no refresh.
- **Web: `Settings.tsx`** (`/settings`, no `allowedRoles` restriction — any logged-in role) — four sections: Profile (email read-only, editable name/phone, client-side phone validation via the same `normalizeSriLankanPhone()` Register.tsx uses), Language (reuses `LanguageSwitcher.tsx` directly), Notifications (reuses `requestAndRegisterFcmToken()` from the existing FCM setup — shows enabled/blocked/unsupported state, an "Enable" button only when permission is still undecided), and Account (role display, a Logout button — duplicating the sidebar's, which is fine, common in real apps). Added as the last item in every role's sidebar nav in `DashboardLayout.tsx`. **Translated** (new `settings` + `nav.settings` i18n keys in all three locale files) since it's reachable by every non-admin role, not an admin-only surface — consistent with the "admin stays English, everyone else gets translated" line drawn earlier in this same session.
- **Mobile: `SettingsScreen.tsx`** (`screens/shared/`, same shared-across-roles placement as `SeverityMapScreen.tsx`) — same four sections, native equivalents (`TextInput`/`TouchableOpacity`), reusing mobile's own `LanguageSwitcher.tsx` and `requestAndRegisterPushToken()`. **Placement respects the existing 5-tab ceiling**: added as a 4th tab for victim/donor/volunteer (all well under the limit), but for admin (already at 5 tabs: SOS, Overview, Aid Requests, Donations, More) it went inside `AdminMoreStack`/`MoreScreen.tsx` alongside Categories/Broadcast/etc., not as a 6th tab. Same `settings`/`nav.settings` i18n keys mirrored into mobile's three locale files (mobile's locale files are a pre-divergence snapshot of web's, not auto-synced — see "Internationalization" — so this was a manual, deliberate mirror, not a copy of web's entire file).
- Verified live: registered a throwaway donor on web, edited name and phone through the real UI, confirmed "Saved ✓", confirmed the sidebar name updated with no reload, confirmed invalid-phone client-side validation blocks submission before hitting the API, and confirmed via direct Firestore read that `createdAt` was correctly preserved (not overwritten) and every other field untouched. Confirmed full translation in Sinhala including the role label (a small gap caught and fixed — `profile.role` was rendering as raw English until routed through the existing `auth.role*` i18n keys). On mobile, `tsc --noEmit` is clean and a real Metro bundle pulled via `curl` returned 200 with `SettingsScreen` and `requestAndRegisterPushToken` both confirmed present in the compiled output — the same "prove it actually bundles" discipline already used elsewhere in this file for mobile, since an on-device click-through isn't possible from this session. Test account deleted afterward.

## Multi-category donations (donor picks several categories in one drop-off)

User-reported gap: "a donor might donate several stuff, baby formula, water, clothing and so on so lets make them be able to choose what the donate and the quantity." Before this, `DonorDonationForm.tsx` (web) and `RegisterDonationScreen.tsx` (mobile) only let a donor pick one category per form visit — donating three things meant visiting the form three separate times.

- **No backend or data-model change at all — this was a pure UI gap, not a schema gap.** Unlike `aidRequests` (which has always supported a multi-category `items[]` array per request), `donations` were already a deliberately single-category-per-document design (see the data model section above: "One donation still targets exactly one category — a multi-category request gets fulfilled by several separate donations/deliveries, one per category"). That architecture already anticipated multi-item giving — matching, delivery creation, chat creation, self-delivery, QR confirmation, CSV export, the audit log, and both mobile admin screens all already operate per-donation. The only thing missing was a way for the donor to submit several of those in one sitting.
- **Fix: both forms now show a checkbox+quantity picker for every category** (mirroring `VictimRequestForm.tsx`'s existing multi-select UI pattern exactly, minus the cap logic — donations have no `max`, so quantity is still the free-text field it always was, e.g. "50 kg rice," not a capped number). Selecting several categories and hitting submit fires one `POST /api/donations` per selected category (`Promise.allSettled`, not sequential — no reason to wait on one before starting the next), all sharing the same pickup location/delivery method/notes chosen once for the whole drop-off.
- **Partial-failure handling, since N independent HTTP calls means N independent failure points now, unlike before.** If every call fails, the error surfaces exactly like before (network/validation error shown on the form). If some succeed and some fail: web navigates to `/donations/mine` anyway (the successful ones are real, already-created donations — hiding that would be dishonest) and shows an amber banner via router state (`donorDonationForm.errorPartial`) pointing back to My Donations; mobile keeps only the failed categories in the form (`results[i].status === "rejected"`, matched back to `selectedCategories` by index) so the donor can retry just those instead of re-entering everything from scratch.
- New i18n keys (all three locale files): `whatAreYouDonating`/`whatAreYouDonatingHint` (replacing the old single-select `category` label), `selectAtLeastOne`, `errorPartial`; `errorNoQuantity`'s wording updated to apply per-selected-item ("Please enter a quantity for each item you selected.") rather than the old singular phrasing.
- **Mobile got the identical treatment** for parity, even though it wasn't explicitly asked for on mobile — `RegisterDonationScreen.tsx`'s single `Picker` was replaced with the same checkbox-list-plus-inline-quantity pattern `SubmitRequestScreen.tsx` already established on mobile (custom `TouchableOpacity` checkboxes, since RN has no native checkbox), keeping web and mobile's donor flow in sync the way this project has throughout (see "Mobile app" above).
- **`DonorMyDonations.tsx` needed zero changes** — it already renders one row per donation independently; after this change a single drop-off just produces multiple rows, exactly as if the donor had submitted the old single-item form multiple times in a row.
- Verified live end-to-end (throwaway donor account, web): selected Baby formula/Clothing/Water, entered distinct quantities for each ("20 tins"/"30 items"/"100 bottles"), dropped a pickup pin via the map picker, submitted once, and confirmed `/donations/mine` showed all three as separate, independently-tracked "Available" donations with the correct category/quantity pairs — no partial-failure banner, confirming the happy path fires all three `POST` calls cleanly. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `web` and `mobile` (no Expo-version-specific API used here — a plain checkbox-row pattern already used elsewhere on mobile); `npm run lint` on web still 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing `any` warnings. Test account and its 3 test donations deleted afterward.

## Medicine requests: no quantity field, notes required; clothing gets an optional sizing hint

User ask, from a screenshot of `/admin/requests`: "when a victim asks for medicine let them choose the box, do not have a capacity and if medicine is choosen make the additional notes compulsory and encourage to say how many male/female cloth size (not compulsary)." Applied to both `VictimRequestForm.tsx` (web) and `SubmitRequestScreen.tsx` (mobile), identically, even though only web was shown in the screenshot — this project has consistently mirrored victim/donor form changes across both platforms.

- **Medicine's quantity input is now hidden entirely — checkbox-only.** Medicine already had `max: null` server-side (no cap, flagged for admin review instead — see "Category management" above), so a quantity number was never actually load-bearing for it; the real need is described in free text. The category row's helper text for medicine changed from a unit/cap string to `(describe in notes below)`, and the conditional `<input type="number">` is now gated on `category !== "medicine"` in addition to `selected`. The category is still added to `quantities` state with a fixed `1` when toggled on (never shown or editable) — purely to satisfy `requestValidation.js`'s `validateItems()`, which requires *some* positive quantity per item regardless of category; the number itself is meaningless for medicine and never surfaced anywhere an admin would read it as real data.
- **Notes become required, client-side, only when medicine is selected.** `handleSubmit()`/`handleSubmit` (web/mobile) gained a check right after the existing "select at least one item" guard: `if (medicineSelected && !notes.trim())` blocks submission with a new `medicineNotesRequired` error, before the request ever reaches the server (no backend change — this is a UX guard, not a new validation rule the API enforces, consistent with `notes` remaining an optional field in the schema for every other category). The notes label itself switches from "Additional notes (optional)" to "Additional notes *" with a red asterisk, plus a one-line hint above the textarea explaining why it's required (name/dosage/condition).
- **Clothing gets a separate, non-blocking hint** — a second, independent conditional line above notes when `clothingSelected`, encouraging (not requiring) mentioning male/female counts and approximate sizes. Both hints can show at once if both categories are selected (verified live, see below) — they're two independent `clothingSelected`/`medicineSelected` checks, not a single combined message, since the medicine one is a hard requirement and the clothing one is a suggestion and conflating them into one string would have blurred that distinction.
- New i18n keys (`notesRequiredForMedicine`, `medicineNotesHint`, `clothingNotesHint`, `describeInNotes`, `medicineNotesRequired`) added to all three locale files on both platforms — Sinhala/Tamil AI-translated, same not-yet-native-reviewed caveat as the rest of this app's translations.
- Verified live end-to-end on web (throwaway victim account, real HTTP submission, no mocked backend): selected Medicine + Clothing, confirmed the category list showed "(describe in notes below)" for medicine with no quantity input while Clothing's quantity input remained; confirmed the notes label switched to "Additional notes *" with both hints ("Required — please describe the medicine needed..." and "Optional — it helps to mention how many male/female...") rendering simultaneously; attempted to submit with empty notes and confirmed it was blocked with the exact `medicineNotesRequired` error text, not a generic one; filled in real notes text and confirmed the request submitted successfully with both items ("Medicine ×1", "Clothing ×1") appearing on `/request/mine`. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `web` and `mobile`; all six locale JSON files re-validated with `JSON.parse`. Test account and its test request deleted afterward.

## Admin tables split into Active/Done sections (Aid Requests + Donations)

Direct user ask, from the same message as the medicine/clothing change above: "lets divide the aid requests to two sections up and down up still pending,verified, in progress to up and rejected delivered succefully to down and donation tab too so its more userfriednly and optimal." A single long, flat, priority-sorted table mixing genuinely-actionable rows with settled ones (rejected weeks ago, delivered and done) made it harder to scan for what still needs attention — splitting by status group surfaces the active work at the top without losing the settled history below.

- **`AdminRequests.tsx`**: two grouped sections instead of one table, both computed via `useMemo` over the already-existing `filteredRequests` (so every filter/search/date-range/stale-only control still applies identically to both sections, nothing about the filtering logic changed) — **Active** (`status` in `pending`/`verified`/`in_progress`) and **Done** (`rejected`/`delivered`), each still sorted by `priorityScore` descending within its own group (unchanged sort, just partitioned). The row-rendering markup (priority badge, item chips, flags, Notes button, Approve/Reject actions) was extracted into a new `RequestsTable` component so both sections share one implementation rather than two copies of the same JSX to keep in sync — a pure refactor of existing markup, no visual or behavioral change to an individual row. The "select all pending" bulk-select checkbox lives correctly in the Active section's header only (computed against that section's own pending count) since Done rows are never selectable; the empty-state message is distinct per section ("No active requests match your filters." vs. "No settled requests match your filters.") so an empty Active section during a genuine lull doesn't read as "no requests at all."
- **`AdminDonations.tsx`**: identical pattern — **Active** (`available`/`matched`, i.e. anything an admin might still need to find a match or assign/reassign a volunteer for) and **Done** (`delivered`). Row markup (the whole `Find match`/volunteer-assignment/reassignment action-cell logic, including the leftover-quantity display from "Donation leftover-quantity tracking" above) extracted into a new `DonationsTable` component the same way, so both sections share the exact same interactive controls rather than duplicating them.
- Each section gets a small header (`ACTIVE`/`DONE` label, a one-line subtitle stating which statuses it covers, and a live row count) above its own table, visually separated as two distinct card blocks rather than one continuous table — same "clearly separated card" visual language already established elsewhere in this app (see "DMC and Community Reports panels visually separated" under the admin map's own polish passes).
- Verified live end-to-end against real, pre-existing data (throwaway admin account, no seeded/synthetic rows): `/admin/requests` correctly split 18 real requests into 7 Active (pending/verified/in_progress) and 10 Done (rejected/delivered) rows — cross-checked every row's status against its section by reading the actual rendered table, not just trusting the count; `/admin/donations` correctly split 17 real donations into 9 Active (available/matched) and 8 Done (delivered), with the "Find match," "Reassign to...," and remaining-quantity ("(0 left)") controls all still rendering and functioning correctly for the relevant rows inside the split view. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run lint` 0 errors, same 32 pre-existing `any` warnings as the pre-change baseline (confirmed by diffing against a `git stash` of this session's changes — the 32-warning count is not a regression, CLAUDE.md's earlier "30 warnings" figure was simply stale). Test admin account deleted afterward.

## Donation quantity picker simplified to match the victim form

User feedback, from a mobile screenshot: the donor's category picker ("Register a Donation") showed **two** text boxes per selected category ("50 kg rice" free-text + a separate numeric "Amount" box) — confusing and visually cluttered next to `VictimRequestForm.tsx`/`SubmitRequestScreen.tsx`'s clean single-number-box picker.

- **Both `DonorDonationForm.tsx` (web) and `RegisterDonationScreen.tsx` (mobile) now use exactly one numeric input per selected category**, same as the victim form — the free-text `quantity` field (needed downstream for display, e.g. "50 kg" on `AdminDonations.tsx`/`DonorMyDonations.tsx`/CSV export) is now derived automatically as `${amount} ${category.unit}` at submit time, never typed by the donor. The category row's hint text changed from a cap string to the unit alone (e.g. "Clothing (items)"), matching the victim form's "(up to N unit)" convention.
- This was also the natural point to build the donation-batching change below (see "Donation batching" — a multi-category submission now goes through one atomic `POST /api/donations/batch` call instead of N separate `POST /api/donations` calls), since both changes touched the same form state.
- Verified live end-to-end (throwaway donor account, real backend): selected Water + Clothing, confirmed each shows exactly one number box with no leftover free-text field; submitted with 20/10; confirmed `/donations/mine` rendered "Water — 20 Liters" and "Clothing — 10 Items" — the derived display strings are correct, unit-aware, and match the category's real unit. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` clean on web (0 errors, same 32 pre-existing warnings); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on mobile.

## Donation batching — a multi-category drop-off stays linked, not fragmented across volunteers

User-reported gap, described precisely: "imagine donor1 is donating 5 categories, its listed as 5 different donations, and when assigning it to a volunteer its going to choose the nearest available person but the volunteer receives it as 5 different requests, volunteer doesnt know all that is give by the same donor in the same place." Donations have always been deliberately single-category-per-document (see "Multi-category donations" above — unchanged, still true), but nothing tied a multi-category submission's several documents back together, so each category's own `POST /:id/match` independently ran nearest-available-volunteer matching, with a real chance of scattering one physical pickup across several volunteers who had no way to know the others existed.

- **`POST /api/donations/batch`** (new, donor-only, `server/src/routes/donations.js`) — replaces the donor forms' old "fire N separate `POST /api/donations` calls, handle partial failure" pattern with one atomic Firestore `batch()` write: still one donation document per category (unchanged data model), but created together, all sharing a generated `dropoffId` (`crypto.randomBytes(8).toString("hex")`, same convention as `generateConfirmToken()`) and a `dropoffItemCount`. Atomicity is a real improvement on its own, not just a grouping label — a category-cap violation on item 3 of 5 now fails the whole submission cleanly instead of leaving 2 real orphaned donations behind for the donor to notice and reconcile. The original single-item `POST /` route is untouched and still works (now also sets `dropoffId: null, dropoffItemCount: 1` for schema consistency) — kept for any future single-category caller, not currently used by either form.
- **`findVolunteerForDropoff(dropoffId, excludeDonationId)`** (new, `server/src/utils/autoAssignVolunteer.js`) — called from `POST /:id/match` *before* `findBestVolunteer()`: if any sibling donation sharing this `dropoffId` already has an active delivery (`pending_acceptance`/`accepted`/`picked_up`) with a volunteer who's still marked available, that same volunteer is reused instead of independently recomputing nearest-by-distance. This is the actual fix — without it, two categories from the same pickup could easily land on two different volunteers simply because availability/workload shifted between each category's own match call. Deliberately restricted to *active* deliveries only: a sibling whose delivery was rejected doesn't default back to that volunteer (they explicitly declined), and one already delivered/confirmed means that volunteer is likely done with the pickup entirely. The match response's `autoAssignedVolunteer` gained a `sameDropoff: true/false` field so the frontend (and the audit log, via `source: "auto_dropoff"` vs `"auto"`) can tell which path fired.
- **Every delivery doc created from a volunteer-delivery donation (auto-assign at match time, and the admin's manual assign/reassign route in `deliveries.js`) now copies `dropoffId`/`donorName` from its donation** — this is what lets a volunteer's own delivery list show the grouping without an extra fetch per donation.
- **Fellow travellers extended to understand same-dropoff siblings, not just destination matches.** `GET /:id/fellow-travellers` now always surfaces a candidate delivery whose donation shares the caller's own `dropoffId`, *regardless of destination district* — the two items came from the exact same physical pickup, which matters even when they end up serving two different victims in two different districts (destination-only matching would otherwise miss this entirely). Each result gets a `sameDropoff` boolean, and same-dropoff results sort first. The chat-start route (`POST .../fellow-travellers/:otherId/chat`) and the handoff route (`POST /:id/handoff`) both gained the identical bypass via a new shared `deliveriesShareDropoff(delivery, other)` helper — a same-dropoff pair can message/hand off to each other even when their destinations don't match, since the existing "must share a destination district" rule was never really about the dropoff case, it was a proxy for "are you both actually near each other," and sharing a `dropoffId` is a stronger, more direct signal of that than destination ever was.
- **Frontend grouping, both platforms**: `AdminDonations.tsx` shows "📦 1 of N-item drop-off" under the donor's name when `dropoffItemCount > 1`, and the "Find match" success message notes "(same volunteer already handling another item from this drop-off)" when `sameDropoff` is true. `VolunteerDeliveries.tsx` (web) and `MyDeliveriesScreen.tsx` (mobile) compute a `dropoffSiblingCount` from the volunteer's own already-fetched delivery list and show "📦 Part of a N-item drop-off from {donorName}" under any delivery card that has siblings *already in that volunteer's own list* — this only ever reflects items assigned to **them**; a sibling assigned to a *different* volunteer surfaces instead through the Fellow Travellers panel (tagged with the same 📦 badge + an orange highlight), which is also where the existing Message/Hand-off buttons let two volunteers actually consolidate or coordinate a split drop-off.
- New i18n keys (`volunteerDeliveries.dropoffBadge`, `volunteerDeliveries.sameDropoffBadge`) added to all three locale files on both platforms.
- **Verified end-to-end via real HTTP calls against the live backend, with actual proof the override fires (not just that it compiles) — three separate scripts, written for this verification and deleted afterward:**
  1. **Isolated auto-assign override proof**: seeded two volunteers, matched a "food" donation from a 2-item batch with volunteer A genuinely nearer (confirmed picked, `sameDropoff: false`); then **swapped both volunteers' locations** so volunteer B was now objectively nearer, and matched the sibling "shelter" donation — it still landed on volunteer A (`sameDropoff: true`), proving the dropoff-preference logic actually overrode a fresh, independently-computed nearest-distance result that would otherwise have picked B. A pre-existing real test volunteer sitting exactly at the pickup's coordinates (distance 0) was temporarily marked unavailable for this run specifically so it couldn't dominate the distance comparison and mask the result, then restored afterward.
  2. **Fellow-travellers/chat sameDropoff bypass proof**: built a 2-item batch, deliberately split it across two volunteers (auto-assign chose one for both, then manually reassigned the second item to a different volunteer, both accepted), with the two items' requests placed in genuinely different districts (Colombo vs. Kandy). Confirmed `GET .../fellow-travellers` surfaced the sibling with `sameDropoff: true` and `originDistrict: "Colombo"`/`destinationDistrict: "Kandy"` — a pairing ordinary destination-matching would never have shown — and confirmed the chat-start route returned `200` with a real `chatId` despite the destination mismatch.
  3. Both scripts cleaned up every test account/donation/delivery/request/chat they created, confirmed via re-reading the documents afterward, not just trusting their own log output.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` and lint clean on both `web` (0 errors, 32 pre-existing warnings) and `mobile`; `node --check` and `eslint` clean on all three edited server files. A real Metro bundle pulled from a throwaway scratch-port instance (freed afterward — the user's real dev-client session on port 8081 confirmed running under its original process the whole time) returned `200` (14.5MB) with `donations/batch`, `dropoffBadge`, and `sameDropoffBadge` all confirmed present in the compiled output. **On-device visual confirmation of the grouping badges was not done from this session** — same standing mobile-testing limitation documented throughout this file; no native module was touched, so this should appear via Fast Refresh on the existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed.

## "Areas Still In Need" turned into a real dashboard (overall totals + per-district grouping)

Direct user ask: "can we make it like a live dashboard as of total stuff overall in need, and then each district all the stuff in need?" `DonorDistrictNeed.tsx` (web) and `DistrictNeedScreen.tsx` (mobile) previously rendered one flat table/list of `district × category` rows with no aggregate view at all.

- **No backend change** — `GET /api/stats/district-need` already returns everything needed (`{district, category, pendingCount, pendingQuantity}` per combination); both totals and district-grouping are pure client-side re-aggregations of the same already-fetched, already-30s-polled `rows` array.
- **Overall need across Sri Lanka** — a new top section, one card per category (sorted worst-shortage-first): total `pendingQuantity` summed across every district, plus a caption stating how many pending requests and how many distinct districts that total spans. Answers "how much water is needed island-wide right now," not just per-district.
- **By district** — the old flat table replaced with one card per district (worst-hit district first by total pending quantity), each listing its own categories and quantities inside — "each district, all the stuff in need," per the user's own phrasing. The existing district search box still narrows this section (moved to sit directly above it, alongside a new "By district" heading), while the overall-totals section above stays fixed regardless of the search — searching for one district's shortfall shouldn't make the island-wide totals disappear.
- Both `web` and `mobile` versions built in parallel, same structure, matching this project's established two-platform-together convention for donor-facing features.
- New i18n keys (`donorDistrictNeed.overallTitle`, `overallCaption`, `byDistrictTitle`) added to all three locale files on both platforms; the old `district`/`stillNeeded` table-header keys are now unused (harmless, left in place rather than risk removing something still referenced elsewhere).
- Verified live in the browser against real, pre-existing data: confirmed the overall-totals cards render one per real category present in the data with correct summed quantities and captions; confirmed the by-district section correctly grouped rows under district headings; confirmed typing a nonsense search string correctly emptied only the by-district section (with the distinct "no match" message) while the overall totals stayed untouched. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both platforms.

## Admin gets a Messages tab on mobile, for tab-bar parity across all four roles

Direct user ask: "add chat tabs and nitification tabs to all 4 roles." Checked the actual current state of all four mobile tab bars before changing anything (not assumed from memory) — Victim, Donor, and Volunteer already had **both** a Notifications tab and a Messages tab (built in earlier passes: see "Follow-on: mobile notification history" and "Mobile: a Messenger-style delivery chat feature" above). Admin already had Notifications but was missing Messages — the one real gap.

- **`AdminTabs.tsx`** gained a `Messages` tab reusing the exact same `MessagesStack` (`ChatListScreen` + `ChatThreadScreen`) the other three roles already use — zero new screens, zero backend changes. `AdminTabParamList` (`mobile/src/navigation/types.ts`) gained the matching `Messages: NavigatorScreenParams<MessagesStackParamList>` field.
- **Deliberately does not grant admin any oversight of other users' chats.** `GET /api/chats/mine` (which `ChatListScreen.tsx` calls via a live `onSnapshot`) filters strictly by `partyAId`/`partyBId == caller.uid` — an admin is never a delivery-chat participant, so this tab will always show an empty conversation list, exactly as honest and correct given the existing, deliberate privacy boundary documented under "Delivery chat + consent-gated contact sharing" above (admin does have a Firestore-rules read carve-out for individual chat documents, but nothing in this pass queries beyond what a signed-in admin's own uid already scopes to, so that carve-out is never actually exercised here). If real cross-user chat oversight is ever wanted, that's a separate, bigger feature — not something this pass silently added.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; confirmed via a real Metro bundle pull that `MessagesStack`/`AdminHomeScreen` both compile in together with no conflicts. On-device confirmation not done from this session (same standing mobile limitation) — no native module touched, so it should appear via Fast Refresh with no rebuild needed.

## Donation leftover-quantity tracking & district inventory

User-reported gap, given as a structured problem statement rather than a feature request: when a donation is matched against an aid request, only part of its items may be consumed, and the leftover wasn't tracked anywhere — once a donation matched once, it was gone (`status: "matched"`) regardless of whether it actually had more to give. There was also no aggregate view of how much of each item type was currently unallocated in a given district ("Kandy: 5 bottles, 7 formula available") without manually cross-referencing every donation record.

**The literal problem statement didn't match the actual schema, and this was corrected before any code was written.** The user's framing described donations with "one or more item lines" (implying a multi-item-per-donation model, like `aidRequests.items[]`) — but `donations` has always been deliberately single-category-per-document (see "Multi-category donations" above: donating several things means several donation documents, one per category). The real gap wasn't multi-item tracking, it was that a single donation's `quantity` was a **free-text display string** ("50 kg rice"), with no real number to do remainder arithmetic against, and matching was all-or-nothing (`status` flipped straight to `"matched"` on the first hit, full stop). Building item-line schema for a problem that was actually "add a real number and stop treating a match as total consumption" would have been unnecessary restructuring — flagged to the user directly before designing anything.

- **`quantityValue` (a real number) added alongside the existing free-text `quantity`, not replacing it** — additive per the user's own constraint ("prefer additive changes over restructuring existing documents"). `quantity` stays exactly what it always was (the display string every existing consumer already reads); `quantityValue` is the one new field the arithmetic actually needs. `donations.js`'s `POST /` validates it's a positive finite number (400 otherwise) and sets both `quantityValue` and a starting `remainingQuantity` equal to it, plus `district` (via the existing `nearestDistrict()` util, same one every other district-derived feature in this app already uses) and `pendingAllocatedQuantity: null` (see below). Donations created before this field existed have neither `quantityValue` nor `remainingQuantity` at all — handled as a real absence, not backfilled: `POST /:id/match` treats a missing `remainingQuantity` as `donation.quantityValue ?? Infinity` (old data can still match, just without quantity-aware partial consumption), and `AdminDistrictInventory.tsx`'s aggregation treats it as `0` (old, un-migrated donations correctly contribute nothing to the inventory total rather than a guessed number).
- **The actual leftover-tracking math, in `donations.js`'s `POST /:id/match`**: `consumedQuantity = Math.min(remainingBefore, neededByItem)` (never consumes more than the donation has left, or more than the matched request item needs — whichever is smaller), `remainingAfter = remainingBefore - consumedQuantity`. The donation's `status` only flips to `"matched"` once `remainingAfter` reaches exactly 0 — while there's still a positive remainder, it **stays `"available"`**, which is what makes it show up again as a match candidate for a *different* request later. The request-item side is deliberately left all-or-nothing (marked fully `"matched"` regardless of whether this one donation could cover its full quantity) — a confirmed, deferred scope decision (see below), not an oversight.
- **A design decision surfaced mid-build via a clarifying exchange with the user, not guessed**: what happens when a victim needs 10 bottles + 2 formula from Kandy, and one donor only donates 10 bottles? The user asked directly whether that's "a separate request just like donations." Answer, confirmed and left as explicit deferred scope: **a single request item is still fulfilled by exactly one donation, all-or-nothing** — the case of one request needing multiple donations to be fully satisfied (e.g. 2 formula still outstanding after a bottles-only match) isn't built in this pass. What *is* built is the donation side: a donation with leftover after a partial match stays available for a *different* request needing the *same category*. Extending to "let several donations jointly fulfill one oversized request item" is real future scope, not silently attempted here.
- **A second real fork, surfaced via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed: should a partially-consumed donation be matchable a *second* time, and if so, how does the leftover actually get delivered?** The safer, smaller-blast-radius option (track the leftover but never re-match it — i.e. ship the inventory *visibility* half of the ask without the re-matching half) was offered alongside the full option. **The user chose the full option: "Allow the same donation to be matched twice."** This is what actually required the multi-delivery-per-donation rework below — a donation that can be matched more than once needs to support more than one linked delivery over its lifetime, which touched donor-facing delivery-tracking UI on both platforms. Documented here because it was a deliberate, informed scope choice by the user, not scope creep introduced silently.
- **My own first proposal for the reject path was wrong, and self-corrected before implementing it — not caught by the user.** Initially proposed (and the user said yes to) "crediting `allocatedQuantity` back to `remainingQuantity` when a volunteer rejects a delivery." After actually re-reading `deliveries.js`'s reject handler in full: a reject only ever resets `assignedDeliveryId`/`deliveryStatus` back to `null` so the admin can reassign — it never un-matches the request item from the donation, and `donation.status` stays `"matched"` throughout a reject-and-reassign cycle, because the underlying commitment (this donation → this request) never actually breaks, only *who delivers it* does. Crediting quantity back on reject would have created phantom, uncommitted-but-marked-available inventory. Stated the correction to the user directly before writing any code, then implemented the actually-correct behavior: **reject is a no-op on quantity, by design** — nothing needed to change there at all.
- **Multi-delivery-per-donation, the rework the "match twice" choice required.** `GET /api/deliveries/by-donation/:donationId` changed from returning a single delivery-or-`null` (`.limit(1)`) to returning **every** delivery tied to that donation, sorted oldest-first — confirmed via grep that its only two callers (`DonorMyDonations.tsx` web, `MyDonationsScreen.tsx` mobile) were both being updated in the same pass, making the breaking response-shape change safe. Both were rewritten (`Write`, not incremental `Edit`, given the scope of the change): `deliveriesByDonation` is now `Record<string, Delivery[]>` instead of a single-delivery map; `load()` fetches the delivery list for **every** donation, not just self-delivery-and-delivered ones (since a donation can now legitimately have zero, one, or several deliveries at any point); the UI renders a `remainingQuantity` note when `>0`, a "not matched yet" message when the array is empty, and otherwise iterates the array rendering one status badge/allocated-amount caption/chat button/QR-if-applicable block per delivery.
- **`allocatedQuantity` threaded through every delivery-creation path, on the same reasoning as `consumedQuantity` above** — a delivery needs to remember how much of the donation *it* is responsible for, distinct from the donation's own (shared, cumulative) `remainingQuantity`. Both the self-delivery and volunteer-delivery branches of `POST /:id/match` set it directly from `consumedQuantity`. The volunteer branch has an extra wrinkle: if `findBestVolunteer()` finds nobody at match time, there's no delivery document yet to attach `allocatedQuantity` to — so it's temporarily parked on the donation itself as **`pendingAllocatedQuantity`**, carried forward to whichever later `POST /api/deliveries` call (admin manually assigning a volunteer) actually creates the delivery, then cleared back to `null` once consumed. This is a transient field, not a permanent one — its only job is bridging the gap between "we know the quantity" (match time) and "we finally have a delivery doc to put it on" (assign time).
- **`GET /api/stats/district-need`** (new, public, no auth) — the donor-facing half's backend. Deliberately **not** a live `onSnapshot` listener like the admin-facing `AdminDistrictInventory.tsx` below — a donor's own client has no Firestore rule allowing it to read other victims' `aidRequests` documents directly (correctly restricted to the request's own victim or an admin), so a real aggregation had to happen server-side via the Admin SDK, the same way `/api/stats/by-area` already does for the severity map. Groups every active request's pending items by `district::category`, cached via the existing `getCached()` TTL utility (same pattern as every other stats/external endpoint in this file) — the donor page polls it every 30s rather than getting a true push update, an explicit tradeoff of "close enough to live" against "no new live-listener infrastructure for a read a donor's own client can't perform directly anyway."
- **`AdminDistrictInventory.tsx`** (`/admin/district-inventory`, new web page) — the admin-facing half, and a **live** `onSnapshot` on `donations` (admin already has full read access via Firestore rules, so no new endpoint needed here, unlike the donor side above — same architecture as `ResourceGapView.tsx`). Pure client-side `useMemo` aggregation: groups by `${district}::${category}` wherever `remainingQuantity > 0`, summing quantity and counting donations, sorted worst-first. A TypeScript narrowing note worth keeping: `nearestDistrict()` returns `string | null`, so `district` is computed and null-checked (`if (remaining <= 0 || !district) continue`) before use in the aggregation key, rather than asserting non-null.
- **`DonorDistrictNeed.tsx`** (`/donations/district-need`, new web page) — the donor-facing live-ish dashboard the user explicitly asked for mid-conversation ("also add another tab in donor role to see the stuff still in need district wise as a live dashboard"), polling `GET /api/stats/district-need` on mount and every 30s. Both new pages are genuinely self-contained routes, not reworks of the existing `AdminDonations.tsx`/`DonorMyDonations.tsx` dashboards — per the user's own instruction to keep this additive rather than restructuring what already existed.
- **Both dashboards were also built on mobile**, per the user's explicit final go-ahead ("a live dashboard both in mobile and web and the rest"): `DistrictInventoryScreen.tsx` (admin, RN port of the web admin page, same `onSnapshot`+aggregation logic rendered as a card list) and `DistrictNeedScreen.tsx` (donor, RN port of the polling dashboard) — both reachable via Home tiles on their respective roles (see "Mobile app" above for the tile-grid navigation pattern every mobile screen uses).
- **`AdminDonations.tsx` needed zero code changes to correctly reflect partial matching** — confirmed directly rather than assumed: its "Find match" button visibility is already gated on `d.status === "available"`, and since a partially-consumed donation now correctly stays `"available"` (not `"matched"`) while `remainingQuantity > 0`, the button re-appears on its own with no new logic. It did pick up a small display addition: the quantity cell now appends `({d.remainingQuantity} left)` whenever `remainingQuantity < quantityValue`, so an admin can see at a glance that a donation is partially spoken-for.
- **Verified end-to-end against the real backend, not just unit-level** — a throwaway-account test script (`server/scripts/test-partial-matching.js`, written for this verification and deleted afterward, not checked in) created a 7-unit `baby_formula` self-delivery donation in Kandy, then three sequential victim requests needing 2/3/2 units respectively, matching the donation against each in turn: confirmed exact `consumedQuantity`/`remainingQuantity` values and `donation.status` transitions at each step (`available` → `available` → `available` → `matched` once fully consumed), confirmed the `by-donation` endpoint's new array shape and each delivery's correct `allocatedQuantity`, and confirmed a fourth match attempt against the now-fully-consumed donation is correctly rejected with 400 ("no remaining quantity left to match"). Separately confirmed via a direct `curl` to `GET /api/stats/district-need` (after full test cleanup) that real, pre-existing Kandy/baby_formula demand from unrelated data was still present and correctly aggregated — the one "failure" the test script initially flagged (expecting zero pending Kandy/baby_formula demand post-cleanup) turned out to be a flawed test assumption, not a code bug, since other real requests for the same district/category already existed independent of the test.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `web` and `mobile` after this pass; `node --check` clean on all three modified backend route files (`donations.js`, `deliveries.js`, `stats.js`).

### Follow-on: a district search filter added to "Areas Still In Need"

Direct user ask: "let it have a search bar to search according to district, both web and mobile." Both `DonorDistrictNeed.tsx` (web) and `DistrictNeedScreen.tsx` (mobile) had no filtering at all — a plain, always-full table/list.

- **A plain type-to-filter text input, not the map-oriented `CountrySearchBox`/`DistrictSearchBox` components already built elsewhere in this app** — those select exactly one district to zoom a Leaflet/react-native-maps view to, which doesn't apply here (no map on this page). A simple `useMemo`'d substring filter (case-insensitive, matched against `row.district`) over the already-fetched rows is the natural fit, same pattern `AdminRequests.tsx`'s own free-text filters already use — no new backend endpoint needed, this is purely a client-side view over data `GET /api/stats/district-need` already returns.
- Both platforms got a ✕ clear button, and a **second, distinct empty state** (`donorDistrictNeed.noMatch`, "No districts match your search") separate from the existing `noNeed` state ("nothing pending anywhere") — so a genuinely empty search result doesn't misleadingly claim there's no need at all.
- New i18n keys (`searchPlaceholder`, `noMatch`) added to the existing `donorDistrictNeed` namespace, all three locales, both platforms.
- Verified live in the browser (throwaway donor account, real pre-existing data): typing "kandy" correctly narrowed 3 real rows down to the 2 real Kandy rows; typing a nonsense string correctly showed the new "No districts match your search" state (not the "nothing pending" one); clicking ✕ correctly restored all rows. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `web` and `mobile`; `npm run lint` on web still 0 errors, same pre-existing warning count. Test account deleted afterward. Mobile's on-device rendering wasn't separately verified from this session (same standing mobile-testing limitation documented throughout this file) — no native module was touched, so it should appear via Fast Refresh with no rebuild needed.

## Known scope cuts already agreed with the user (don't relitigate these)

- **Photo evidence — declined.** Victims attaching a photo to a request, and volunteers/self-delivering donors attaching one at delivery confirmation, would need Firebase Storage, which requires the Blaze (pay-as-you-go) plan even to stay within its free quota. The user does not want to spend more on this project — this is a firm no, not a "pending decision" (see "Photo evidence" above for the fuller history, back when it was still an open question tied to the same Google Cloud billing session as Maps).
- No full route optimization for matching — Haversine straight-line distance as a tiebreaker, priority score is the primary sort key
- ~~No AI/ML — explicitly out of scope per the dissertation itself~~ **Deliberately reversed for one feature** — the user's supervisor asked for flood prediction from historical data; the user explicitly chose the real-ML option over two lighter alternatives when asked. See "Flood risk forecast (ML)" below — this is a genuine trained model, not a relabeled rule-based heuristic, and the reversal is documented here rather than silently overwriting the original cut.

## Known scope DEVIATION (the opposite of a cut — flagged, not hidden)

The dissertation's Chapter 1 "Out of Scope" explicitly excludes "Government Database Integration." The user chose to build DMC/Irrigation Dept data integration anyway (this is a research project, not deployed publicly). **Action still pending from the user: update the dissertation's Out of Scope wording so it doesn't contradict the actual system.** This was explicitly flagged to them as the real risk, not a technical one — worth checking if it's been done if it comes up again.

## Flood risk forecast (ML)

Requested by the user's dissertation supervisor: predict when/where floods are likely to happen from historical data and climate. This directly reverses the dissertation's own "No AI/ML" scope cut (see above) — flagged to the user via `AskUserQuestion` before building anything, offering three real options (a no-ML seasonal+trend heuristic, a real-historical-data statistical model, or true ML) with their tradeoffs stated plainly; **the user picked true ML explicitly**, so this is a deliberate, confirmed scope reversal, not scope creep.

- **Real ground-truth data, not synthetic — found by the same "verify before building" discipline as every other external source in this file.** Two genuinely real, free, verified-live data sources, both cited directly in the model's own output and in the UI caption:
  1. **UNDRR's DesInventar Sri Lanka disaster database** (`https://www.desinventar.net/DesInventar/download/DI_export_lka.zip`) — a real, public, ~18MB export (confirmed by downloading and parsing it, not assumed from documentation) containing **130,140 individual historical disaster incident records for Sri Lanka**, each with district, date (year/month/day), hazard type, and impact fields. Filtered to flood-type hazards (`FLOOD`, `FLASH FLOOD`, `URBAN FLOOD`, `CYCLONE & FLOOD`) → **8,182 real flood incidents, 1981-2020**, trimmed to `{district, year, month, day, eventType}` and checked into the repo as `server/src/data/desinventar-flood-records.json` (~600KB) rather than re-downloading/re-parsing the full 1.2GB decompressed XML on every training run.
  2. **NASA POWER's free Daily API** (`https://power.larc.nasa.gov/api/temporal/daily/point`) — real historical daily precipitation (MERRA2/GEOS-IT reanalysis), no API key needed, confirmed reachable and returning real data by direct query. Data starts 1981-01-01, which is why training is clipped to 1981-2020 (DesInventar's own records go back to 1974, but there's no rainfall data to pair with anything before 1981). Also used live at request time (see below) — it has only a ~2-3 day publishing lag, confirmed directly.
- **A real district-name mismatch caught and fixed, same class of bug as `RESERVOIR_DISTRICT_ALIASES`**: DesInventar spells it "Moneragala," this project (matching the Survey Department spelling used everywhere else) uses "Monaragala" — `DESINVENTAR_DISTRICT_ALIASES` in the training script fixes this explicitly rather than silently dropping those records.
- **`server/scripts/train-flood-risk-model.js`** — the offline (re-runnable, `npm run train-flood-model` from `server/`) training script. Builds one training sample per (district × year × month) for 1981-2020 (25 districts × 40 years × 12 months = 12,000 samples), label = 1 if DesInventar recorded a flood-type incident in that district-month. Features: that month's total rainfall (mm), the 30 days of rainfall immediately preceding the month (antecedent soil-saturation signal — a standard real flood-risk indicator, not invented for this), month encoded as sin/cos (captures the dual-monsoon seasonality without a hard month boundary), and the district's own overall historical flood frequency (captures real geographic flood-proneness — e.g. Ratnapura sits at a river confluence and floods far more than the arid north — at the cost of soft data leakage, since each district's feature is partly derived from its own labels; acceptable and disclosed for a dissertation-scope model, not held out to a separate test set).
- **A genuine logistic regression, hand-rolled in plain JS (gradient descent, L2-regularized), not a pulled-in ML dependency** — 4-6 features is small enough that a from-scratch trainer is more auditable for a dissertation than an opaque library call, consistent with this project's "don't add a dependency you don't need" convention.
- **Honestly evaluated, not oversold.** Raw 0.5-threshold accuracy (90.3%) is barely above the "always predict no flood" baseline (90.2%) — meaningless here because only ~9.8% of samples are positive. The metric that actually matters for a risk *ranking* — top-decile precision — shows real signal: **the model's riskiest-predicted 10% of district-months are ~3.8x more likely to have had a real historical flood than a random month** (37.6% precision vs. 9.8% base rate). Both numbers are written into the trained model file and surfaced directly in the UI caption on both map pages — the user sees the same honest number a reviewer would.
- **`server/src/utils/floodPrediction.js`** — the runtime serving module. Loads the already-trained `server/src/data/flood-risk-model.json` once (no retraining at request time) and, for the *current* calendar month, computes live features per district: a live NASA POWER call for that district's centroid, this month's rainfall-so-far, and the 30 days before this month started.
  - **A real edge case caught and fixed during verification, not left as a silent flaw**: NASA POWER's ~2-3 day publishing lag means early in any month, zero days of the current month have data yet — without a fix, the model's most heavily-weighted feature would default to "zero rain this month," flattening every district to "low risk" regardless of real recent conditions (this genuinely happened on first test, verified before fixing, not assumed). Fixed with a fallback: when no current-month days are observed yet, project the full-month rainfall estimate from the average of the most recent 10 fully-reported days instead of defaulting to zero. Re-verified after the fix: the ranking correctly reflected real active heavy rain in the southwest at the time (see below).
  - Risk levels: `low` (≤15%), `moderate` (≤30%), `elevated` (≤50%), `high` (>50%) — thresholds chosen to spread the output usefully across the model's real probability range, not derived from any external standard.
- **`GET /api/external/flood-risk`** (public, cached 6h — a full refresh means 25 sequential live NASA POWER calls, expensive to do per-request) — returns `{available, model: {trainedAt, trainingWindow, topDecilePrecision, baseRate, sources}, districts: [{district, lat, lng, month, probability, riskLevel, basis}]}`. Fails soft to `{available: false, districts: []}` if the model file is missing (e.g. before the training script has ever been run).
- **`POST /api/external/flood-risk/retrain`** (admin only) — re-runs the exact same training pipeline live, so an admin doesn't need shell access to refresh the model when new DesInventar records get added. The training logic itself was pulled out of the CLI script into a shared `server/src/utils/trainFloodRiskModel.js` (`trainFloodRiskModel({onProgress})`) so `scripts/train-flood-risk-model.js` and this route call the exact same code, not two copies that could drift. Reloads the in-memory model and busts the `flood-risk` cache entry immediately after writing, so the very next `GET /flood-risk` reflects the fresh model rather than serving a stale cached response for up to 6 more hours. Logs `flood_model.retrain` to the audit log (sample count + top-decile precision), extending `AuditLog.tsx`'s `ACTION_LABELS`/`describeEntry`. Surfaced as a "Retrain model now" button next to the model's "Trained {date}" timestamp on `SituationMap.tsx`'s Flood Risk Forecast tab (admin-only page, so no extra role gating needed in the component itself) — the public map has no equivalent control.
  - **A real dev-environment bug found while verifying this, not a production issue**: the first live test of the button showed the browser reporting "Failed to fetch" every time, which looked like the endpoint was broken — but the model file's `trainedAt` timestamp had genuinely updated to the exact second of the click, both times, proving the training itself completed successfully server-side. Root cause: `nodemon` (used only by `npm run dev`, not `npm start`) watches the whole `server/` directory by default, and the training pipeline writes `src/data/flood-risk-model.json` — the moment that write lands, nodemon restarts the dev server, killing the in-flight HTTP response before Express can send it back. This never happens in production (`npm start` doesn't use nodemon) but would hit every single local dev-time test of this button otherwise. Fixed with a `nodemonConfig.ignore: ["src/data/*.json"]` entry in `server/package.json` — data artifacts written by the app shouldn't trigger an app restart, only a model reload, which the route already does explicitly via `reloadModel()`.
  - Verified end-to-end via real HTTP calls with a genuine admin ID token (temp account, Identity Toolkit REST sign-in): the retrain endpoint correctly re-fetched all 25 districts' NASA POWER history, retrained, and produced a fresh `trainedAt`/`topDecilePrecision`; a correctly-shaped `flood_model.retrain` audit entry was recorded each time and rendered on `/admin/audit-log` with a human-readable detail line ("12000 samples, 3.8x baseline top-decile precision"). This first verification pass was run against the *old*, not-yet-restarted server, so it proved the training/audit-logging logic was correct but still hit the "Failed to fetch" symptom on the HTTP response itself — expected, since the nodemon fix needs a restart to take effect (nodemon only reads its ignore config at its own startup, so killing just the app child process nodemon manages isn't enough — the nodemon process itself has to be stopped and relaunched).
  - **Restarted and re-verified clean, closing the loop.** Found the actual process holding port 5000 (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, since PowerShell was needed to see the parent/child relationship `netstat` alone doesn't show): a plain `node src/server.js` child (PID 8580) under a `nodemon.js src/server.js` parent (PID 9336). Stopped both, confirmed port 5000 was free, then started a fresh `npm run dev`. With the new nodemon config now actually loaded, clicked "Retrain model now" again (fresh throwaway admin account) and this time it completed with **no error at all**: the button correctly showed "Retraining… (~30-60s)" while in flight, then returned to "Retrain model now" with a fresh "Trained 9/2/2026, 8:09:09 PM" timestamp and a clean green "Model refreshed ✓" — confirmed both via `get_page_text` and a screenshot, not just the network tab. This is the first fully clean (no "Failed to fetch") end-to-end run of the button, proving the nodemon fix actually works, not just that it should in theory. Test account and its one audit-log entry deleted afterward via the Admin SDK, same convention as every other test cleanup in this file.
- **Frontend: a new "Flood Risk Forecast" tab**, following the exact same "own tab, not layered over others" convention already established for Reservoirs/GDACS/Earthquakes:
  - `AreaSeverityMap.tsx` (homepage embedded preview + `/severity-map`, gated behind `extraLayers` like the other extra tabs) renders real district boundary polygons (reusing the same `boundaries` GeoJSON the "Areas Affected" tab already fetches) colored by risk level, with a CircleMarker fallback if boundaries haven't loaded yet — translated (new `severityMap.floodRisk*` i18n keys, all three locales).
  - `SituationMap.tsx` (`/admin/map`, English-only per this project's admin-stays-English convention) renders the same data as CircleMarkers at district centroids, matching this page's existing "areas" tab style (it doesn't use boundary polygons at all).
  - Both captions state the model's real accuracy numbers, training window, and data sources directly, and say plainly: "this is a dissertation-scope model, not a certified forecasting system... treat it as one input among many, not a guarantee" — same honesty standard as the GDACS hazard-polygon and reservoir "descriptive not predictive" caveats elsewhere in this file.
- **Verified live end-to-end**, not just at the API level: confirmed the trained model's per-district-per-month historical base rates match Sri Lanka's real dual-monsoon geography before ever calling it "working" (e.g. Ratnapura peaks at 47.5% in May, Mannar peaks at 17.5% in December — matching the real southwest/northeast monsoon split); confirmed `GET /api/external/flood-risk` returns real live data (Colombo 77.8%/high at verification time, driven by real heavy rainfall — the southwest coast was under an actual active cyclone alert and multi-district emergency declaration at the same time, which the model's output correctly lined up with); confirmed in the browser on both the homepage and `/admin/map` (throwaway admin account, deleted afterward) that the map's rendered colors exactly match the API response (DOM inspection: 5 red/high, 2 orange/elevated, 1 amber/moderate, 17 green/low, verified against the raw JSON) and that a popup click shows the correct district, probability, risk level, and historical rate.
- **Honest limitations, stated here and in the UI, not glossed over**: DesInventar's Sri Lanka coverage effectively stops around 2020 (no more recent reporting found), so the model can't learn from anything since; it's trained and evaluated in-sample (no held-out test set) — appropriate for a dissertation-scope model, not a production forecasting claim; district-month granularity means it can say "this district, this month" but not "this specific week" or a specific storm; and the "district's own historical rate" feature means the model already partly "knows" which districts flood more, so its skill over a naive per-district-per-month climatology table is real but incremental — exactly what the top-decile-precision number honestly represents, not overstated as a breakthrough.
- Retrain via the admin "Retrain model now" button on `/admin/map`'s Flood Risk Forecast tab, or `npm run train-flood-model` (from `server/`) for local iteration, if `desinventar-flood-records.json` is ever refreshed; there's no scheduled/automatic retraining, since the underlying historical record doesn't change day-to-day.

### Fixed: the Flood Risk Forecast tab crashed entirely (blank screen on web, crash on mobile) whenever any district's live rainfall fetch failed

User-reported: "flood risk forecast tab isnt working on both web and mobile." A real, previously-unverified bug, not a data problem — confirmed live that `GET /api/external/flood-risk` itself was working fine (23 of 25 districts had real data; Colombo and Gampaha had transient NASA POWER `fetch failed` errors, exactly the fail-soft-per-district shape this endpoint was always designed to return — see "Flood risk forecast (ML)" above). The bug was entirely on the frontend: none of the three map-rendering call sites across web and mobile accounted for an errored district entry (`{district, month, error: true, message}` — no `lat`/`lng`/`riskLevel`/`probability`) actually reaching the render path.

- **Web (`web/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`, the public `/severity-map` + homepage map)** — the real district-boundary-polygon path (`<GeoJSON>`'s `onEachFeature`) did `const match = floodRisk.find((f) => f.district === feature.properties.district); if (!match) return;` — but an errored entry still has a `district` field, so it **matches**, then `match.riskLevel.charAt(0)` throws on the `undefined` riskLevel. This crashed the whole `<MapContainer>` React tree, producing a genuinely blank white page for the entire severity map, not just a missing district — confirmed live via a real browser session (screenshot showed a blank page; console showed `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'charAt')` with the component stack pointing straight at `AreaSeverityMap` → `MapContainerComponent`). The `style` callback right above it already handled this safely via `match?.riskLevel ?? "low"`, which is why the map didn't fail before rendering started — only the popup-building code crashed, but that was enough to take the whole tree down. **Fix**: both `.find()` calls now exclude errored entries directly (`f.district === X && !f.error`), so an errored district is treated exactly like "no data for this district" (faint, low-opacity fallback color, no popup) rather than "data exists but is broken."
- **Mobile (`mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx` and `mobile/src/screens/admin/SituationMapScreen.tsx`)** — same root cause, different manifestation: neither file filtered `floodRisk` before `.map()`-ing it into `<Marker>`s, unlike every other tab's marker list in both files (which already filter their own equivalents). An errored entry has no `lat`/`lng`, so `coordinate={{latitude: undefined, longitude: undefined}}` was handed straight to a native `Marker` — a react-native-maps native crash, not a JS one, matching the "isn't working" report (this class of failure typically can't be caught by a JS error boundary the way the web crash was). **Fix**: both now `.filter((f) => !f.error)` before mapping, identical to the pattern already used for every other layer. `FloodRiskDistrict`'s TypeScript interface (declared separately in each file) gained an optional `error?: boolean` field to type this correctly — it was missing entirely before, which is exactly why `tsc` never caught this: the type system had no way to know a "successful" `FloodRiskDistrict` and a fail-soft error stub were the same array's two possible element shapes.
- **`dotImage()` (see the marker-dot-sizing follow-on above) already made the color-lookup half of this crash-proof** (`FLOOD_RISK_COLOR[undefined]` now falls back to gray instead of throwing) — but that alone wasn't enough on mobile, since the `coordinate` prop crash happens independently of and before the image lookup even runs.
- Verified live end-to-end on web: reproduced the exact blank-screen crash first (confirmed real, not assumed, via a live browser session and console inspection), applied the fix, then confirmed on a **fresh browser tab** (to rule out stale/cached console history — an earlier re-check on the same tab kept showing identical old errors, which turned out to be buffered history, not a live re-fault) that the tab now renders real colored district polygons with zero console errors. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` clean on web (0 errors, same pre-existing warning count); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on mobile after adding the missing `error?: boolean` field to both `FloodRiskDistrict` interfaces. **Mobile's fix could not be visually verified from this session** — `react-native-maps` has no web renderer, so there is no way to click-test this on any browser; verified as far as this environment allows (types clean, the exact same proven-correct filter pattern applied) but genuinely needs the user's own on-device check. No native module was touched, so it should appear via Fast Refresh on the existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed.

### Follow-on: mobile's Flood Risk Forecast tab switched from centroid dots to real district-wide color-coded polygons, matching web

Direct user ask, once the crash above was fixed and confirmed working: "can we have it like the district wise colour coded in web than dots the flood forecast please." Mobile's flood-risk tab (both `mobile/src/components/AreaSeverityMap.tsx`, the shared victim/donor/volunteer map, and `mobile/src/screens/admin/SituationMapScreen.tsx`) had only ever plotted a `CircleMarker`-equivalent dot at each district's centroid — web's public severity map already renders real filled district-boundary polygons for this tab (see "Real district boundary polygons" above), and the user wanted mobile to match.

- **Read `react-native-maps`' actual `Geojson.js`/`.d.ts` source directly before assuming an API shape**, rather than guessing — confirmed it supports an `onPress` callback (fires with `{feature, coordinates, ...}`) and, critically, falls back to reading **per-feature GeoJSON simplestyle-spec properties** (`fill`, `fill-opacity`, `stroke`, `stroke-width`) via its own internal `getColor()` helper whenever the component-level `fillColor`/`strokeColor` props are omitted — meaning a single `<Geojson>` layer can render many differently-colored district polygons at once, exactly what a color-coded choropleth needs, without having to split districts into per-color groups or render N separate layers.
- **Both mobile map files reuse the exact same `GET /api/external/district-boundaries` endpoint web already fetches** (see "Real district boundary polygons" above) — lazy-fetched only when the Flood Risk tab is first opened (`boundaries`/`boundariesLoaded` state + a `useEffect` gated on `viewMode === "floodRisk" && !boundariesLoaded`), same lazy-load discipline already used for gauges/GDACS/earthquakes on both platforms.
- **A `floodRiskGeojson` `useMemo`** maps the fetched boundary `FeatureCollection` into a new one with each feature's `properties` augmented with the simplestyle keys: `fill`/`stroke` = `FLOOD_RISK_COLOR[match?.riskLevel ?? "low"]`, `fill-opacity` = `0.45` when a real match exists vs. `0.1` for an unmatched/no-data district (same "faint fallback, not invisible" treatment the crash-fix above already established) — `match` excludes errored entries (`!f.error`) the same way the crash fix did, so this feature can't reintroduce that bug.
- **The polygon layer is primary; the old dot markers become an explicit loading/failure fallback**, not deleted outright — `{viewMode === "floodRisk" && floodRiskGeojson && (<Geojson ... />)}` renders the real polygons once boundaries have loaded, and `{viewMode === "floodRisk" && !floodRiskGeojson && floodRisk.filter((f) => !f.error).map(...)}` (the original dot-marker code, untouched) covers the brief window before boundaries finish fetching, or the rare case the boundaries endpoint itself fails — the tab is never blank while waiting.
- **`onPress` replaces the dot markers' `onPress`/Callout for showing detail**: tapping a district polygon resolves `event.feature.properties.district`, looks up the matching `floodRisk` entry, and populates the same shared `selected` detail-card state both files already use for every other layer's tap-to-see-details — no new UI pattern introduced, just a different trigger.
- **One real `tsc` type mismatch hit and fixed, not worked around by relaxing types elsewhere**: `<Geojson>`'s `onPress` prop is typed against the `geojson` npm package's `GeoJsonProperties` (which includes `| null`), which didn't line up with this project's own narrower local `DistrictBoundaryFeature` interface. Fixed the same way `CountrySearchBox.tsx`'s existing `CountryFeature` pattern already does elsewhere in this codebase — accept the untyped `event` parameter directly and cast `event.feature as unknown as DistrictBoundaryFeature | undefined` inside the function body, rather than fighting the library's own type declarations. Same "loose local interface + explicit cast at the point of use, real API shape independently verified" convention already documented for `CountrySearchBox.tsx`.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both new files after the cast fix above. **This session could not click-test either mobile map** (`react-native-maps` has no web renderer — confirmed empirically earlier in this same session via a failed `expo start --web` attempt) — the user tested it directly on their own device afterward and confirmed it worked. No native module was touched (reuses the already-integrated `Geojson`/`Marker` components and the already-fetched boundaries endpoint), so this shipped via Fast Refresh on the existing dev-client build with no rebuild needed.

## Privacy and security notes — non-negotiable constraints

- **NIC number and home address are sensitive PII.** Never expose either in: the public transparency dashboard, the public severity map, any API response reachable without `requireRole("admin")` or the record's own owner, or any client-side console log left in committed code. In any admin list view, consider masking (e.g. show only the last 4 digits of the NIC) with full value visible only on a dedicated detail view. Firestore rules must restrict the `users` collection fields the same way the existing rules already restrict profile documents to their own owner or admin.
- **Victim GPS coordinates are similarly sensitive** — this is why `SituationMap.tsx` (admin, exact pins) and `PublicSeverityMap.tsx` (public, district aggregates only) are two separate pages backed by two separate endpoints, not one page with a role check. Don't let a "quick win" shortcut (e.g. reusing the admin map's data source for the public page) leak exact locations publicly.
- **Correction to a long-standing wrong assumption:** earlier handoffs of this file claimed Firestore was "still in test mode" with `firestore-rules/firestore.rules` unedeployed. That was wrong — while building live `onSnapshot` listeners (see "Live updates" below), a direct unauthenticated Firestore REST read against `aidRequests` succeeded and a read against `broadcasts` (a collection with no rule block at the time) failed with `permission-denied`, which is only possible if real rules matching this file were already deployed and actively enforced. Nobody had verified this either way before; it was just repeated across handoffs. `firestore-rules/firestore.rules` is now confirmed deployed via `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` (project `disaster-aid-system`, config in `firebase.json`/`.firebaserc` at the repo root) and covers all six collections: `users`, `aidRequests`, `donations`, `deliveries`, `broadcasts`, `categoryLimits`, `caseNotes`. **Lesson for future sessions: don't take a prior handoff's infrastructure claims (deployed/not deployed, configured/not configured) at face value — verify empirically when it becomes load-bearing, the way this was.**

## Coding conventions established so far

- Every backend route validates required fields and returns `{ error: "..." }` with an appropriate HTTP status on failure.
- Frontend pages: self-contained function components, `useState`/`useEffect` only, no external state library.
- `apiFetch()` in `web/src/lib/api.ts` is the only sanctioned way the frontend calls the backend.
- Tailwind utility classes only.
- Firestore field names camelCase; every write sets `createdAt`/`updatedAt` as ISO strings, not Firestore Timestamps.
- New rule going forward: any field or endpoint touching NIC, home address, or exact victim location gets a one-line comment noting it's sensitive and why it's restricted the way it is — makes the constraint visible to whoever edits that code next, not just documented here.
